STRONGEST EXACT CLAIM
Let F be an explicit n by n rational-complex matrix and lambda_i>0 rational.
Suppose all but t rows are monomial relative to one permutation p: for each
regular i, F[i,j]=0 unless j=p(i). The t exceptional rows are arbitrary, dense,
and have arbitrary phases. Define
 c_k(F,lambda)=sum_{I,J disjoint, |I|=|J|=k}
                 (product_{i in I}lambda_i)|det F[I,J]|^2.
All c_k, all row/column occupation-prefix partitions, exact zero tests, and
conditional samples can be ACQUIRED in
 O(n^2 (t+1)^2 5^t) exact arithmetic operations per partition computation,
with polynomial bit lengths. Hence t<=C log n for fixed public C admits a
polynomial-bit exact counting algorithm and expected-polynomial-time exact
sampler, or a fixed-time epsilon-TV random-bit sampler at charged precision.
This strictly admits dense arbitrary-phase rows beyond the monomial subclass.
No coefficient/partition oracle or imported FPRAS is used.

For the corresponding fixed-k full-basis homogenization with d=n-2k unit
old-to-dummy coordinate lines, an acquired two-replica kernel redraws the
FIRST full-basis configuration independently from this law. It is reversible
for pi tensor pi, and for EVERY real f1,f2,
 E_P[f1(A)+f2(B)] = Var_pi f1.
Thus the requested additive energy bound holds with L=1 in this admitted
subclass. This is not a construction for arbitrary F or arbitrary calibrated
coordinate auxiliaries. The unrestricted OA113 transfer remains unresolved.

FULL DERIVATION
1. Expand the determinant on regular rows.
Fix selected exceptional rows A=I intersect H. Let R=I minus H. A nonzero
minor requires p(R) subset J. Since p is injective, expansion along every
regular row gives
 det F[I,J] = sign(I,J) (product_{i in R}F[i,p(i)]) det F[A,B],
 B=J minus p(R), |B|=|A|.
The sign disappears on taking the squared modulus. Conversely every choice
of R and B with |B|=|A| and disjoint I,J yields precisely this term; there is
no expansion multiplicity. Therefore the desired partition sums over
regular row choices and exceptional free-column choices, weighted by
 product_{i in R}lambda_i |F[i,p(i)]|^2,
 product_{a in A}lambda_a,
 |det F[A,B]|^2.

2. Encode the exceptional squared determinant by a double exterior algebra.
For fixed A in H, let E_A have ordered basis e_a, a in A. For each column j
put h_j=sum_{a in A}F[a,j] e_a. If B is processed in ANY fixed order,
 h_{j1} wedge ... wedge h_{j|A|}
   =det F[A,B] e_A,
with the column-order sign understood. In a second copy use conjugate h_j.
The top coefficient of the product of both wedge strings is exactly
|det F[A,B]|^2. Process a free-column choice b_j=1 by applying
 C_j: u tensor v -> (u wedge h_j) tensor (v wedge conjugate(h_j)).
Process b_j=0 by the identity. This keeps the full mismatch registers P,Q;
it never projects ket/bra, replicas, or common occupied cores onto one cut.
Intermediate coefficients can have either sign and can be complex. This
is an EXACT arithmetic DP, not a positive sampling rule for those registers.
Only completed nonnegative prefix partitions are used as probabilities.

Explicit matrix element: for a notin P and b notin Q,
 (P,Q) -> (P union {a}, Q union {b})
 has multiplier F[a,j] conjugate(F[b,j]) times
 (-1)^(|{x in P:x>a}|+|{x in Q:x>b}|).
The two exterior degrees always agree. There are
 sum_{q=0}^{|A|}binom(|A|,q)^2=binom(2|A|,|A|)<=4^|A|
possible (P,Q) registers.

3. Process the monomial permutation cycles.
At site i let r_i indicate that row i is selected, let m_i indicate incoming
monomial column occupancy, and let b_i indicate selection of this column by
the exceptional determinant. The hard disjoint condition is exactly
 r_i+m_i+b_i<=1.
The actual column occupation is c_i=m_i+b_i. If i is regular, outgoing
occupancy on p(i) is r_i, and choosing r_i=1 contributes
lambda_i |F[i,p(i)]|^2 and one row-count degree. If i is exceptional,
r_i=1 iff i in A, choosing it contributes lambda_i and one row degree, and
its outgoing monomial occupancy is zero. A free-column choice b_i=1 applies
C_i. Prefix constraints simply prohibit the incompatible r_i or c_i.

Write each cycle in p order. Enumerate its incoming boundary bit 0 or1
(only0 if the last row is exceptional), process all sites, and retain terms
whose last outgoing bit equals the initial bit. This traces precisely the
circular occupancy condition, with each valid configuration counted once.
Carry the exterior registers across cycles, initially empty. At the end
retain only P=Q=A; the row-degree-k coefficient is the desired contribution.
Summing over all A subset H gives c_k and the same formula for each prefix.

4. Costs and numerical representation.
For fixed A, carry at most O(n 4^|A|) register/count/boundary states. Each
site update has O((|A|+1)^2) branches. There are n sites and at most two
boundary choices per cycle; their contributions are summed, not multiplied
into a separate exponential. Summing over A costs
 sum_A (|A|+1)^2 4^|A| <=(t+1)^2 5^t.
This proves the displayed O(n^2(t+1)^2 5^t) arithmetic bound.

Rational-complex entries can be converted to Gaussian integers with one
common denominator D of polynomial bit length. Each c_k then has the factor
D^(-2k). For lambda_i=a_i/b_i, multiply every partition by Q=product b_i;
at each site use factor b_i when r_i=0 and a_i when r_i=1, in place of1 and
lambda_i. The common denominator QD^(2k) is independent of the occupied
sets, including under prefix constraints. All DP operations are now exact
Gaussian-integer operations. The log of the sum of absolute intermediate
path contributions is polynomial in n and input length: there are at most
3^n occupation choices, at most (t!)^2 exterior expansion terms, and at most
2n matrix/activity factors in each contribution. Thus cancellation does not
require exponentially many precision bits; exact integer bit lengths are
polynomial. The output can be exponentially small as a real number without
causing inverse-probability runtime. When t grows linearly, 5^t is explicitly
exponential and is not hidden as an oracle.

5. Acquire prefix samples.
At site i compute the three completed partitions for (r_i,c_i) equal to
(0,0),(1,0),(0,1), conditioned on all earlier outcomes. Choose a branch in
proportion to those nonnegative exact weights. The chain rule gives the
specified determinant-squared law. Zero branches are recognized exactly.
At most3n partition calls are made, for
 O(n^3(t+1)^2 5^t) arithmetic operations per draw, plus bit arithmetic.
An exact integer categorical draw by rejection uses expected at most two
attempts. For a fixed-time epsilon-TV version, approximate each 3-category
choice by a dyadic grid with denominator2^b, b=ceil(log2(3n/epsilon))+1;
per-call TV<=3/2^b and sequential coupling charges the sum over n choices.
Random bits, exact rational threshold comparisons, and the charged b are
part of the algorithm. This is not a fixed bounded-time exact non-dyadic
random-bit claim.

6. Actual full-basis point law, rather than an aggregate-hole oracle.
Let d=n-2k. Add d private dummy coordinates and all coordinate pairs
(e_j,e_dummy), activity1, to the original pairs (e_i,f_i), activity lambda_i.
A full basis must choose exactly d coordinate pairs, covering every dummy
and distinct old coordinates. For every I,J in c_k, the d remaining old
coordinates can be bijected to the d dummies in d! ways. Private-row
expansion gives determinant modulus |det F[I,J]| in each case. Thus the
full-paired partition is d! c_k. Sample I,J by the DP and an independent
uniform bijection; this samples the exact microscopic full-basis line law.
All point weights are direct rational determinant values, and all required
partitions are computed by the DP above. No f(U) hole coefficient is supplied.

7. Explicit two-replica kernel and additive energy.
On (A,B), draw A' independently from the acquired full-basis law pi and keep
B. For positive-law states,
 pi(A)pi(B) P((A,B),(A',B))=pi(A)pi(B)pi(A')
 is symmetric in A,A', proving reversibility. For Fpair=f1(A)+f2(B),
 E_P(Fpair)=1/2 E_(A,A' iid pi)[(f1(A)-f1(A'))^2]=Var_pi f1.
It holds for arbitrary f2 without a full pair gap, exactly the OA113/L07
additive-function interface. If a symmetric choice of refreshed replica is
preferred, its energy is (Var f1+Var f2)/2 and L=2. Arbitrary positive rational
row activities lambda along annealing preserve the DP and the same energy
constant. A certified finite-time approximate draw is charged separately;
it does not turn an approximate transition into an exactly stationary kernel.

8. Acquire the structural certificate rather than supplying it silently.
Inspect each row by exact zero tests. A row with more than one nonzero must
be exceptional. Among singleton rows sharing a nonzero column, retain one
regular row per column and mark all others exceptional. Zero rows are
regular and can be assigned distinct unused permutation images. Extend the
resulting partial injection to a permutation by assigning the remaining
unused columns to exceptional rows. This finds a valid certificate with the
minimum number of exceptional rows: every multi-entry row is forced out,
and at most one singleton row in each column group can remain regular.
All operations are polynomial-bit. A supplied permutation is unnecessary.
The current diagnostic implementation accepts a certificate and verifies it;
this straightforward certificate-acquisition routine is not yet coded.

ACTUAL OA113 GATE AND UNRESTRICTED ATTACK LIMIT
Read the actual copied OA113 pair-kernel/energy proof, source lines1573-2137,
and the L07 full replication proof, before proposing this kernel. The
positive discrepancy-orbit, common-core-cut projection and tiny determinant
reassignment problems recorded in cycle5 are not resolved by ordinary DPP
row/column Gibbs updates. In particular a permutation directed even cycle
has two alternating full occupancies, each fixed by both conditional row
and conditional column updates. Adding a whole-replica swap does not give
an all-additive bound: choose f1=f2 equal to the alternating-label indicator;
its sum is conserved under that swap. Coordinate auxiliaries or a genuinely
richer microscopic step are required in the unrestricted class.

The constructed refresh bypasses those obstructions ONLY by acquiring the
entire target law with a bounded exceptional-row DP. It does not imply that
an unrestricted volume-crossover refresh is cheap. In the general case the
same exterior state grows as5^n; trying to call it a polynomial microscopic
kernel would supply the unresolved counting work for free. Arbitrary
calibrated coordinate-edge activities are also outside this exact DP.

INDEPENDENT INITIAL — c01_s02 — 7 October 2026
Origin: requested gpt-6.1-sol / max; observed model UNKNOWN.
No other cycle6 worker artifact was read and no peer scientific result was received.

STATUS

Two complete algebraic mechanism obstructions are proved below. The first
strengthens the baseline microscopic delta^4 reassignment counterexample:
the bad ratio occurs for an already balanced, dense off-diagonal positive,
uniformly well-conditioned rational F, and survives every common ambient
preconditioner, independent pair field, and invertible within-pair gauge.
The second is a general additive-energy escape-capacity lemma, applied to
coordinate-augmented directed cycles. It rules out all bounded line-edit
plus arbitrary union-preserving replicated kernels when their target
coordinate defect mass is exponentially small.

Neither result refutes every microscopic energy compiler. In particular,
the cycle obstruction does not cover the polynomial calibrated coordinate
probe activities supplied by the intended OA113 transfer. The dense four-
site ratio is not a mixing lower bound: alternative moves can be good.
The main general determinant-parity counting/sampling gate remains OPEN.
Novelty is unverified. These are internally proved candidate lemmas, not
external validation or a claimed field-breaking advance.

EXACT INTERFACE

Let the ambient dimension be even r. A line l has two explicit rational
vectors v_l,w_l in Q(i)^r and positive rational activity a_l. A microscopic
full-basis state A is a set of r/2 lines. Set

  W(A)=(product_{l in A} a_l) |det V_A|^2,

where V_A contains both columns of every selected line in a fixed order.
The law pi(A)=W(A)/Z is restricted to states of positive weight. Point
weights, determinant ratios and zero tests are polynomial-bit computable;
the sum Z is not supplied. For the canonical hard-BCS class v_i=e_i and
w_i=f_i, the transpose of row i of F, one has

  |det V_A|^2=|det F[A,A^c]|^2

when r=n and |A|=n/2. Coordinate lines (e_i,e_j) may also be added.
For a reversible kernel P on pi tensor pi, write

  E_P(H)=1/2 sum_{x,y} (pi tensor pi)(x) P(x,y)(H(x)-H(y))^2.

The intended comparison quantifies over ALL real f1,f2:

  Var_pi f1 <= C E_P((A,B) -> f1(A)+f2(B)).                 (E)

The equal-functions direction f1=f2 is included. Controlling only
f2=0, or only antisymmetric functions, is insufficient.

1. A BALANCED POSITIVE WELL-CONDITIONED REASSIGNMENT COUNTEREXAMPLE

For any integer L>=2 put d=2^-L and use one-based labels with

             [0  1+d  1  1]
  F_d =      [1+d  0  1  2].
             [1   1   0  1]
             [1   2   1  0]

Every off-diagonal entry is in [1,2], every diagonal is zero, and F is real
symmetric. All line activities can be one. Take the full-basis line sets

  A={1,2}, B={3,4}, A'={1,3}, B'={2,4}.

Each pair tuple uses every original line exactly once, so the multiset
union is preserved. The four complementary minors are exactly

  det F[A,A^c]=det F[B,B^c]=1,
  det F[A',A'^c]=det F[B',B'^c]=d.

Consequently

                 W(A')W(B')/[W(A)W(B)]=d^4=2^-4L.          (1)

This is a four-line reassignment with perfectly comparable activities.
It exhibits exponential Metropolis rejection in the binary input length,
although no matrix entry is small and no complex phase is present.
Cancellation of two positive products in a selected minor is sufficient.

Full-matrix conditioning is uniformly bounded. With F_0 obtained by d=0,

 F_0^-1=[-1  1/2   0  1/2;
          1/2 -1/2 1/2 0;
          0  1/2  -1  1/2;
          1/2 0   1/2 -1/2].

Its maximum absolute row sum is 2. It is real symmetric, hence its
spectral norm is at most 2. The perturbation F_d-F_0 has spectral norm d.
The Neumann inverse bound therefore gives

  ||F_d^-1||_2 <= 2/(1-2d) <=4,
  ||F_d||_2 <= ||F_d||_infinity <=4+d <=17/4,
  kappa_2(F_d)<=17.                                       (2)

No asserted lower bound on every selected minor follows from (2).

The ambient-hole law is already within the exact baseline balance window.
For even holes U write f(U)=Z_{[4]\U} and g(U)=f(U)/f(empty).
The all-occupied partition is the sum over the six two/two cuts:

                 f(empty)=4(1-d+d^2).                     (3)

For two holes {i,j}, let {a,b} be their complement. Since F is symmetric,
f({i,j})=2 F_ab^2. For 0<d<=1/4 this gives

  13/4<=f(empty)<=4,
  1/2<=g({i,j})<=32/13<4.

Thus EVERY two-hole coefficient is bounded between two positive constants,
and each row maximum r_i=max_{j!=i} g({i,j}) satisfies the required
1/8<=r_i<=4 without any balancing oracle. Aggregate stability and exchange
are fully compatible with the exponentially bad microscopic ratio (1).

The ratio cannot be repaired by gauge choice. More generally, let
G in GL_r(Q(i)) be a common ambient preconditioner and let each line's
two columns be multiplied by U_l in GL_2(Q(i)). Let its activity change
arbitrarily to a'_l>0. Then

 W'(A)=|det G|^2 product_{l in A}[a'_l |det U_l|^2] |det V_A|^2.

For any A,B,A',B' having the same multiset union, each line has the same
total multiplicity in numerator and denominator. All G, U_l and activity
factors cancel. The reassignment product ratio is EXACTLY unchanged.
Adding coordinate auxiliary states changes Z but cannot change this ratio
between the four pre-existing full-basis states. Normalization cancels too.

Therefore an analogue of the graph proof's acceptance-one union swap or
bounded comparable-activity weight comparison cannot hold for every such
determinant reassignment, even after balancing or general linear
preconditioning. A legal energy proof must sum over better moves, retain
extra information, or use an integrated bound that tolerates individual
exponentially small capacities. This statement does not prove that any
particular complete proposal has small energy.

2. AN ADDITIVE ESCAPE-CAPACITY OBSTRUCTION

Lemma. Let nu be a stationary law for a finite Markov kernel P. Let H be
a subset with nu(H^c)=eta. Suppose a function T takes values in [0,2] and
is constant on every legal transition with both endpoints in H. Then

                        E_P(T)<=4 eta.                    (4)

Reversibility is not needed for this bound; stationarity suffices.
Indeed only directed edges incident to H^c can contribute. Their total
stationary flow is at most

  sum_{x in H^c,y}nu(x)P(x,y)+sum_{x in H,y in H^c}nu(x)P(x,y)
  <=eta+eta=2eta,

where stationarity bounds the second term by all inflow to H^c, namely eta.
The squared difference is at most 4; multiplication by 1/2 proves (4).

For a product law nu=pi tensor pi, let an ordinary subset O have pi(O)=1-b.
Take H=O x O. Then eta=2b-b^2. If a one-layer h in [0,1] has
h(A)+h(B) invariant on every H-to-H transition, comparison (E) with
f1=f2=h requires

               C >= Var_pi h/[4(2b-b^2)].                 (5)

Arbitrary rapid motion inside H, including global layer swaps, does not
remove this additive variance direction. A stationary refresh proposal
whose useful target entrances are rare is subject to the same bound.

3. EXACT COORDINATE-AUGMENTED CYCLE APPLICATION

Take n=2q>=4, original canonical lines (e_i,e_{i+1}) on the cyclic
coordinates i modulo n, with unit activity. F is the cyclic permutation
matrix; its condition number is one. Add one coordinate line (e_i,e_j)
of activity epsilon for EVERY unordered pair i<j. The original lines and
coordinate lines retain distinct colors when their endpoints agree.
Every nonzero full determinant has absolute value one. Thus microscopic
states are colored perfect matchings of the complete coordinate graph.

Exactly two states contain no coordinate lines: the two alternating
matchings A_O and A_E on the cycle, each of weight one. Let O={A_O,A_E}
and let b be the target mass outside O. With M=n+binom(n,2) total line
labels and 0<epsilon<=1, at most 2^M line subsets exist. Each rare state
uses a coordinate line and has weight at most epsilon. Therefore

                  b<=(epsilon 2^M)/2.                    (6)

Consider ANY stationary two-replica kernel whose permitted transitions
are each of either type:

 (i) replace at most b0<q original line labels IN EACH layer; coordinate
     line edits and proposal probabilities may otherwise be arbitrary;
 (ii) preserve the multiset union of all line labels across both layers,
      allowing unbounded global interchanges and arbitrary decomposition.

No oracle restrictions are needed for this lower bound. It holds even
for a proposal designed with perfect knowledge of the target law.

For (i), an H-to-H transition cannot change either layer, because A_O and
A_E are disjoint q-line sets and switching them replaces q original
labels. For (ii), the number of ordinary layers equal to A_O is determined
by original-line multiplicities in the union, hence cannot change.
Set h(A)=1_{A=A_O}. Then h(A)+h(B) is invariant on H-to-H transitions.
Since pi(A_O)=(1-b)/2,

                 Var_pi h=(1-b^2)/4.

Combining this with (5) proves the exact necessary bound

             C >= (1-b^2)/[16(2b-b^2)].                    (7)

Choose epsilon=2^-(M+L) with L>=1. Then b<=2^-(L+1), so (7) implies,
for example, C>=3*2^L/64. The bit length of epsilon is M+L+1. For fixed
q>b0 this is an exponential lower bound in a line-activity encoding
length, despite perfectly conditioned F, computable point weights,
strictly positive coordinate auxiliaries, and unlimited union swaps.
An explicit coordinate-dominated initial law does not imply a polynomial
energy bound at this endpoint. At epsilon=0 the energy is exactly zero
for h+h, so no finite additive comparison exists in this move class.

This example is a graph-matching subclass, already easy to count and
sample by other algorithms. It is NOT evidence of computational hardness.
It does NOT contradict OA113: its endpoint lacks the source's calibrated
reference network guaranteeing adequate auxiliary capacities. With
inverse-polynomial defect activities the estimate above is only a
polynomial necessary loss and may be harmless. The useful implication
is that a proposed compiler must supply quantitative target escape
capacity, not only positive auxiliaries, a base refresh and pair swaps.

STRONGEST CLAIM

For rational-complex F of size n and nonnegative rational row activities
lambda_i, acquire an exact determinant-parity norm and all orientation prefix
masses, then an exact classical microstate sampler, in
  O(n(k+1)(d+1)^2 7^w) Gaussian-integer ring operations per contraction,
where an explicitly supplied site ordering has right frontier width w and
maximum future support degree d. The denominator clearing and every ring
operand have polynomial bit length in n and binary input length L.
Recomputing at most 3n contractions gives a complete implemented sampler
with O(n^2(k+1)(d+1)^2 7^w) ring operations per sample. No unknown coefficient,
balancing, conditional-orientation, or success-probability oracle is supplied.
This is polynomial bit time for w=O(log n), including arbitrary phases and
nonbipartite support. It is exponential for unrestricted w.

A full-refresh transition using this sampler has exactly rational acquired
point probabilities, acceptance one, spectral gap one, and an additive
two-coordinate energy comparison with constant one when both coordinates
are refreshed. These mixing claims concern the acquired bounded-width
refresh, with its charged contraction cost. They are not polynomial mixing
claims for an unrestricted local determinant sampler.

An uncapped fair-random-bit program is expected-time exact. The implemented
capped version uses at most R rejection trials per categorical choice; one
complete sample has TV error at most n 2^(-R). Thus a bounded-execution
TV-eta sampler follows using R>=ceil(log2(n/eta)). It avoids conditioning
on the rare event of an original projection and uses no floating arithmetic.

EXACT INTERFACE AND ENLARGED STATE

Fix k with 0<=2k<=n. A microstate is a pair (I,J) of disjoint subsets of sites,
|I|=|J|=k. Its point weight and partition are
  W(I,J)=prod_(i in I) lambda_i * |det F[I,J]|^2,
  C_k=sum_(I,J disjoint,|I|=|J|=k) W(I,J).
If C_k=0 the program reports zero exactly. Otherwise mu(I,J)=W/C_k.
Cauchy--Binet identifies the I marginal with the original selected paired-line
law det Gram({e_i,row_i F : i in I}) times prod lambda_i. J is an acquired
auxiliary orientation register, not an input-dependent uncharged oracle.
For the ambient-hole partition f(U), forbid sites in U and take
k=(n-|U|)/2. All remaining sites are then automatically occupied. Outside
U, row/column orientation is summed. Local allowed-label constraints support
all prefixes, force/exclude row/column sites, and impose a fixed retained set.

The support graph has undirected edge ij when i!=j and F_ij or F_ji is nonzero.
Diagonals never occur because I and J are disjoint. Relabel by the supplied
ordering 0,...,n-1. After processing 0,...,t, define the right frontier
  B_t={j>t : some i<=t has support edge ij}.
Set w=max_t |B_t| and d=max_i #{j>i : support edge ij}. This is a computed
certificate for an acquired ordering, not a free optimal-pathwidth oracle.
A bandwidth-b matrix has w<=b. Some long-edge graphs, including cycles in
the natural order, have w=2 even though the edge span is n-1.

EXACT TWO-DETERMINANT FRONTIER CONSTRUCTION

Label each site a_i in {0,+1,-1}; +1 means I, -1 means J, 0 is unoccupied.
For fixed labels define the skew matrix on occupied sites by, for i<j,
  K_a(i,j)=F_ij if a_i=+1,a_j=-1;
           -F_ji if a_i=-1,a_j=+1;
           0 otherwise.
The grouped row/column form is [[0,F[I,J]],[-F[I,J]^T,0]]. Hence
Pf(K_a)=s(a)*(-1)^(k(k-1)/2) det F[I,J], where s(a) in {+1,-1} is the
ordering sign. Therefore |Pf(K_a)|^2=|det F[I,J]|^2.
The Pfaffian expansion in site order is
  Pf(K_a)=sum_M (-1)^(cr(M)) prod_(ij in M,i<j) K_a(i,j),
where cr(M) counts pairs of arcs i<r<j<s. Expand the squared modulus as
independent perfect matchings M1,M2 on the SAME occupied sites, with common
labels, weights from K and its complex conjugate, and both crossing signs.
These are determinant replicas inside one point-weight calculation. No
same-cut restriction is imposed between two independent Markov microstates.

Scan sites in increasing order. A pending endpoint j>t in replica ell means
an edge from a processed vertex has been opened to j; its other endpoint has
not been processed. The frontier state records two endpoint subsets A,B and
a row-type subset P of A union B. Column type is the complement of P inside
the union. It also records the number q of row labels already processed.
At each frontier site there are exactly seven allowed records:
  no pending endpoint;
  pending in replica1 only, replica2 only, or both, each with row/column type.
Thus at most (k+1)7^w frontier states exist.

At site v, if v is pending in either replica its label is forced by P.
Otherwise choose 0,+1,-1, subject to allowed-label restrictions. Label0
is legal only with no pending endpoints, and opens nothing. An occupied
label closes each pending replica edge at v, or, in a replica without one,
opens a new edge to a future support neighbor j. A future endpoint j may
not already be pending in the same replica. Its required label is opposite
to the current one; if it is pending in the other replica, that type must
agree. A newly assigned future type is stored. Multiply lambda_v once when
v has row label and increment q. Both replicas independently enforce degree
one at every occupied site. End at the empty frontier and q=k.

The sign is acquired without storing an exponential permutation list:
when opening v->j in one replica, multiply (-1)^m, where m is the number
of its existing pending endpoints e with e<j. Every existing open edge has
an earlier opener u<v. If v<e<j it crosses the new edge; if v<j<e it nests
and does not cross. Thus every crossing is charged once, at the later
opener, and every nesting is charged zero times. Closing carries no new
sign. The program implements this as popcount(mask & ((1<<j)-1)).

Each fixed label/pair-of-matchings term occurs exactly once: the vertex scan
uniquely determines its close/open choices, and any legal DP history defines
such a term. The transition weight is precisely its Pfaffian-product weight
including the conjugate and the two crossing signs. Summing final states
therefore gives C_k with allowed labels. This proves the contraction, not
just its agreement on finite fixtures.

NEGATIVE INTERMEDIATE WEIGHTS ARE ESSENTIAL

The transfer coefficients and DP messages may be negative or complex.
They are exact Gaussian-integer algebra, not a positive matching circuit or
an even-windable decomposition. No signed DP edge is interpreted as a sample
probability. Only complete conditional norm sums are sampled, and those
are nonnegative by the determinant-squared identity just proved. Therefore
the four-site even-windability and shared-core sewing obstructions are not
contradicted or bypassed by silently assuming a positive representation.

DENOMINATORS, OPERAND HEIGHTS, AND COSTS

Let D clear the real and imaginary denominators of every F entry, so
G=DF is Gaussian integer. Let A clear all activity denominators and put
alpha_i=A lambda_i in nonnegative integers. The DP computes the integer
  N_k=sum_(I,J) prod_(i in I) alpha_i * |det G[I,J]|^2,
then C_k=N_k/(D^2 A)^k. Allowed-prefix denominators are the same, so every
categorical branch ratio is an exact rational ratio of nonnegative integers.
Use an LCM or product for clearing: its bit length is bounded by the sum of
input denominator bit lengths, hence polynomial. No eigenvalue, square root,
normalization, numerical determinant tolerance, or random zero test is used.

Write H>=1 for a bound on the absolute real/imaginary parts of G entries,
and Amax=max(1,max alpha_i). A partial DP coefficient is a signed sum of at
most 3^n(n+1)^(2n) local double-matching histories. A history has at most
2n edge factors and n activity factors. Its absolute magnitude is at most
(2H)^(2n) Amax^n. Thus every intermediate operand has
O(n(log(n+1)+log H+log Amax)) bits (with a harmless additional constant for
complex components), polynomial in input length. Cancellation cannot cause
unbounded operand height or a floating precision floor, because all arithmetic
is exact. Standard integer arithmetic consequently yields the claimed
polynomial-bit times 7^w cost.

There are at most three label options and (d+1)^2 endpoint choices per state.
One contraction takes O(n(k+1)(d+1)^2 7^w) ring operations and stores
O((k+1)7^w) Gaussian integers, plus input and masks. Mask operations have
polynomial(n) bit cost included in the bit complexity. The actual Python
implementation recomputes whole contractions during self reduction; it does
not claim a precomputed forward/backward optimization. A sample uses at most
3n+1 contractions. Storage stays at the single-contraction bound. Common
input-denominator factors are not repeatedly expanded inside the DP.

SELF-REDUCTION, INITIALIZATION, AND ACCEPTANCE

At a fixed prefix a_0,...,a_(v-1), compute the three exact child masses by
forcing a_v=-1,0,+1. They sum to the current mass. Choose a child by an
integer categorical draw and continue. The conditional masses are exactly
those of mu, so multiplication of the branch probabilities gives W/C_k.
A zero child is never selected. If C_k>0 an actual supported initialization
is acquired without a support theorem: at each site choose the first positive
child. This costs the same order as one sample and yields a witness. There
is no exponentially large precomputed prefix table.

For a uniform integer in [0,N), take b=ceil(log2 N) fair bits and reject
values >=N. Acceptance is at least1/2, and accepted values are uniform.
At most two trials are needed in expectation, with O(b) expected bits.
Every N has polynomial bit length. With R trials per choice and a positive
lexicographic fallback on failure, the implemented run has a fixed polynomial
bit budget times 7^w. Couple to the uncapped ideal algorithm; the chance of
any failure in n choices is at most n2^(-R). This proves TV<=eta for the
stated R. Conditioning on successful capped runs is NOT claimed to preserve
exactness. The ideal computational random primitive is an explicit fair-bit
assumption; Python's seeded diagnostics are not hardware-randomness evidence.

Set P(x,y)=mu(y). All transition probabilities are now acquired rationals
because both point weight and C_k are computed. Every transition is accepted;
detailed balance mu(x)P(x,y)=mu(x)mu(y) is exact. One refresh erases dependence
on the initialized state. On L2(mu), P is projection onto constants; centered
eigenvalues are zero and the spectral gap is one. The lazy version (I+P)/2
has gap1/2. For two coordinates and full-pair refresh Q=P tensor P,
  E_(mu tensor mu,Q)(f1(x)+f2(y))=Var_mu f1+Var_mu f2.
Hence Var_mu f1<=E with constant1. Independent single-coordinate refresh
chosen uniformly instead has gap1/2 and additive constant2. These are the
strongest supported mixing statements, with refresh cost paid in full.
The capped sampler approximates the refresh in TV and is not assigned the
exact stationary-law/spectral-gap claim.

ANNEALING AND TOTAL COMPUTATION

No annealing is necessary for this admitted class: C_k is computed exactly
and every requested rational activity law can be refreshed directly. If a
caller insists on rational multiplicative annealing for positive lambda_i,
use adjacent activities changing by at most c=1+1/n. At most k row factors
occur, so unnormalized point-weight ratios lie in [c^(-k),c^k], and normalized
density ratios in [c^(-2k),c^(2k)] subset [e^(-1),e] for k<=n/2.
If all endpoint activities lie in [2^(-B),2^B], connecting them to1 takes
O(n(B+1)) such tiers. Each tier costs its own acquired contraction/sample;
there is no free tier sampler or ratio oracle. Norm-ratio estimation is
unnecessary because both norms are exact outputs. Rational bit lengths grow
only polynomially along this schedule. Zero activities are handled directly
by deletion/forbidden row branches, not by a falsely positive annealing path.
The algorithm's physical energy use and backend compute energy are UNKNOWN;
only arithmetic, memory, random-bit and observed small-script costs are given.

INITIAL: OBSTRUCTION TO COMPRESSING THE STABLE HOLE DERIVATIVE INTO ACQUIRED DETERMINANT PRIMITIVES
Worker c02_s01. Requested gpt-6.1-sol / max; observed model UNKNOWN.
7 October 2026. Independent before peer exposure. Status: internally derived representation obstruction, not general counting hardness or a field-breaking algorithm.

STRONGEST CLAIM
There is an explicit sparse rational family of the actual hard-BCS matrix class whose signed ambient-hole polynomial cannot be represented by one constant-kernel determinant with each physical variable entering through a rank-one update, regardless of the number of fixed auxiliary modes or whether the kernel is Hermitian. A positive mixture of constant-parity Hermitian determinant boundary signatures preserving ALL hole coefficients requires at least ceil((4/3)^q) components for q independent four-site blocks. Thus the known chiral mixed derivative cannot generally be replaced by a polynomial-size positive mixture of those directly evaluable Gaussian/determinantal primitives.

This excludes a representation class distinct from the prior nonnegative matching/windability and shared-core-cut obstructions. It does not exclude a scalar-only reduction, a kernel with variable-dependent rational entries, higher-rank/cancellation determinant gadgets, an interacting auxiliary law, a localized flow, mismatch-register purification, or a general determinant-parity FPRAS. The block instances themselves are easy.

INTERFACE AND EXPLICIT WITNESS
For F in C^(n x n) and even R define Z_R(F)=sum_{I subset R, |I|=|R|/2}|det F[I,R\I]|^2, with Z_empty=1 and odd R zero. Write Q_F(x)=sum_R (-1)^(|R|/2) Z_R x^(V\R). Baseline proves Q_F is multiaffine real stable via a chiral determinant and positive mixed derivatives; that stability is not newly claimed here.
Take n=4 and F0=J4-I4: all twelve off-diagonal entries are one; diagonal entries zero. Every two-retained partition equals 2. Every complementary two-by-two minor on all four sites is the all-ones matrix and has determinant zero. Consequently Z_[4]=0 and
 Q0=x1x2x3x4-2 sum_{i<j}x_i x_j.
(The complement of a two-set is another two-set, so no indexing ambiguity remains.)
This uses unit entries; failure is not precision or a large-weight effect.

LEMMA 1: NO CONSTANT COMPLEX KERNEL
Suppose Q0=det(diag(x1,x2,x3,x4)+A) for an arbitrary complex 4x4 A. Comparing principal-minor coefficients gives Aii=0, Aij Aji=2 for every pair, every principal three-minor zero, and det A=0.
Diagonal similarity preserves all principal minors. Normalize A1j=i sqrt(2), Aj1=-i sqrt(2) for j=2,3,4; this is legal because every pair product is nonzero. For the principal triangle {1,i,j}, its determinant is
 A1i Aij Aj1+A1j Aji Ai1=2(Aij+Aji).
Thus Aji=-Aij. The pair products then give Aij^2=-2, so Aij=+/- i sqrt(2). All entries now have the form A=iK with K real skew-symmetric and every upper off-diagonal entry +/-sqrt(2). Therefore
 det A=det K=Pf(K)^2
 = [K12 K34-K13 K24+K14 K23]^2
 =4 or 36,
because the bracket is twice the sum of three numbers in {+1,-1}. This contradicts det A=0. The argument is elementary and excludes non-Hermitian constant kernels as well.

POSITIVE NONZERO ROBUST VARIANT
Put F_delta=F0+delta E12, rational 0<delta<=1/4. Exact enumeration gives
 Z_[4]=2 delta^2,
 Z_12=2+2delta+delta^2,
 Z_ij=2 for the other five pairs,
 and every odd Z_R=0.
A hypothetical constant complex determinant kernel is again diagonally similar to iK real skew, now |Kij|=sqrt(Z_ij). Its three Pfaffian-term magnitudes are
 p=sqrt(4+4delta+2delta^2), 2, 2.
As 2<=p<=2+2delta, every signed sum of those magnitudes has absolute value at least 4-p>=2-2delta. Hence det A >=(2-2delta)^2>2delta^2, contradiction. Choosing delta=2^-L gives a positive requested full norm and an O(L)-bit input, still with no exact such representation. No asymptotic numerical simulation is used.

LEMMA 2: FIXED AUXILIARY MODES CANNOT REPAIR A RANK-ONE PHYSICAL DETERMINANT
Consider any finite-dimensional representation
 Q(x)=c det(A+sum_{i=1}^n x_i u_i v_i^T),
where A,u_i,v_i are fixed complex data and [x1...xn]Q=1. The top coefficient being nonzero forces the u_i to be linearly independent and independently the v_i to be linearly independent: the coefficient is alternating multilinear in each family. By invertible left/right transformations place u_i and v_i^T on the first n coordinate axes. The transformed pencil becomes
 [ X+B  C ; D  E ], X=diag(x).
Its x1...xn coefficient is a nonzero scalar times det E, so E is invertible. Schur complement reduces the polynomial, after normalization of its top coefficient, to
 Q(x)=det(X+B-C E^-1 D).
Thus any number of fixed auxiliary modes and any nonorthogonal rank-one boundary directions reduce to a constant n-by-n kernel. Lemma 1 rules them out for F0 and F_delta. The Hermitian/PSD rank-one case u_i v_i^T=b_i b_i* is a special case. Empty auxiliary block has determinant one.
This lemma is not a no-go for arbitrary linear determinant circuit representations: higher-rank physical coefficients can exploit cancellations and are outside this interface.

LEMMA 3: EXPONENTIAL POSITIVE DETERMINANT-MIXTURE SIZE
For q>=1 take F^(q)=diag(F0,...,F0) on q blocks V_b of size four. Its partitions factor:
 Z_R=product_b Z_(R intersect V_b)(F0).
Indeed block-diagonal complementary minors vanish unless each block has equal selected row/column counts, then determinants and squared sums multiply. Thus a retained block may be empty (weight1), a two-set (weight2), or full/odd (weight0).
Assume its full hole signature is a positive mixture
 Z_R=sum_{ell=1}^s c_ell a_ell(R), c_ell>0,
with each a_ell(R)=(-1)^(|R|/2)det A_ell[R] for even R, each odd principal minor zero, each even a_ell(R)>=0, and A_ell Hermitian. Components are monic at R=empty; sum c_ell=1. These are the exact constant-parity positive Hermitian determinant primitives under consideration.
For i,j in different blocks, target Z_ij=0. Since a_ell(ij)=|A_ell,ij|^2>=0, every A_ell,ij=0. Each A_ell is block diagonal. Target Z_(V_b)=0 and nonnegative component weights give det A_ell[V_b]=0. Odd principal minors zero give trace zero and all size-three principal minors zero. The characteristic polynomial of the Hermitian four-by-four block is therefore
 t^4 - S t^2, S=sum_{i<j}|A_ell,ij|^2>=0.
Hence each block has eigenvalues sqrt(S),-sqrt(S),0,0 and rank at most two.
For any such Hermitian rank-two zero-diagonal block write A=u u*-v v*. The zero diagonal implies |u_i|^2=|v_i|^2=p_i. Its pair matrix W_ij=|Aij|^2 has the decomposition
 W=2 p p^T - w bar(w)^T - bar(w) w^T, w_i=u_i bar(v_i).
Consequently rank W<=3, including missing-edge/zero-block cases. This derivation does not require every component support to be complete or a real-skew gauge.
Now make a 4^q-by-4^q matrix T indexed by row tuple i=(i1,...,iq) and column tuple j=(j1,...,jq). If all ib!=jb, its entry is the partition on the retained two-set {ib,jb} in each block; if some ib=jb use the corresponding odd retained block and entry0. For the target,
 T=W0 tensor ... tensor W0, W0=2(J4-I4),
 so rank T=4^q (W0 eigenvalues6,-2,-2,-2). For component ell its matrix is the tensor product of its q pair matrices, each of rank<=3, hence rank<=3^q. Rank subadditivity in the positive mixture yields
 4^q=rank T<=s 3^q, so s>=ceil((4/3)^q).
The bound permits arbitrarily large fixed auxiliary determinants in every component by Lemma 2 in the Hermitian rank-one boundary case. It excludes exact polynomial mixtures preserving all coefficients. Signed mixtures, components with nonzero odd coefficients cancelling across components, or nonlinear output relabelings are not covered.

C02_S02 INDEPENDENT INITIAL — GENERAL VECTOR-PAIR STABILITY AND ITS LIMIT
Worker c02_s02; requested gpt-6.1-sol/max; observed model UNKNOWN.

STATUS / EXACT STRONGEST CLAIM

INTERNALLY DERIVED, relying on the established Borcea–Branden finite-degree
symbol theorem: the signed ambient-hole polynomial is real stable for EVERY
finite list of arbitrary complex vector pairs, with arbitrary nonnegative real
pair activities. The e_i,row_i(F) shape and coordinate-only auxiliary restriction
are unnecessary. Therefore arbitrary vector-pair additions, deletions, ambient
basis changes, and any finite pair-subdivision gadgets retain this stability.
This broadens the baseline's earned coordinate-pair closure. It does not turn
an implicit coefficient sum into a polynomial-time coefficient oracle.

For its positive Hurwitz rotation, all nonzero bit-pinned distributions and
all positive external fields satisfy the sharp absolute row-influence bound
 sum_{j != i}|P(X_j=1|X_i=1)-P(X_j=1|X_i=0)| <= 1.
Consequently Cov(X) <= 2 diag(p_i(1-p_i)), and both the nonhomogeneous hole
polynomial and its complement homogenization are 1/2-fractionally log-concave.
The parameter 1/2 is sharp. These influence/FLC facts are established sector-
stability technology (AASV Thm51/Lemmas69,71), reconstructed below using a strip
Schwarz argument, not claimed as new general coefficient inequalities.

Fixed ambient-hole-cardinality slices retain a constant sector of aperture
pi/2 by AASV Theorem61, hence at least 1/4-FLC by its Lemma69. They need not be
Hurwitz stable. This is a different representation from the bad line-label
polynomial. An aggregate down-up walk exists with polynomial gap conditional
on coefficient access, by that paper's Theorem24; coefficient access remains
precisely absent for general determinant parity.

A new robustness formulation also rules out a proposed rescue: no constant-FLC
positive marginal lift can approximate the natural hard-cycle pair-label law
within any fixed TV delta with 1-delta-4delta^2>0, uniformly in cycle length.
For a 2q-cycle its FLC parameter must be <=1/[q(1-delta-4delta^2)]. Thus adding
positive hidden variables cannot repair this particular label representation
while preserving constant FLC and accurate positive marginalization.

I. GENERAL VECTOR-PAIR THEOREM: COMPLETE DERIVATION

Supply n, m, vectors u_l,v_l in C^n and activities lambda_l >= 0, l=1,...,m.
For U subset [n], let R=[n]\U and define f(U)=0 if |R| is odd; otherwise
 f(U)=sum_{A subset [m], |A|=|R|/2} lambda_A |det V_A[R]|^2,
where V_A consists of the ordered columns (u_l,v_l) for l in A and lambda_A
is their product. The empty determinant is one, so f([n])=1. Repeated or zero
vectors and zero activities are allowed. Define
 Q(x)=sum_U (-1)^((n-|U|)/2) f(U) x^U.
Claim: Q is real stable and multiaffine, without any genericity assumption.

Put A_l=u_l u_l*+v_l v_l*, Hermitian PSD of rank at most two, and
 P(x,t)=det(diag(x)+sum_l t_l A_l).
When all x_i,t_l have strictly positive imaginary part, the imaginary part
of this matrix is diag(Im x)+sum_l(Im t_l)A_l >0, so P is nonzero. P has real
coefficients because its value is real for real x,t, is multiaffine in each
x_i, and has degree at most two in each t_l by rank(A_l)<=2.

On polynomials with degree at most two in t_l, define the linear map
 T_l(a+b t_l+c t_l^2)=a-lambda_l c.
Extend it as the identity on all other variables. For degree bound
kappa=(1,...,1;2,...,2), its algebraic symbol is
 (s_l^2-lambda_l) prod_i(x_i+y_i)
                         prod_{k != l}(t_k+s_k)^2.
Every linear sum is upper-half-plane nonzero. The factor s_l^2-lambda_l has
only the real roots +/-sqrt(lambda_l), also for lambda_l=0. Thus the whole
symbol is real stable. Borcea–Branden 0809.0401v3, Theorem1.2(b), proves that
T_l preserves real stability or gives the zero polynomial. Applying all T_l
therefore preserves stability or zero. The coefficient x^[n] remains one,
so the output is nonzero.

To identify it, Cauchy–Binet expands P as
 P(x,t)=sum_{B subset the 2m vector columns, |B|<=n}
   (prod_{column in B} t_{line(column)})
   sum_{R subset[n], |R|=|B|} |det V_B[R]|^2 x^[n]\R.
A monomial with t_l-degree one is killed by T_l. Degree zero is retained;
degree two acquires -lambda_l. The surviving B select both columns from
exactly the lines A. Thus the output is exactly Q, with the asserted signs
and f(U). No row-shape or orthogonality condition was used.

In particular, append ANY arbitrary pair (u,v) with activity lambda and repeat
the same proof with one more PSD rank-two matrix. This closes arbitrary
vector-pair extensions at the representation level. In general the updated
Q cannot be computed solely as a fixed coordinate-derivative operator on
old Q: cross-minor interference retains information missing from the old
norm coefficients. The theorem reconstructs Q from the supplied vectors;
it does not assert an old-Q coefficient recursion for noncoordinate pairs.

II. HURWITZ ROTATION, TWISTS AND PINNINGS

Define h(x)=i^(-n)Q(i x)=sum_U f(U)x^U. Its coefficients are nonnegative,
its parity is n modulo2, and h is nonvanishing when Re x_i>0, since Im(i x_i)>0.
For arbitrary T subset[n], the reciprocal twist
 h^T(x)=x^T h((1/x_i)_{i in T},(x_i)_{i not in T})
is multiaffine, positive-coefficient and Hurwitz stable: Re(1/z)>0 when
Re z>0. Its coefficients are f(T symmetric-difference U); its parity shifts
by |T|. Positive external fields, zero specializations and coefficient-of-x_i
operations preserve Hurwitz stability or zero. The latter coefficient is
lim_{a->infinity} h(x_i=a)/a, by multiaffinity and Hurwitz continuity.
Every nonzero bit-pinned law is therefore included. Ordinary real upper-
half-plane stability of unsigned h is neither needed nor asserted.

III. SHARP INFLUENCE BOUND: SELF-CONTAINED ANALYTIC STEP

The next proof works for ANY nonzero nonnegative multiaffine Hurwitz-stable h,
without a parity assumption. Supply arbitrary positive fields lambda. Fix i
with 0<p_i<1 and write h=A+x_i B. A and B are Hurwitz stable and nonzero, by
boundary specialization and coefficient limits. For other arguments z in the
right half-plane, the root -A(z)/B(z) cannot have positive real part. Hence
Re(A/B)>=0. Since A/B is positive real at positive real arguments, the strong
minimum principle yields Re(A/B)>0 everywhere, so R=B/A has positive real
part as well. This also covers constant ratios.

For any signs s_j in {-1,+1}, j!=i, put z_j=lambda_j exp(s_j t). If
|Im t|<pi/2, these z_j remain in the right half-plane. The analytic function
 L(t)=Log R(z(t))
with principal logarithm maps this strip into itself; L(0) is real. The maps
zeta -> 2 artanh zeta and w -> tanh(w/2) are inverse maps from the unit disk
to this strip. Therefore
 F(zeta)=tanh((L(2 artanh zeta)-L(0))/2)
is an analytic disk self-map vanishing at0. Schwarz's lemma gives
 |L'(0)|=|F'(0)|<=1.
But
 L'(0)=sum_{j!=i} s_j [P(X_j=1|X_i=1)-P(X_j=1|X_i=0)].
Choose the signs of the bracketed real numbers; this proves the asserted
absolute influence sum. Deterministic coordinates can simply be omitted.
All pinnings and arbitrary positive fields are already included by II.

Let C=Cov(X), d_i=p_i(1-p_i). For i!=j the influence is C_ij/d_i, so
sum_{j!=i}|C_ij|<=d_i. For every real v,
 v^T C v <=sum_i[d_i+sum_{j!=i}|C_ij|]v_i^2 <=2 sum_i d_i v_i^2.
This proves C<=2 diag(d), including deterministic coordinates.

For any nonnegative generating polynomial and alpha>0, direct differentiation
at x=1 gives Hess log h(x^alpha)=alpha^2 C-alpha diag(p); positive scaling
transports this identity to every point. At alpha=1/2 the covariance inequality
therefore proves global fractional log-concavity.

For the complement homogenization
 H(x,y)=sum_U f(U)x^U y^[n]\U=(prod_i y_i) h(x_i/y_i),
its covariance is [[C,-C],[-C,C]]. For arbitrary a,b,
 (a-b)^T C(a-b)<=2 sum_i p_i(1-p_i)(a_i-b_i)^2
                  <=2 sum_i[p_i a_i^2+(1-p_i)b_i^2],
using the exact identity
 p a^2+(1-p)b^2-p(1-p)(a-b)^2=(p a+(1-p)b)^2.
Thus H is also 1/2-FLC under arbitrary x,y fields. Equivalently H has constant
aperture pi/2 directly, since x_i/y_i lies in the right half-plane when both
x_i,y_i have arguments between -pi/4 and pi/4. The 1/2-FLC improvement over
the generic quarter bound is the established AASV Lemma71.

Sharpness: h=1+x_1 x_2 is Hurwitz stable. At equal fields, C=(1/4)J_2,
p_i=1/2, so along (1,1), the FLC Hessian eigenvalue is alpha^2/2-alpha/2,
positive for alpha>1/2? This displayed eigenvalue is alpha^2/2-alpha/2 at
probability 1/2 and would only obstruct alpha>1. The sharp global limit instead
uses fields tending to zero: for both-occupied probability p->0, its eigenvalue
is 2 alpha^2 p(1-p)-alpha p, which is positive for alpha>1/2 and sufficiently
small p. The complement homogenization is sharp already at equal fields:
Cov of its four indicator variables has top eigenvalue1, diag(p)=I/2,
so alpha^2-alpha/2>0 for alpha>1/2. This records the field-sensitive distinction.

IV. CARDINALITY SLICES AND THE ORACLE BOUNDARY

AASV 2102.02708v3 Theorem61 states that every homogeneous degree component
of a constant-parity Hurwitz-stable polynomial is either zero or sector-stable
with aperture pi/2. Its proof uses a univariate even-degree reduction and
half-plane stability of elementary symmetric polynomials of roots. Together
with Lemma69 this gives 1/4-FLC for each fixed-hole-cardinality law.
The full Hurwitz property is lost: the degree-two part of
 (1+x_1 x_2)(1+x_3 x_4)
is x_1 x_2+x_3 x_4; it vanishes at x_1=x_2=exp(i*pi/4),
x_3=x_4=exp(-i*pi/4), all in the right half-plane.

Theorem24 of the same primary paper then supplies a polynomial-gap constant-
block down-up walk on the hole states (or on complement-homogenized states).
Every up-step evaluates polynomially many coefficients f(U). These f(U) are
sums of determinant squares over all line subsets; general evaluation is the
original counting problem. Even f(empty) is the target partition. A single
microscopic determinant is computable in polynomial bit time, but is not
that coefficient sum. Stability, good support acquisition and a polynomial
number of calls to an unavailable coefficient oracle do not close acquisition.
No unknown balancing or coefficient ratios are silently supplied here.

V. ROBUST POSITIVE-LIFT OBSTRUCTION FOR NATURAL LINE LABELS

For the directed 2q-cycle matrix F, the hard full bases have only the two
alternating pair subsets A,B, of size q, with equal unit weights. Let mu be
their equal mixture. Let nu be any distribution on q-subsets of [2q], with
TV(nu,mu)<=delta. Set v_i=+1 on A and -1 on B, and Y=sum_i v_i X_i.
Then |Y|<=q, mu(Y)=0, mu(Y^2)=q^2, while nu(A union B)>=1-delta.
Therefore E_nu Y^2>=(1-delta)q^2 and |E_nu Y|<=2delta q, whence
 Var_nu Y>=(1-delta-4delta^2)q^2.
If the natural multilinear generating polynomial of nu is alpha-FLC, its
Hessian criterion gives alpha Cov(X)<=diag(p). Testing v and using
sum p_i=q yields alpha Var Y<=q. Thus
 alpha <=1/[q(1-delta-4delta^2)]
whenever the denominator is positive. This remains O(1/q) at delta=0.1.

If a positive hidden-variable polynomial G(z,a) is constant-alpha FLC and
specializing its hidden variables a to positive constants produces this
accurate pair-label marginal, that specialization remains alpha-FLC.
Consequently no such family with alpha>0 fixed can work uniformly in q,
regardless of hidden-variable count. Exact sector-stable positive lifts are
also excluded: marginal specialization retains its sector, but the exact
cycle polynomial has a zero at arguments +/-pi/(2q). Approximate sector
lifts inherit the constant-FLC obstruction via AASV Lemma69. This statement
allows leakage to other q-subsets and applies to accurate TV approximation;
it does not exclude nonlinear decoding, signed amplitudes, nonpositive
representations, or hole-label algorithms. The cycle family itself has an
easy dynamic program, so this is not hardness or a universal sampler lower bound.

STRONGEST EARNED CLAIM

An acquired inverse representation enlarges the hard-BCS bipartite compiler
at FULL SINGLE OCCUPANCY. Input F is an explicit n by n Gaussian-rational
matrix, n=2k. If F is invertible and the off-diagonal support of F^{-1} is
bipartite, the normalized hard k-pair state of F is, up to one known global
phase, exactly the hard k-pair state of H=F^{-1}-diag(F^{-1}). Thus its norm,
every physical spin-prefix mass, exact zero/support decision, and coherent
phase can be compiled through the existing bipartite graph-Slater interface.
Recognition, rational representation, coefficient oracles and deterministic
prefix witnesses are acquired; the conditional imported counting FPRAS and
coherent circuit are NOT executed by this worker.

A finite explicitly specified list of rational scalar diagonal shifts may
be tried before inversion. Diagonal shifts preserve every hard-sector minor;
at n=2k, an accepted shifted inverse again gives a global scalar identity.
Trying s shifts costs O(s n^3) rational-complex arithmetic plus support scans.
The default executable recognizer tries zero; any finite shift list and its
encoding are part of the charged interface, not an uncharged gauge oracle.

This is a representation-class extension relative to the common baseline,
not a general weighted-parity FPRAS, acquired hole balancing, a new gauge
principle, or an externally reviewed breakthrough. Classical Jacobi duality
and half-filled projected-state gauge invariance have prior art. The scoped
conditional compiler corollary and implemented admission/support interface
are the contribution claimed here; priority is unverified.

1. TARGET AND COMPLETE INVERSE IDENTITY

At hard t_i=0, amplitudes in canonical up-then-down Fock order are
 a_F(I,J)=(-1)^{k(k-1)/2} det F[I,J],
 where |I|=|J|=k and I intersect J is empty.
For n=2k, every surviving pair has J=I^c. Let K=F+D be invertible, with D
diagonal. Disjoint minors never use diagonal entries, so
 det F[I,I^c]=det K[I,I^c].

The complementary-minor identity gives, for EVERY I of size k,
 det K[I,I^c]=sigma det K det K^{-1}[I,I^c],
 sigma=(-1)^{n(n-1)/2}=(-1)^k.                         (1)

Self-contained block derivation: permute K's rows into (I,I^c) and columns
into (I^c,I). Their joint permutation sign is sigma because the two index
sums cover all coordinates and k(k-1) is even. In the resulting block matrix
T=[[A,B],[C,E]], A=K[I,I^c], the lower-right inverse block is precisely
K^{-1}[I,I^c]. If A is invertible, the Schur formulas yield
 det T det (T^{-1})_22
 = det A det(E-CA^{-1}B) det(E-CA^{-1}B)^{-1}=det A.
After multiplying away det K denominators this is a polynomial identity,
so it holds by continuity for every invertible K, including singular A.
Using det T=sigma det K proves (1). No conjugation or state-dependent sign
is introduced. Removing the diagonal of K^{-1} likewise does not affect
the complementary minors. Therefore for H=stripdiag(K^{-1}),
 a_F(I,I^c)=gamma a_H(I,I^c), gamma=(-1)^k det K.       (2)

Every physical prefix restricts the very same set of spin strings. Equation
(2) holds term by term, so its unnormalized mass obeys
 Z_F(prefix)=|det K|^2 Z_H(prefix).                    (3)
The common scalar cancels from all conditional probabilities and contributes
only a global phase to the normalized state. No inverse original norm or
postselection probability occurs.

2. ACQUIRED BIPARTITE GRAPH-SLATER COMPILER

Scan H_{ij} and H_{ji} exactly and two-color its undirected nonzero support.
Rejection returns an odd closed walk. Let colors A,B have sizes a,b. With
P=H_AB and Q=H_BA, the existing exact both-spin particle-hole transformation
on B produces rank-2b graph Slater
 G=[M;I_(2b)], M=[[0,P],[-Q^T,0]]
in mode order (up_A,down_A,hole-up_B,hole-down_B). All entries are rational
complex; the bottom identity ensures full column rank independently of H.

At physical full single occupancy, the transformed quota is exactly one
mode at EVERY site. Thus if 2b != n the norm is exactly zero. Otherwise this
is the already admitted N63 single-site partition quota, not an overlapping
flavor quota. A balanced total color count is not assumed sufficient: exact
row-linear/partition matroid intersection still decides whether a support
exists. It also handles unbalanced connected components and cancellations.

Physical spin prefixes map explicitly to forced/excluded transformed rows:
 on A, U chooses up_A and D chooses down_A;
 on B, U chooses hole-down_B and D chooses hole-up_B.
The other row at a fixed site is excluded. The code contracts the forced
rows in the row-linear matroid and the one-per-site partition matroid,
then uses shortest augmenting paths to acquire a maximum common independent
set. Dependence of forced rows is an exact zero certificate. When no path
exists, the reachable set R gives the standard contracted rank-sum bound
 r_linear(E\R)+r_partition(R)=|chosen|;
adding the forced count is less than n and certifies infeasibility. Positive
outputs include an actual physical up/down witness and its nonzero rational
minor evaluated in ORIGINAL F. Every output is independently checkable.

The executable implementation uses straightforward exact Gaussian-rational
elimination, not a randomized FPRAS output, to make these decisions. A
naive bounded implementation needs O(n) augmentations, O(n^2) rank queries
per augmentation and O(n^3) field operations per rank: O(n^6) rational-complex
operations per prefix. This intentionally loose polynomial charges the
support acquisition. It is not the optimized matroid-intersection exponent.

3. BIT, COUNT, PHASE AND RESOURCE INTERFACES

Let L be the total binary encoding length of F and of the finite shift list.
Clear real/imaginary denominators by their product D, whose bit length is
at most L. Gaussian-integer determinants and cofactors have O(n(L+log n))
bits by their permutation/Hadamard bound; exact inversion consequently has
polynomial entry and output length, even for ill-conditioned K. A tiny
determinant changes the encoded rational size, not the number of inversions.
Exact rank operations likewise have determinant-bounded polynomial bits.

Coefficient oracles use |det G_S|^2 and the one-per-site quota indicator.
For an acquired common denominator E of G, positive squared minors are at
least E^{-2n}; a Hadamard upper bound provides a polynomial log coefficient
range. Prefix restrictions inherit that bound. Thus all Chen--Liu/N63
input witnesses, rational oracles and coarse range bounds are acquired.

Conditional on Chen--Liu 2610.06724v1 Theorem 5.1/Remark A.4 and N63's
finite-bit reversible compiler, counting/sampling/coherent preparation have
no inverse original hard norm factor. With m=2n and
 B=m+log(R_f R_g)=poly(n,L), the inherited coherent counting component is
 O(m^13 B^2 eps^{-2} log^2(2Bm/eps) log(m/(delta eps))).
Add exact recognition/prefix arithmetic, reversible histories/uncomputation,
polynomial-bit rotation and phase synthesis, and the known O(n^2) fermionic
PH/mode-permutation circuit. The inverse branch needs no physical gauge
circuit because (2) already identifies the normalized physical vectors.
The optional global phase gamma/|gamma| is computable from its rational
real/imaginary parts. Source success is over the compiler tape; good tapes
are not assumed efficiently recognizable.

This worker executed only algebra and support code. No field-acquisition
FPRAS, conditional Born sampler, synthesized quantum circuit, Hamiltonian
ground-state calculation or hardware experiment was executed. Gate depth,+physical energy and backend GPU/tokens are UNKNOWN. The inherited very high
polynomial does not imply practical preparation.

4. STRICT EXTENSION AND EXACT BOUNDARY

Take
 K=[[2,0,1,1],[0,3,1,2],[1,1,5,0],[1,2,0,7]],
 F=K^{-1}=(1/121)*[[78,17,-19,-16],[17,58,-15,-19],
                  [-19,-15,31,7],[-16,-19,7,25]].
F is symmetric and all six undirected off-diagonal edges are nonzero:
its support is K4, so the existing bipartite pair-matrix recognizer rejects.
Removing K's diagonal leaves bipartite support A={0,1},B={2,3}; the inverse
compiler accepts. At k=2, exact norms are Z_H=12 and Z_F=12/14641.
This is an explicit dense nonbipartite admitted input, not a relabeled
monomial or a claim about bipartite mean-field Hamiltonians.

The restriction n=2k is essential. In the SAME n=4 instance at k=1,
 I={0},J={1} has F_{01}=17/121 !=0 but H_{01}=0.
Hence the proposed inverse replacement changes support when holes are
allowed. Jacobi then maps to COMPLEMENTARY minors with overlapping index
sets and degree n-k; it does not return the original hard sector. This
exact counterexample refutes an unrestricted all-k inverse shortcut.

STRONGEST CLAIM

For every explicit rational-complex opposite-spin pair matrix F of size n,
the fully hard-projected BCS charge coefficients

 c_k(F) = sum_{I cap J=empty, |I|=|J|=k} |det F[I,J]|^2

and every physical occupation prefix are exactly computable by a local
Grassmann elimination algorithm in poly(n,L) 2^{O(w)} bit operations, given
an elimination order of width w for the undirected off-diagonal site support
of F. Its direct, deliberately loose arithmetic count is

 O((n+d) (n+1)^2 3^{4(w+1)}),

where d is the number of nonzero off-diagonal directed entries. This includes
all coefficients in one call. Sequential canonical or grandcanonical Born
sampling takes at most 3n+1 such calls and n ceil(log2(2n/epsilon)) random
bits, up to an immaterial n=0 convention, with TV error <=epsilon on every
execution. It has no inverse sector norm or filter-success-probability cost.

This gives an acquired exact polynomial-bit counting/sampling subclass when
the site support has treewidth O(log n): obtain a width <=2w+1 decomposition
using Korhonen's primary Theorem 1 in 2^{O(w)} n time, turn it into an
elimination order, and apply the explicit counter. The external treewidth
acquisition algorithm is a stated dependency and was NOT implemented here.
The implemented counter acquires and checks a greedy order; its correctness
holds for every order, but greedy width need not approximate optimal width.

The result applies to connected nonbipartite matrices of unbounded rank and
component size, with arbitrary complex phases and ill conditioning. It is
not the general weighted-parity FPRAS. It does not establish approximation
hardness, postselected universality inside this precise static interface,
novelty, or a field-breaking result. Bounded-width Grassmann/tensor contraction
is established general methodology; target-specific priority is unverified.

STATUS: complete self-contained algebra/algorithm derivation below, with
exact executable finite checks. Correctness is internally derived and awaits
independent review. F-only logarithmic-treewidth acquisition imports a
published graph algorithm, rather than supplying an unknown balancing or
counting oracle. General hard F remains UNRESOLVED.

1. EXACT INPUT AND GRAPH

F in Q(i)^(n x n) is fully supplied. L is its full binary encoding length.
Physical modes are u_i,d_i. The k-pair sector, in canonical up-then-down
order, has amplitudes (-1)^{k(k-1)/2} det F[I,J] with |I|=|J|=k.
Hard onsite projection kills I cap J !=empty. The target Born law is the
nonnegative squared-amplitude law on physical 0/u/d occupations, conditioned
on k if requested. Zero sectors must return exact zero, not a normalized
sample. The grandcanonical norm at fugacity one is sum_k c_k.

The graph G_F has sites as vertices and {i,j}, i!=j, as an edge exactly
when F_ij or F_ji is nonzero. Diagonal F is irrelevant: every surviving minor
has disjoint row/column sets and cannot use F_ii. This deletion is used only
at hard sites; this checkpoint's implemented theorem has every site hard.
No pair-matrix acquisition, Hamiltonian ground-state assumption, variational
optimization, bipartite Hamiltonian claim, or favorable physical overlap is
included.

An order has induced width w if, eliminating its sites and cliquing the
remaining neighbors at each deletion, each deleted site has at most w
remaining neighbors. This property is checked exactly from the support graph
in polynomial time. A width-w tree decomposition yields such an order by
leaf-bag elimination: eliminate vertices exclusive to a leaf bag, delete
redundant bags, and repeat. Its fill edges stay within a containing bag,
so width does not increase.

2. CHIRAL DETERMINANT IDENTITY

Introduce diagonal X=diag(x_i), Y=diag(y_i), and let

 D_F(x,y)=det [[X,F],[-F*,Y]],

where F* denotes Hermitian transpose. The following coefficient expansion
is exact as a formal polynomial:

 D_F(x,y)=sum_{|I|=|J|} |det F[I,J]|^2
                     product_{i notin I} x_i product_{j notin J} y_j. (1)

To prove (1), choose which diagonal entries of X and Y are used in a
determinant term. The unused top rows/columns form I; the unused bottom
rows/columns form J. The remaining matrix is
 [[0,F[I,J]],[-F*[J,I],0]].
It has zero determinant unless |I|=|J|. At common size k, exchanging the
two k-column blocks gives sign (-1)^k, and the k minus signs in the lower
left block cancel it. Its determinant is therefore |det F[I,J]|^2. Deleting
principal diagonal rows and columns has positive cofactor sign. This proves
each coefficient and hence (1), including singular and complex inputs.

Define a local linear functional T_i on the four possible monomials:

 T_i(1)=0, T_i(x_i)=1, T_i(y_i)=z, T_i(x_i y_i)=1.

Then product_i T_i applied to D_F is exactly sum_k c_k z^k. The four local
choices correspond respectively to forbidden double occupancy, down only,
up only, and empty. The z exponent counts up occupations; (1) ensures this
equals the pair count. For a prefix, simply set the functional to zero on
any physical 0/u/d choice excluded by that prefix. Spin-mode prefixes can
be converted into the permitted local choices in constant time per site.

3. FOUR-GENERATOR LOCAL GRASSMANN LIFT

Work in an exterior algebra over Q(i)[z] with generators ordered by sites,

 (bar u_i, u_i, bar d_i, d_i), i=0,...,n-1.

For every two distinct generators theta_a,theta_b, theta_a theta_b equals
minus theta_b theta_a, and theta_a^2=0. Put A_i=bar u_i u_i and
B_i=bar d_i d_i. Let integral denote extraction of the coefficient of the
full ordered product product_i A_i B_i, with that coefficient normalized
to +1. The standard determinant identity is

 integral exp(sum_{a,b} bar psi_a M_ab psi_b)=det M.       (2)

For completeness, expanding the exponential can supply a full monomial
only by choosing each bar psi and psi once. Thus terms are indexed by
permutations; sorting their generators produces the permutation sign,
and their coefficients multiply the corresponding matrix entries. This
is precisely the Leibniz determinant. The generator order convention makes
M=identity have coefficient +1, which fixes the only global sign.
Reordering spin labels into site order permutes both rows and columns of
M, leaving its determinant unchanged.

All bilinear terms have even degree and commute in this algebra. Hence
the diagonal part of (2) factorizes as product_i (1+x_i A_i)(1+y_i B_i).
Applying T_i to its factor gives the local hard weight

 P_i=A_i+z B_i+A_i B_i.                                  (3)

The off-diagonal factors for each directed nonzero entry F_ij, i!=j, are

 E_ij=(1+F_ij bar u_i d_j)
       (1-conjugate(F_ij) bar d_j u_i).                   (4)

Therefore the exact requested polynomial is

 Z_F(z)=integral [product_i P_i product_{i!=j,F_ij!=0} E_ij]. (5)

This has one local four-generator factor per site and a constant-size
even factor per support edge. It preserves complex interference inside
determinants; its intermediate coefficients need not be nonnegative.
There is no positive matching-lift assertion. Restricting any prefix only
removes terms from (3), without changing the support graph or width.

4. EXPLICIT ELIMINATION AND ITS SIGNS

Represent a message by a dictionary (generator subset, z degree) -> Q(i)
coefficient. Multiplication is the exterior product: products sharing a
generator vanish; otherwise sort the union and multiply by (-1)^N where
N counts inversions between the two input subsets. Add coincident terms.
This is implemented directly, including exact real and imaginary Fractions.

Initially factors have scopes {i} or {i,j}. At elimination of site i,
multiply all factors whose scope contains i, then extract only monomials
containing ALL FOUR generators of i and delete those four. The resulting
message has scope equal to the bucket's scope minus i. Keep factors outside
the bucket unchanged. Finish by multiplying the scalar messages.

This computes the integral (5). Indeed, only bucket factors contain any
generator of i, so factors outside can be pulled past that integral. Every
factor has even total degree, all messages remain even after removing four
generators, and a complete four-generator site block commutes with every
monomial. Thus moving that full block to perform its coefficient extraction
causes no additional sign. If some monomial lacks any generator of i, no
outside factor can complete it, so it contributes zero. This justifies
the local elimination step and proves correctness inductively for ANY order.
This is not an appeal to a sign-free contraction heuristic.

Factor scopes stay cliques in the graph formed by the order's fill edges.
Consequently the union of each elimination bucket has at most w+1 sites.
Only 4(w+1) exterior generators are ever needed in one multiplication.
The z degree never exceeds n. There are at most (n+1)2^{4(w+1)} table
entries, and disjoint-subset exterior multiplication has at most
3^{4(w+1)} choices times (n+1)^2 degree pairs. There are O(n+d) factor
multiplications in total. Polynomial scanning, support, width checking,
and dictionary overhead are additional. This proves the stated arithmetic
bound. Space is also poly(n)2^{O(w)}.

All rational arithmetic has polynomial-size operands. Clear real/imaginary
input denominators using their product D, with log D=O(L). Every original
edge coefficient occurs at most twice in an expanded term, so one may use
a common denominator dividing D^2 for every intermediate coefficient.
A loose expansion bound allows <=2^{2d}3^n terms, each multiplying <=2d
entry components of bit size O(L). Therefore every coefficient has
O((n+d)(L+1)) bits, with a harmless larger polynomial bound if a single
global denominator is used at each primitive arithmetic step. No numerical
condition number or inverse determinant magnitude is assumed. Exact zeros
and phases do not need an additive-to-relative approximation conversion.

5. ACQUIRED CLASSICAL SAMPLES WITH FINITE RANDOM BITS

Count the requested sector (or sum coefficients for grandcanonical), and
reject exactly when its norm is zero. Visit physical sites one by one.
For each allowed local value 0/u/d, count the prefix with that site fixed.
The three nonnegative rational masses sum exactly to the current prefix
mass because they partition its support. Normalize them using exact rational
division. Width never increases because only local factors are restricted.

Choose D=2^B >=2n/epsilon. Approximate the two internal cumulative probability
boundaries by their floors on the D-grid, keep endpoints 0,D, draw B unbiased
bits, and use that dyadic interval. Zero branches have exactly coincident
boundaries and remain zero. For a three-way conditional law each cumulative
rounding error is <1/D; its TV change is at most 2/D. The standard sequential
coupling/telescoping bound gives total TV <=2n/D<=epsilon. This uses a fixed
nB random bits and at most 3n+1 exact counter calls. There is no rejection
onto a rare canonical sector, no inverse c_k, no exponentially large prefix
table, no support oracle, and no approximate nonzero-norm oracle.

The supplied implementation includes this full sampler. Its measured sample
trace is a finite execution, not empirical certification of a mixing theorem
or the written uniform TV bound. No Markov chain is used.

6. F-ONLY TREEWIDTH ACQUISITION AND BASELINE INCREMENT

Korhonen (FOCS 2021), Theorem 1, gives a graph algorithm which in 2^{O(w)}n
time outputs a decomposition of width <=2w+1 or determines tw(G)>w.
Primary exact theorem and introduction were checked at
https://arxiv.org/html/2104.07463v1; the published version is
https://ieee-focs.org/FOCS-2021-Papers/pdfs/FOCS2021-5stbVHiOp5jRHWlSl41FkR/205500a184/205500a184.pdf.
Its internal graph algorithm was not reconstructed or executed here.
Trying increasing w up to a promised logarithmic bound acquires a legal
decomposition in polynomial time; an acquired width certificate can be
verified independently. A supplied decomposition is thus not a hidden
scientific oracle in the theoretical F-only subclass. In the executable,
only the acquired greedy-order implementation is available.

The baseline closes arbitrary bipartite pair support conditionally on
Chen--Liu/N63, weighted permutations exactly, fixed rank/pair count by
enumeration, and logarithmic COMPONENT SIZE by per-component enumeration.
The width result permits one connected component of unbounded size and
unbounded rank with odd cycles and more than one nonzero entry per row.
For example, a chain of triangles has treewidth two, and a chain of growing
logarithmic cliques has logarithmic width. It is not covered by the stated
component-size or monomial condition, nor by a bipartite support promise.
It does not improve the unrestricted worst-case counting problem.

General bounded-width tensor/Grassmann contraction is prior methodology;
this explicit four-generator norm, signs, prefix, bit and rarity accounting
are a targeted reconstruction. Novelty relative to the entire literature
has not been established. No first-efficient-projected-BCS claim is made.

8. POSTSELECTED UNIVERSALITY: CURRENT SCREEN AND NEXT DEEP ATTACK

The primary DiVincenzo--Terhal paper distinguishes non-destructive charge
measurements and parity measurements from Gaussian single-mode operations:
https://arxiv.org/html/quant-ph/0403031v1, Sections 2,4,5.
Its grouped no-double measurement has one outcome P_0+P_1 and another P_2;
its universality discussion concerns circuits with interleaved measurements,
feedforward or postselected branches. Such an import is not already a
static single-F hard-projected BCS norm reduction.

The needed preservation gate is concrete: compile an arbitrary postselected
branch into an explicit rational opposite-spin pair matrix F' and a requested
sector/prefix, with only the final onsite projectors, preserving its positive
branch probability or conditional output law to multiplicative accuracy and
polynomial bit length. Extra coherent multi-mode bras, intermediate Kraus
operators, general Pfaffian spin mixing, magic input states, or arbitrary
Gaussian measurements must not be silently declared supplied by F'.

Naively moving all hard projectors to the end is false. Let a Gaussian mode
swap exchange u_i and u_j, and let the input be u_i^dagger d_j^dagger|0>.
The initial configuration is hard-allowed. Swapping creates double occupancy
at j, so applying the final hard projector gives zero. Applying that hard
projector before swapping leaves the initial state unchanged and then gives
a nonzero double-occupied state. Thus P G != G P exactly, even for a rational
mode permutation. This refutes the simplest reordering, not every enlarged
static gadget construction or hardness reduction.

Next decisive gate: construct a rational local teleportation/static Gaussian
gadget for the interleaved no-double branch whose remaining physical modes
retain the exact opposite-spin pair interface, or prove that the candidate
gadget imports a forbidden coherent measurement. I will continue this attack
independently after freezing this checkpoint. No approximation-hardness claim
has been earned here.

C03_S02 INDEPENDENT INITIAL — FULL-SUPPORT HARD-PARITY LOCAL-WALK BARRIER
