c05_s03 INITIAL — FINITE CP-RANGE LIFT WITH EXPLICIT COMPLEXITY DEPENDENCE
7 October 2026. Independent Sol cohort; requested gpt-6.1-sol/max;
observed model UNKNOWN. No cycle6 peer output read; no peer messages.

STRONGEST CLAIM / STATUS

Self-contained derived construction, pending independent audit: an arbitrary
finite-dimensional bistochastic CP map T whose range unit ball admits a
bounded-coefficient k-generator description has an actual finite EB lift,
with an explicit normalized infinity-to-one modulus independent of Hilbert
dimension and trace-block weights. An acquired finite algorithm is given;
its worst-case enumeration cost is exponential, not claimed efficient.

The generator/cover complexity dependence is essential to this proof. This
does NOT prove a dimension-free modulus for arbitrary growing CP ranges,
the unrestricted beta_m->0 theorem, or bounded-Holevo broadcasting=>EB.
The exponent 3^{-(k-1)} is already present in current almost-commuting
literature. Novelty of the lift/cost formulation is unverified.

1. EXACT INTERFACE AND MODULUS

Let A be any finite-dimensional C*-algebra with faithful normalized TRACE
tau. Let T:A->A be UCP and tau-preserving; T need not be selfadjoint,
positive as a Hilbert-space map, or compatible. All norms below are
normalized tracial norms; the input norm is ordinary operator norm.

Supply Hermitian contractions B_1,...,B_k and a verified approximation
description: for each Hermitian contraction A, there are real c_i and a
scalar a such that

  ||T(A)-a I-sum_i c_i B_i||_2 <= epsilon,
  sum_i |c_i| <= K.                                             (1)

Supply/check ||[B_i,B_j]||_2<=delta for every i,j. The c_i may depend on
A; a common CP extension of these coefficients is NOT assumed. Instead,
the original map T is preserved and only its OUTPUT is pinched.

Define F_1(delta)=0, F_k(0)=0, and for k>=2, delta>0,

  t=((k-1)delta)^(1/3),
  F_k(delta)=min{1, sqrt[t^2+F_(k-1)(min{2,delta+4t})^2]}.          (2)

There is a maximal abelian subalgebra D within A, with trace-preserving
conditional expectation P_D, such that

  max_i ||B_i-P_D(B_i)||_2 <= F_k(delta).                         (3)

Consequently E=P_D T is UCP, tau-preserving, and EB, with

  sup_(A=A*, ||A||<=1) ||E(A)-T(A)||_1
        <= epsilon+K F_k(delta).                               (4)

The epsilon coefficient is ONE: I-P_D is an L2 orthogonal projection,
so ||(I-P_D)R||_2<=||R||_2 for the approximation remainder in (1).
For fixed k, F_k(delta)->0, independent of matrix dimension, block weights,
number of inputs, input labels, spectral gaps, or smallest eigenvalue.

There are explicit constants C_k with F_k(delta)<=C_k delta^alpha_k,
alpha_k=3^{-(k-1)}. One permissible recurrence is C_1=0 and

 C_k^2=(k-1)^(2/3)
       + C_(k-1)^2 [1+4(k-1)^(1/3)]^(2/3^(k-2)).                (5)

For delta<=1, use delta+4t <= [1+4(k-1)^(1/3)]delta^(1/3).
For delta>=1, F_k<=1<=C_k delta^alpha_k (k>=2). Formula (5) and induction
prove the claim. The recurrence, rather than an optimized constant, is
the reproducible modulus.

2. ONE-STEP PINCHING: FULL DERIVATION

Work first in a full matrix block, or in a direct sum of already selected
blocks. Diagonalize a Hermitian contraction X within those blocks, with
eigenvalues x_a. Choose width h>0 and a shift u uniform in [0,h). Partition
the spectrum into half-open intervals [u+zh,u+(z+1)h), keeping the existing
block labels. Let P_u be the associated block pinching.

Two eigenvalues a,b land in different intervals with probability

  Pr_u(separated)=min{1,|x_a-x_b|/h} <= |x_a-x_b|/h.             (6)

For any Hermitian contraction Y, writing its entries in this eigenbasis
and including the central trace weights w_a constant in each block,

 E_u ||Y-P_u(Y)||_2^2
  = sum_(a,b) w_a |Y_ab|^2 Pr_u(separated)
  <= h^(-1) sum_(a,b) w_a |x_a-x_b| |Y_ab|^2
  <= h^(-1) ||[X,Y]||_2 ||Y||_2
  <= delta/h.                                                 (7)

The second inequality is scalar Cauchy-Schwarz with factors
sqrt(w_a)|x_a-x_b||Y_ab| and sqrt(w_a)|Y_ab|. This derivation uses
normalization tau(I)=1 and contraction Y, not the number of entries.

For Y_1,...,Y_(k-1), sum (7). Some single shift u has

  sum_j ||Y_j-P_u(Y_j)||_2^2 <= (k-1)delta/h.                   (8)

Replace X on each new block by that interval's midpoint, clipped to
[-1,1], obtaining X_0. It is central in the new block algebra, a Hermitian
contraction, commutes with X, and ||X-X_0||_infty<=h/2. Let
Y'_j=P_u(Y_j). Every Y'_j remains a Hermitian contraction because pinching
is UCP. Choose h=((k-1)delta)^(1/3)=t. Then each ||Y_j-Y'_j||_2<=t.

For any j,l,

  ||[Y'_j,Y'_l]||_2
    <= ||[Y_j,Y_l]||_2
       +2||Y'_j-Y_j||_2+2||Y'_l-Y_l||_2
    <= delta+4t.                                              (9)

The universal contraction bound also gives <=2. All statements hold with
arbitrary faithful central block weights. A SINGLE global shift can be
chosen by minimizing the total weighted loss; no division by a small
block weight is needed.

3. RECURSION AND EB LIFT: FULL DERIVATION

Induct inside the new finite block algebra on the k-1 compressed Y'_j,
using its restricted faithful trace. This yields a MASA and expectation
P' there, with maximum displacement <=F_(k-1)(min{2,delta+4t}). Extend its
diagonal expectation to all of A. The final P_D refines P_u.

Because X_0 is block-central, P_D(X_0)=X_0 and

  ||X-P_D(X)||_2<=||X-X_0||_2<=h/2.                            (10)

For every Y_j, the two terms in

  Y_j-P_D(Y_j)=(Y_j-P_u(Y_j))+(Y'_j-P'(Y'_j))

are orthogonal in L2(tau): the first is off the new blocks, the second
lies inside them. Thus

  ||Y_j-P_D(Y_j)||_2^2
     <=t^2+F_(k-1)(min{2,delta+4t})^2.                         (11)

Every conditional expectation is an L2 orthogonal projection. Therefore
||B_i-P_D(B_i)||_2<=||B_i||_2<=1, giving the minimum in (2). For k=1,
diagonalize B_1 exactly. For delta=0, simultaneously diagonalize the
commuting family exactly. This completes the induction for (3).

Let Q_z be the minimal projections of D and p_z=tau(Q_z)>0. Then

  P_D(X)=sum_z tau(Q_z X) Q_z/p_z,
  E(A)=sum_z tau(M_z A) f_z,
  M_z=T_dagger(Q_z)>=0, sum_z M_z=I, f_z=Q_z/p_z>=0,
  tau(f_z)=1.                                                 (12)

Here dagger is the tracial adjoint. CP of T implies positivity of its
weighted adjoint; tau-preservation makes T_dagger unital. Formula (12)
is an explicit finite measurement/prepare channel, not a merely positive
approximation to an operator system. There are at most sum_b d_b outcomes
for A=directsum_b M_(d_b). The actual physical form for a central faithful
reference sigma is rho ->sum_z Tr(M_z rho) sigma f_z. Input pinching into
A extends this to an ordinary full-domain EB channel on ambient matrices.

P_D fixes scalars. Apply I-P_D to (1), use (3), triangle inequality,
orthogonal-projection contraction for the remainder, and ||.||_1<=||.||_2.
This proves (4). No trace-norm/measurement maximization is moved through
an average; ONE final P_D works on every bounded input.

4. TWO USEFUL COMPLEXITY COROLLARIES

(a) If the real Hermitian range of T has rank r, compactness gives a
maximum-volume basis B_1,...,B_r selected from T's image unit ball.
Relative to ANY coordinate projection that is injective on that range,
replace one basis column by X=T(A). Its determinant is c_i times the
maximal determinant, so |c_i|<=1. Therefore (1) holds exactly with K=r,
epsilon=0. If the ENTIRE range's commutator supremum is <=delta, (4) gives

      inf_EB ||E-T||_(infty->1, Hermitian) <= r F_r(delta).      (13)

This is uniform for bounded r; r may not be silently bounded as dimension
grows. An acquired finite rational-net version below has K<=2r.

(b) Supply an L2 epsilon-net {B_i}_{i=1}^N of T's entire Hermitian unit
ball image, with every B_i itself such an image. Use K=1 in (1), with one
nonzero coefficient. Then

      inf_EB ||E-T||_(infty->1) <= epsilon+F_N(delta).           (14)

Thus a supplied/acquired covering profile N(epsilon) yields an explicit
modulus, even when literal range rank grows. An empirical net without a
uniform covering certificate does not meet this premise.

Apply to T=Phi^m in the campaign's positive selfadjoint selfcompatible
class. The baseline proves delta_m<=2/sqrt(m+1)+4/(m+1). If range rank
r is bounded, (13) proves beta_m->0 for that subclass. If r or N grows,
one needs r F_r(delta_m)->0 or epsilon_m+F_(N_m)(delta_m)->0 respectively.
The exponent makes the resulting allowed growth very restrictive. At
delta=10^-12, the direct recurrence already gives F_5=1. This proof does
not establish the arbitrary growing-range theorem.

5. ACQUISITION AND COSTS — NO FREE FRAME/NET/EIGENBASIS

Finite rational implementation interface: supply or acquire the full
rational superoperator of T:M_a->M_b with input/output bit precision L;
check its Choi PSD, unital and trace-preserving equations. An EXACT full
matrix-output oracle needs a^2 public Hermitian basis queries to acquire
it. Finite-precision noisy queries do not give the exact interface without
an additional verified uncertainty envelope.

Select r independent coordinate rows and columns by rational Gaussian
elimination. Public Hermitian basis inputs e_ii, e_ij+e_ji,
i(e_ji-e_ij) are contractions. Let mu>0 be the absolute determinant of
their selected r-coordinate output matrix. This is a computed positive
rational lower bound on the optimum basis determinant.

Choose rational zeta<=min{1,mu/[4 r^(1+r/2)]}; replacing the radical by
a larger integer in this denominator is harmless. Enumerate Hermitian
input matrices G with each real coordinate on a mesh xi=zeta/(4a) in
[-1,1]. Retain those with -(1+zeta/2)I<=G<=(1+zeta/2)I, checked exactly
by rational PSD calculations, and use G/(1+zeta/2). This is an operator-
norm zeta-net of the input unit ball: entry rounding costs <=zeta/2 in
operator norm, and scaling costs <=zeta/2. It has at most

      M <= (1+8a/zeta)^(a^2)                                 (15)

points. UCP contraction makes its output a coordinatewise zeta-net.
Enumerate every r-tuple and choose maximum determinant D. Multilinearity
and Hadamard give total determinant approximation error <=
r^(1+r/2)zeta<=mu/4, so D>=3mu/4. For ANY output X and any basis-column
replacement, its nearest net replacement has determinant <=D, and the
one-column perturbation costs <=r^(r/2)zeta<=mu/(4r). Thus

      |c_i|<=1+1/(3r), sum_i |c_i|<=r+1/3<=2r.                (16)

This acquires a frame with K<=2r. Naive cost: M image evaluations and
M^r exact r-by-r determinants; every matrix entry, denominator and
determinant is charged. Reading a full explicit T costs O(a^2 b^2)
coefficients. log(1/zeta) is controlled by the computed mu and polynomial
determinant bit height, but M is exponential in a^2 and can be enormous.
No polynomial-time frame search is claimed or executed.

For a SUPPLIED frame, the spectral construction uses <=k-1 pinching
stages. At one stage its shifted partition changes only at eigenvalue
residues modulo h, at most sum_b d_b breakpoints. Evaluate the loss on
each interval and choose a minimizing shift. Dense real-arithmetic cost
is bounded by O(k^2 D^3), D=sum_b d_b; storage O(k D^2), plus the map.
This real-arithmetic statement does not assert polynomial bit complexity
of iterative exact algebraic diagonalization.

A gap-independent finite-bit EXISTENCE/search fallback avoids silently
charging inverse eigenvalue gaps: enumerate complex entry-grid matrices
Q approximating unitaries, discard ill-conditioned Q, and use the exact
polar unitary V=Q(Q*Q)^(-1/2). If ||Q-U||<=eta/4 then ||V-U||<=eta/2.
The corresponding diagonal expectations satisfy

      ||P_V(X)-P_U(X)||_2<=4||V-U|| ||X||_2.                   (17)

This follows by telescoping the two unitary conjugations around ordinary
diagonal pinching. A mesh eta/(8D) gives a search space at most
(1+16D/eta)^(2D^2). Exact algebraic calculations/interval certification
select a V passing frame-error threshold F_k(delta)+8eta. Formula (12)
then supplies actual positive effects and preparations. Its additional
uniform error is <=8K eta. This costs finite algebraic root isolation
and exponential search, not inverse unspecified spectral gaps. No full
certified algebraic engine or hardware compiler was implemented here.

The EB descriptor has <=D outcomes, D preparations, and D effect matrices;
in full M_d this is O(d^3) stored coefficients if effects are expanded,
and Kraus rank <=d^2. Keeping T and the diagonalizing unitary as a factored
descriptor reduces storage, but operational POVM/preparation/precision
costs remain charged. No dimension-independent apparatus size or energy
bound is claimed. Backend energy, token usage and hardware runtime are
UNKNOWN. Local diagnostics were bounded CPU work on the shared M4.

STRONGEST CLAIM AND STATUS

A complete conditional extension of the existing spin-half XXZ transfer to
heterogeneous HIGHER SPINS is derived below. For q_v=2S_v positive integers,
let S_v^a denote the standard spin-S_v operators and take the physical energy
Hamiltonian to be -H, where

 H = sum_(uv) [alpha_uv(S_u^x S_v^x+S_u^y S_v^y)
                         +gamma_uv S_u^z S_v^z]
                   +sum_v [b_v S_v^x+c_v S_v^z],
 alpha_uv >= |gamma_uv|, b_v>=0, c_v arbitrary rational.

The graph is any explicitly supplied loopless graph, including nonbipartite
graphs. Couplings, beta>=0 and accuracy parameters are rational. Define
 Q=sum_v q_v and C=3 sum_(uv)alpha_uv S_u S_v
                         +sum_v (b_v+|c_v|)S_v.

Conditional on Chen--Liu 2610.06724v1, HOMOGENEOUS Theorem5.1/RemarkA.4:
 - Tr_(tensor spin spaces) exp(beta H) has a relative randomized scheme with
   bit cost polynomial in input bits, Q, numerical beta C and inverse error;
 - lambda_max(H) has a relative randomized scheme polynomial in input bits,
   Q and inverse error, because C/5<=lambda_max(H)<=C when C>0;
 - the joint S^z measurement law has an acquired classical sampling reduction
   with the same numerical-temperature dependence and no rare-sector cost.

For fixed spins, or spins supplied in unary / with polynomially bounded Q,
the eigenvalue result is a conventional bit-FPRAS. Binary descriptions of
exponentially large spin do NOT get a polynomial-in-log(S) guarantee.

Origin: independent c06_s01 construction. New relative to the supplied
spin-half baseline: an explicit homogeneous Lorentzian spin-projector gate,
and a compressed Euler product with that projector inserted EVERY layer.
The mathematical reduction is complete internally but unreviewed. The fresh
Chen--Liu algorithm remains an imported preprint dependency. Its full counting
routine is NOT implemented or executed here. An actual rational coefficient-
oracle compiler and small exact contraction tests are implemented below.
Historical priority and external correctness remain UNKNOWN; no field-breaking
advance is asserted. The spin representation / polarization mechanism itself
has established prior art and is not claimed as new representation theory.

1. EXPLICIT RATIONAL PROJECTOR AND ITS LOG-CONCAVITY

Realize spin S=q/2 as Sym^q(C^2). In the q-qubit computational basis, its
orthogonal projector Pi_q has entries

 (Pi_q)_(r,c) = 1/binom(q,|r|) if |r|=|c|, and 0 otherwise.

Indeed, its charge-r block is the all-one matrix divided by binom(q,r),
the rank-one projector onto the normalized Dicke vector. These blocks are
orthogonal and exhaust the q+1-dimensional symmetric subspace. All entries
are nonnegative rationals. No normalized Dicke square roots are input data.

Keep row bits and complement column bits, exactly as in the baseline transfer.
With q row variables x and q complemented-column variables y, the local
projector polynomial is

 p_q(x,y)=sum_(r=0)^q e_r(x)e_(q-r)(y)/binom(q,r).

It is homogeneous, multiaffine, degree q, with EVERY q-subset of its 2q
variables in support. Its positive coefficient range is at most 2^q.
The q=1 case is linear. Here is an exact signature proof for every q>=2.

Take a nonzero derivative of order q-2, using a row and b column variables,
a+b=q-2. Let m=q-a=b+2, ell=q-b=a+2 be the remaining row/column counts and

 A=1/binom(q,a+2), B=1/binom(q,a+1), D=1/binom(q,a).

The resulting quadratic Hessian has row-row off-diagonals A, column-column
off-diagonals D, cross entries B, and zero diagonals. On row-sum-zero vectors
it has eigenvalue -A; on column-sum-zero vectors it has eigenvalue -D.
The remaining two-dimensional block is

 [[A(m-1), B sqrt(m ell)], [B sqrt(m ell), D(ell-1)]].

Its determinant is ZERO, since

 AD/B^2 = binom(q,a+1)^2/[binom(q,a+2)binom(q,a)]
        = m ell/[(m-1)(ell-1)].

Its trace is positive. Thus every such derivative has exactly one positive
eigenvalue, one zero and all other eigenvalues negative. Repeated-variable
derivatives vanish by multiaffinity. Full uniform-matroid support is M-convex.
The established Branden--Huh characterization (Theorem2.25, explanatory
paragraph following it; Theorem2.30) therefore makes p_q Lorentzian and
completely log-concave. This is a proof for every q, not an extrapolation
from the executable q<=12 arithmetic checks.

Equivalently p_q is the separate x/y polarization of (X+Y)^q; this observation
explains why this projector has a legal gate polynomial. The displayed
signature calculation is the certificate actually used here.
Primary source: https://arxiv.org/html/1902.03719v8 and
https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2020/192-3/p04 (published established theorem).

2. HIGHER-SPIN HAMILTONIAN AS AN EXPLICIT QUBIT OPERATOR

Give site v its q_v constituents and define J_v^a=(1/2)sum_i sigma_(v,i)^a.
Restricted to Sym^(q_v)(C^2), these are S_v^a. The embedded Hhat uses pair
couplings alpha_uv/4, gamma_uv/4 between every constituent of u and v, and
one-qubit fields b_v/2,c_v/2 on every constituent of v. The embedded total
Hamiltonian is invariant under every site permutation. Consequently it
commutes with Pi=product_v Pi_(q_v), and its restriction is EXACTLY H.

For each embedded pair, use the baseline PSD shift
 A_e=3(alpha_uv/4)I+(alpha_uv/4)(XX+YY)+(gamma_uv/4)ZZ.
For each embedded field, use
 A_i=((b_v+|c_v|)/2)I+(b_v/2)X+(c_v/2)Z.
These are PSD, their sum is Hhat+CI, and sum_i ||A_i|| <=D:=2C.
This C equals the physical expression above because q_v=2S_v.
Terms with zero coefficients may be omitted. If C=0, H=0 and return the
known dimension product_v(q_v+1), zero eigenvalue and uniform spin law.

3. COMPRESSION AT EVERY LAYER: RELATIVE TRACE PROOF

For C>0, 0<eps<=1, let tau=beta D, take

 m=ceil max{1,4tau,20tau^2/eps}, delta=tau/m, s=beta/(2m),
 W=product_i(I+s A_i), B0=WW*, B=Pi B0 Pi restricted to ran(Pi).

Every factor I+sA_i>=I, so the least singular value of W is >=1 and
B0>=I. Therefore B>=I_(physical space), including when individual gates
do not preserve the symmetric subspace. The expansion of B0 gives

 ||B0-I-delta(Hhat+CI)/D|| <=exp(delta)-1-delta<=delta^2,
 ||B0-I|| <=exp(delta)-1<=2delta,

because the sum of norms of all increments in the ordered product is <=delta
and delta<=1/4. Compressing is norm-contracting and Pi commutes with Hhat, so

 ||B-Iphys-delta(H+CIphys)/D||<=delta^2,
 ||B-Iphys||<=2delta.

For E=B-Iphys>=0, functional calculus gives
 ||log(I+E)-E||<=||E||^2/2<=2delta^2.
Thus ||m log B-beta(H+CIphys)||<=3tau^2/m<=eps/4.
Hermitian min--max bounds each ordered physical eigenvalue by this error.
Exponentiating the SCALAR eigenvalues and summing yields

 e^(-eps/4) Trphys e^(beta(H+CI)) <=Trphys B^m
                                  <=e^(eps/4) Trphys e^(beta(H+CI)).

Count Trphys B^m at relative eps/8, compute e^(-beta C) at relative eps/8
using finite-bit scalar arithmetic, and multiply; for eps<=1 the total relative
error is at most eps. No operator monotonicity of exp is used. No additive
trace error is divided by a tiny symmetric-sector probability.

The full-qubit trace of (Pi B0 Pi)^m equals Trphys B^m. Cyclicity and Pi^2=Pi
allow its expansion as a circuit containing m copies of Pi, with all local
projectors Pi_(q_v) at each copy, and 2m copies of every retained Euler term.
This trace is nonnegative rational and has an explicit local tensor network.

4. HOMOGENEOUS COUNTING INTERFACE IS FULLY ACQUIRED

Every pair Euler polynomial is the baseline homogeneous quadratic
 a(x1x2+x3x4)+b(x1x4+x2x3)+c(x1x3+x2x4),
 a=1+s(3alpha'+gamma'), b=1+s(3alpha'-gamma'), c=2s alpha'.
Its signature / triangle criterion holds because alpha'>=|gamma'|.
Every field gate [[v,d],[d,u]] has d^2<=uv and uses the homogeneous lift
 d z1z2+(u/2)x(z1+z2)+(v/2)y(z1+z2)+dxy.
All projectors use p_q proved above. Distinct gate variables keep their product
P multiaffine, homogeneous and log-concave.

If there are e pair gates, f field gates and projector arities q_j, the number
of physical wires is Wphys=2e+f+sum_j q_j. There are 2f dummy variables and
N=4e+4f+2sum_j q_j total variables. P has degree N/2. Set

 Qpartner=product_(physical wire h,h')(x_h+x_h')
                                      * e_f(all 2f dummy variables).

Qpartner is homogeneous LC of degree N/2 and its positive coefficients are
one. Exact-once constraints give P exactly Wphys selected physical incidences.
Homogeneity then gives P exactly f selected dummy incidences; the partner
supplies their complementary size-f set with coefficient one. Summing the
dummy choices recovers each field matrix entry. Thus

 [x^all] P Qpartner =Trphys B^m

(times an explicitly handled factor 2 for any unused constituent qubit).
No spin basis square roots appear in P,Qpartner.

Both coefficient oracles are explicit programs. P queries each local table
from the requested subset and multiplies; a projector query is one binomial
coefficient after a charge check, not an exponentially expanded polynomial.
Qpartner tests every physical wire and total dummy cardinality. Positive seeds
are acquired locally from zero-to-zero diagonal entries and legal dummy choices;
Qpartner selects one incidence per wire and any f dummies. In fact the all-zero
physical trace configuration also certifies positive intersection for this
thermal circuit. Projector coefficients cost O(q) bits and their log range is
at most q. All other local entries have polynomial rational bit lengths.
Summing local log-range upper bounds gives an explicit polynomial bound for P.
Conditioning restricts that range rather than requiring an unknown minimum.

Let p be the number of embedded retained terms. Then
 e<=2m sum_edges q_u q_v, f<=2m sum_sites q_v,
 sum_j q_j<=m Q, so N<=8mp+2mQ.
The imported homogeneous arithmetic/oracle bound is
 O(N^10 Bbit^2 eta^-2 log^2(2Bbit/eta)),
 Bbit=N+ln(R_P), with Qpartner range one. Oracle, rational-arithmetic, bounded-
coin and amplification costs are additional polynomial factors, not free.
This has very large exponents. Its dependence on beta C is numerical.

Acquired executable coefficient compiler:
 work/cycle6/c06_s01/spin_projection_transfer.py
It provides local projector/field/edge gates, variable orientation/wiring,
P and Qpartner coefficient queries, positive seeds and log-range bit bounds.
The executable exact enumerator is bounded to small diagnostics and is
EXPONENTIAL outside that bound; it is not the FPRAS.

5. EIGENVALUE AND SPIN-Z SAMPLING CONSEQUENCES

Put A=sum_edges alpha_uv S_u S_v, Bf=sum_v b_v S_v, T=sum_v |c_v|S_v.
The product of maximal S^x coherent states has energy A+Bf. The product of
maximal signed S^z states matching c_v has energy at least T-A. Therefore

 lambda_max(H)>=max(A+Bf,T-A)
             >=(4/5)(A+Bf)+(1/5)(T-A)
             =(3A+4Bf+T)/5>=C/5.

The norm triangle bound gives lambda_max(H)<=C. The physical log dimension is
sum_v ln(q_v+1)<=Q ln2. Choosing beta=20(Q+1)/(eps C) and a constant-relative-
error count gives a relative-eps largest-eigenvalue estimate with beta D
polynomial in Q,eps^-1. Coupling magnitudes can be exponentially large binary
rationals; inverse-C normalization removes numerical magnitude cost for this
specific eigenvalue objective. Arbitrary scalar energy offsets require separate
tracking and would invalidate this relative lower bound.

The two compressed Hermitian generators also give the baseline Gibbs trace-
distance bound t<=tanh(kappa/2), kappa=||m log B-beta(H+CI)||. Diagonal prefix
counts on the projected circuit remain homogeneous LC after pinnings. Relative
child estimates, exact-zero outputs and bounded rational coins yield classical
computational-qubit measurement samples without inverse prefix probabilities.
Aggregating each site's constituent Hamming weight is exactly the S^z
measurement law: its charge-r symmetric subspace is the one-dimensional Dicke
line. This is classical measurement sampling, not a quantum Gibbs-preparation
algorithm. The same fresh Chen--Liu counting dependency remains.

6. TWO IMPORTANT FAILED SHORTCUTS

6a. One projection only cannot inherit the unweighted relative trace proof.
For K=diag(-M,M), Pi=|0><0|, L=K+kappa X, ||L-K||=kappa. With
R=sqrt(M^2+kappa^2),

 Tr Pi e^L =cosh R-(M/R)sinh R,
 Tr Pi e^K =e^-M.

For fixed kappa>0 and M->infinity their ratio grows asymptotically like
kappa^2 e^(2M)/(4M^2), although K commutes with Pi. Small off-diagonal leakage
can reach an exponentially heavier excluded sector. Thus a naive final
projection does NOT follow from the baseline Hermitian generator norm bound.
The layerwise compression in section3 solves this exact problem.

6b. The projector is a legal inserted gate but I+sPi_4 is NOT a legal
infinitesimal Hamiltonian gate. In row variables x0..x3 / column y0..y3,
q_I=product_i(x_i+y_i), q= q_I+s p_4. After differentiating x0,x1, let
p=x2+x3+y2+y3 and w=y0+y1 denote the coordinate vectors with these entries
one. The Hessian quadratic restriction on span{p,w} is

 [[8+13s/3,5s/3],[5s/3,s/3]],
 determinant=(4s/3)(2-s)>0 for 0<s<2.

Both diagonal entries are positive, so this derivative has at least TWO
positive eigenvalues. A multiaffine LC polynomial's derivatives are LC by
the standard positive-coordinate limit. The quadratic signature criterion
therefore refutes LC of q_I+s p_4 in this range. This excludes a claimed
arbitrary high-order symmetric-projector Hamiltonian extension through the
same infinitesimal gates; it does not prove computational hardness.

c06_s02 8cda53ae87abcf745e9cf636f3303b9facf5eca228855e1c362443309dac2f4d

C06_S02 INDEPENDENT INITIAL: XXZ OBSERVABLE/SUPPORT/BIT AUDIT
7 October 2026. Frozen before peer exposure. Origin: c06_s02, requested
gpt-6.1-sol/max; actual backend model metadata UNKNOWN.

STRONGEST CLAIM AND STATUS

Conditional on Chen--Liu arXiv:2610.06724v1 Theorem 5.1 and its every-execution
rational-bit implementation (Remark A.4), the baseline easy-plane XXZ Gibbs
pipeline admits additive-epsilon estimation of ANY supplied Hermitian
observable O of norm at most one whose computational-basis rows can be listed
in polynomial time and have at most polynomially many nonzero entries.
The observable may act on all n qubits; all n-site Pauli strings are included.
Runtime is polynomial in the binary Hamiltonian/observable description,
numerical beta*C, row sparsity r, epsilon^{-1}, and confidence amplification.
No inverse diagonal probability, matrix-entry size, spectral gap, or original
thermal weight occurs. The exact estimator requires O(epsilon^{-2}) samples;
the finite-bit clipped implementation below retains that sample order, while
the costs of sampling and each entry FPRAS are separately charged.

This is a new extension relative to the supplied baseline's log-size reduced
matrix reconstruction interface, not a claimed novel Monte Carlo mechanism
or field-breaking result. The underlying variance idea is close to familiar
local-estimator methods. The full FPRAS is imported and unimplemented.
The extension is internally derived; external correctness/priority UNKNOWN.

The directly inserted X-boundary route is REFUTED: it creates 1+xy, which
is not log-concave. The importance estimator supplies a different legal route.
No fatal error has been found in the baseline's precise claimed XXZ cone.

BASELINE READ AND EXACT INPUT

Read COMMON/own assignment; release arrived before scientific reads. Read
00_START_HERE, 01_CORE, NEW_FRONTIER, ACTIVE_CONTEXT, NEXT_ROUND protocol,
cycle3/xxz_thermal_audit, cycle2/tensor_contraction_audit, the field addendum,
cycle2/counting_gate_audit and primary Chen--Liu theorem/bit excerpts.
Pinned card N63/AGENTS were retrieved using git show at
37a01798e92a0763e64a4649ae00024079d2a266; checkout was not mutated.
No cycle6 peer results were read or received.

Use the baseline Hamiltonian
 H=sum_uv[alpha_uv(XX+YY)+gamma_uv ZZ]+sum_v[b_v X+c_v Z],
 alpha_uv>=|gamma_uv|, b_v>=0, c_v arbitrary, rational beta>0.
Physical convention is -H. C=3 sum alpha+sum(b+|c|), tau=2 beta C.
C=0 is trivial. The audited rational symmetric Euler circuit K=B^m, B=W W*,
has entrywise nonnegative coefficients, K positive definite, and
 ||log K-beta(H+CI)||<=5 tau^2/m.
Choose m=ceil max(1,4tau,100 tau^2/epsilon). Its normalized state
 sigma=K/Tr K differs from rho=exp(beta H)/Tr exp(beta H) in trace norm
 at most 2 tanh(||log K-beta(H+CI)||/2)<=epsilon/20.
For ||O||<=1 this already spends at most epsilon/20 expectation error.

1. EVERY FIXED GLOBAL ENTRY HAS A LEGAL COUNT

For a circuit with e edge gates and f field gates, baseline homogeneous P has
degree 2e+2f on N=4(e+f) distinct coordinates. Physical wires number W=2e+f;
there are 2f dummy coordinates. Q is the product of physical wire factors
(x_h+x_h') times the dummy elementary symmetric polynomial e_f.

Open the cyclic boundary on every active qubit. For fixed computational
strings a,b, pin the first row legs to a and the complemented last column
legs to 1-b. Put Delta=|a|-|b| on active qubits. Internal physical wires still
force exactly one selection. Selected boundary physical coordinates number
n_active+Delta, so P's total selected physical count is W+Delta. Hence P
selects f-Delta dummies and Q must use e_(f+Delta), not e_f.

After pinning/removing the 2 n_active boundary coordinates, the degrees are
 deg P'=N/2-n_active-Delta,
 deg Q'=N/2-n_active+Delta;
their sum is the remaining ground-set size. When the dummy rank lies outside
[0,2f] the entry is exactly zero. Otherwise the dummy uniform-matroid factor
is still homogeneous LC. Derivative/zero-variable pinnings preserve nonzero
multiaffine LC factors. P' is still a product of local tables with disjoint
coordinates; positive support seeds are acquired by inspecting each constant
size pinned table separately, rejecting a zero table. Q' support is explicit.
Its positive coefficients are one. Range bounds restrict the original rational
local products and thus have polynomial logarithmic length. An inactive qubit
contributes delta_(a_v,b_v), handled directly. If no variables remain, evaluate
the constant directly instead of applying the n>=1 theorem.

Consequently an unnormalized full entry K_(a,b) has a relative-error count
whose every-tape bit cost is polynomial in circuit size and accuracy. This
does not require constructing the exponentially many-entry matrix K.
K_(b,b)>=1 because B>=I and powers preserve that spectral bound. The true
denominator is therefore positive, though its normalized probability can
be exponentially small. Positive counts can output zero on failed tapes;
the implementation below explicitly handles that case. Zero numerators are
zero on every tape by the imported support-preservation contract.

2. EXACT VARIANCE LEMMA (COMPLETE PROOF)

Let sigma be ANY PSD trace-one matrix. Sample b with p_b=sigma_(b,b), and
for p_b>0 put g_b=(O sigma)_(b,b)/p_b. A zero diagonal has zero entire row
and column by PSD Cauchy--Schwarz, so define g_b=0 there. Then
 E g_b=sum_b (O sigma)_(b,b)=Tr(O sigma).
Using Cauchy--Schwarz on sigma^(1/2) O* e_b and sigma^(1/2) e_b,
 |(O sigma)_(b,b)|^2 <= (O sigma O*)_(b,b) sigma_(b,b).
Divide by p_b and sum to obtain
 E |g_b|^2 <= Tr(O sigma O*) <= ||O||^2.
This proof has no dimension or minimum-positive-diagonal factor.

In our real symmetric nonnegative sigma, replace Hermitian O by its entrywise
real part A=(O+conj O)/2. It is real symmetric, ||A||<=||O||<=1, keeps row
sparsity at most r, and Tr(O sigma)=Tr(A sigma). Then
 g_b=sum_a A_(b,a) K_(a,b)/K_(b,b)
is a real number computable using at most r+1 entry counts. Supplied row-list
algorithms and coefficient precision are part of the input and runtime.
All-n Pauli strings have r=1. Odd-Y strings have exactly zero expectation
for this real sigma/rho and need no stochastic estimate.

Let D be the ENTRYWISE absolute value of A and define
 u_b=(D sigma)_(b,b)/p_b>=0.
Every entry of A has modulus<=1. Hermiticity and r-row sparsity imply the same
column sparsity; max row sum of D<=r, so ||D||<=r. Applying the same lemma,
 E u_b^2<=r^2 and E u_b<=r.

3. FINITE-BIT REPAIR OF RARE ENTRIES AND FAILED TAPES

Fix 0<epsilon<=1, r>=1 (zero observable is handled directly),
 T=10/epsilon, eta=epsilon/(100r),
 delta_entry=epsilon/[100 T(r+1)],
 delta_sample=epsilon/(100T).
Use an independently seeded baseline diagonal sampler with TV error at most
delta_sample. At each sampled b, estimate K_(b,b) and the at most r needed
K_(a,b) to relative eta, individually boosted to failure delta_entry.
If the estimated denominator is zero return Y=0. Otherwise form the rational
ratio sum h_b and return Y=clip(h_b,[-T,T]). All loops and random tapes use
the inherited deterministic caps; clipping is rational arithmetic.

On the event all r+1 counts are relatively accurate, eta<=1/100 and
 |h_b-g_b|<=3 eta u_b.
Clipping is 1-Lipschitz. On a bad entry event the two clipped values differ
by at most 2T. Thus under the true diagonal law,
 |E Y-E clip(g)|<=3 eta E u+2T(r+1)delta_entry
                  <=.03 epsilon+.02 epsilon.
The exact clipping bias is at most
 E|g-clip(g)|<=E g^2/T<=.1 epsilon.
Replacing the diagonal law by its TV approximation changes expectation by
at most 2T delta_sample=.02 epsilon. Including the Gibbs approximation error,
total estimator bias is at most .22 epsilon (using the conservative .05
epsilon Gibbs bound). This is an expectation bound over failed tapes; no
union over every Monte Carlo call and no rare-event lower bound is needed.

The sample second moment also stays bounded. On good counts,
 Y^2<=h_b^2<=2g_b^2+18 eta^2 u_b^2.
On bad counts Y^2<=T^2. Consequently
 E_true_diagonal Y^2<=2+18 eta^2 r^2+T^2(r+1)delta_entry
                    <=2+.0018+.1=2.1018.
Replacing the law changes this NONNEGATIVE bounded expectation by at most
T^2 delta_sample=.1, so E Y^2<=2.2018.
With M=ceil(256/epsilon^2) independent complete draws, Chebyshev gives
 Pr[|mean(Y)-E Y|>epsilon/2]<=4*2.2018/(M epsilon^2)<.035.
The output therefore has additive error below epsilon with success above
3/4. Independent median amplification costs O(log(1/delta_final)) repeats.
Each FPRAS has polynomial encoded outputs on all tapes, so arbitrary bad
ratios still have polynomial bit lengths before clipping.

4. COSTS AND SHARP BOUNDARIES

The circuit uses 2mp gates and N=8mp lifted coordinates, p original local
terms. The imported homogeneous count costs
 O(N^10 (N+log R)^2 eta^-2 log^2(2(N+log R)/eta))
arithmetic/oracle calls before rational-bit/oracle and amplification costs.
For fixed full entries, pinning only reduces the coordinate set and the
original coefficient range. Diagonal sampling to delta_sample=Theta(epsilon^2)
uses 2n relative prefix counts with the baseline accuracy/failure budgets
Theta(delta_sample/n). Every observable draw adds at most r+1 entry counts
at eta=Theta(epsilon/r) and log inverse failure O(log(r/epsilon)). Multiply
these separately charged costs by M=O(epsilon^-2) and confidence repetitions.
No claim of a practical exponent, near-optimal total runtime, or executed
full counting engine follows from the O(epsilon^-2) sample statement.

This covers additive sparse-observable expectations. It does NOT reconstruct
a global state, approximate a tiny expectation relatively, estimate arbitrary
dense observables from concise circuit descriptions, prepare a Gibbs state,
or license signed/complex Hamiltonian gates. A dense succinct operator can
have exponentially many row entries and falls outside the input contract.
Exact binary rational input is assumed; calibration uncertainty is a distinct
physical error budget. beta*C remains a numerical runtime parameter.

5. EXACT COUNTEREXAMPLE TO THE INSERTED-OBSERVABLE SHORTCUT

A boundary X flips the original wire equality to unequal bits. Complementing
the column endpoint turns this into both-or-neither selected endpoints, so
the partner polynomial is 1+xy. At x=y=1/2,
 Hess log(1+xy)=[[-4/25,16/25],[16/25,-4/25]].
The direction (1,1) gives positive curvature 24/25. This is not LC.
For a single qubit H=bX, b>0, Tr(X exp(beta H))/Tr exp(beta H)=tanh(beta b)>0,
so ignoring the insertion is not a harmless zero-observable convention.
The sparse importance route above avoids modifying the partner polynomial.

EXACT INPUT AND THE THERMAL LIFT

H_phys=-H, with
 H=sum_e[alpha_e(XX+YY)+gamma_e ZZ]+sum_v[b_v X+c_v Z],
 alpha_e>=|gamma_e|, b_v>=0, all data rational, any loopless graph.
For a rational s>=0, use the baseline shifted Euler gates:
 A_e=3alpha_e I+alpha_e(XX+YY)+gamma_e ZZ,
 A_v=(b_v+|c_v|)I+b_vX+c_vZ, F_i=I+sA_i.
The pair polynomial on four coordinates, row bits retained and column bits
complemented, is
 a(x0x1+x2x3)+b(x0x3+x1x2)+c(x0x2+x1x3),
 a=1+s(3alpha+gamma), b=1+s(3alpha-gamma), c=2salpha.
The field polynomial is homogenized, as proved in the baseline, to
 d z0z1+(u/2)x(z0+z1)+(v/2)y(z0+z1)+dxy,
 d=sb, u=1+s(b+|c|-c), v=1+s(b+|c|+c).
Each positive local table has fixed degree two on four coordinates, with at
most six entries. Zero entries are genuinely absent, not perturbed.

For e pair gates and f field gates, g=e+f and N=4g. The first measure mu is
the product of the gate tables. The second measure nu is coefficient one on
one coordinate per physical wire, plus a uniform quota f among the 2f dummy
coordinates. Every coordinate belongs to exactly one group in each factor.
Both have degree N/2. The hard coefficient is the trace circuit, multiplied
by 2 per physically unused qubit. The compiler actually creates these
halfedges/wires; it does not infer them from an arbitrary polynomial oracle.

IMPLEMENTED CANCELLATION LEMMA

Let S be supported by mu, u in S, and S'=S-u. Every local factor has fixed
degree two. If v lies in a different factor from u, S'+v has degree one in
u's factor and degree three in v's factor, and its mu coefficient is zero.
If v is in u's factor, all other factors cancel from

  z_v mu(S'+v) / sum_w z_w mu(S'+w).

Only the at-most-three local reinsertion choices remain. Their exact weights
are z_v times the corresponding entry of u's four-variable table. A pinning
restriction merely removes forbidden local choices. If u is forced in,
only self-reinsertion remains; skipping this self loop is exact.

The nu side has the analogous fixed-quota cancellation. A physical wire has
at most two choices. For a dummy deletion u, the candidates are u plus all
currently unselected, non-excluded dummy coordinates. Their weights are z_v.
A binary sum tree stores the latter set, excluding all currently selected
coordinates. First choose reinsertion u with probability
 z_u/(z_u+tree_total); otherwise descend the tree by exact rational left-mass
probabilities. A move u->v makes the two leaf updates eligible(u)=z_u and
eligible(v)=0. A field update changes just one eligible leaf. Cost O(log N).
No global mu coefficient product is evaluated in a production step.

Both base states have together N present copies. Choose one uniformly. For
the chosen copy u, do a deletion/resampling attempt with probability 1/2 if
u is in the other set, t/2 otherwise. Reinsert using the cancelled weights,
including u itself. This is EXACTLY the lazy chain

    P = I - [t/(2N)] L_q,

where q is source Eq.(19). The non-self q rate is
 t^(-1[u in other]) z_v mu(S-u+v)/sum_w z_w mu(S-u+w).
The factor t_v is NOT inserted into the reinsertion law. Detailed balance
holds because the deletion t_u^(-1[u in other]) cancels the departing overlap
penalty. In particular adding t_v by intuition would implement a different
chain; this exact detail was checked against a literal global-oracle version.

Support is preserved: every candidate has a positive coefficient; every
denominator is positive because self-reinsertion is supported. State mutation
occurs after all random choices succeed. A coin-cap abort raises TapeAbort;
the enclosing sample/count must abort wholly, rather than return a biased
partial state. Production code does not claim a whole-count wrapper yet.

COMPLEMENT-SYMMETRY LEMMA — COMPLETE DERIVATION

Assume mu(S)=mu(U\S), nu(T)=nu(U\T), both supported on N/2-subsets. At z=1,
the soft law is
  pi_t(S,T) proportional to mu(S)nu(T)t^|S intersect T|.
Under (S,T)->(U\S,U\T), the overlap becomes
  N-|S|-|T|+|S intersect T|=|S intersect T|.
Thus the involution preserves every soft weight, and swaps the zero-copy
and two-copy events at every coordinate. Their probabilities p0,p2 agree.

The imported Lemma5.5 supplies a gap-one continuous exchange chain uniformly
over unequal penalties and positive fields. Here is the required consequence,
reconstructed explicitly rather than treating Theorem5.1 as a black box.
For any coordinate u with p0p2>0, remove its overlap penalty and multiply its
field by s=sqrt(t p0/p2). The resulting copy-count probabilities satisfy
  (q0,q1,q2) proportional to (p0,s p1,p0), so q0=q2<=1/2.
The chain removes a unique present copy at total rate <=1 and cannot go
directly from two copies to zero. For the absent-coordinate indicator g,
its Dirichlet form is at most q1. The gap-one inequality therefore gives
 q0(1-q0)<=q1, whence q1/q0>=1/2 and p0p2<=4t p1^2.
Cases p0p2=0 are immediate. Since p0=p2,
 p0+p2=2sqrt(p0p2)<=4sqrt(t)p1<=4sqrt(t).
Union over N coordinates gives
 P(T!=U\S)<=4N sqrt(t)=1/4 when t=1/(256N^2).
Consequently the hard event has probability >=3/4 without any acquired field.
Square roots here are proof devices; the actual fields are exactly one.

Every pair gate is complement-invariant on its four variables. For a field
gate c_v=0 implies u=v, and every monomial has the same coefficient as its
four-variable complement. The product mu is consequently complement-invariant.
Each physical wire's quota is half its size, and the dummy quota f is half
2f; their nu factor is complement-invariant. This proves the XXZ subclass
claim for any circuit size and ordering. It covers gamma=+-alpha, zero X
fields, and inactive qubits. It does not cover arbitrary c_v!=0. Pinnings
used later in soft normalizer self-reduction need not preserve the involution;
their mixing still uses the imported uniform gap, with the unchanged z=1.

COUNTING REDUCTION AND EXPLICIT CAPS

Let K=2N, 0<rho<=1 and 0<delta<=1/4. Put alpha=rho/(128N). In each of the
K membership stages, draw M=ceil[2alpha^-2 ln(8K/delta)] independent
conditional soft samples, each within TV alpha/2. Estimate the next-bit
frequency and choose its empirical majority. Denote the selected empirical
frequency by phat_j>=1/2. Retain one sampled witness with that chosen bit;
it always exists and supplies the next conditioned supported state.

For each stage, Hoeffding bounds fluctuation by alpha/2 except with
probability <=delta/(4K). Sampling bias is at most alpha/2. Thus the true
selected conditional probability pj satisfies |pj-phat_j|<=alpha and
pj>=1/2-alpha>=1/4. Summing the logarithmic error gives
 |sum_j log(pj/phat_j)|<=K*4alpha=rho/16.
For the final supported state X, the exact telescoping identity is
 Z_soft=W(X)/product_j pj. Hence the empirical denominator estimates Z_soft
within exp(+-rho/16), with total statistical failure <=delta/4.

Separately estimate p_hard with
 Mc=ceil[2(64/rho)^2 ln(8/delta)] independent unconditional soft samples,
each within TV rho/128. Hoeffding plus bias gives absolute error <=rho/64
except with probability <=delta/4. Since p_hard>=3/4, its relative error
is <=rho/48. Multiply this estimate by the soft normalizer. Its total
multiplicative error is inside (1+-rho) for rho<=1. There is no inverse
thermal trace, prefix probability, or projection rarity in these sample caps.
All bitwise-conditioned measures retain fixed degrees and are LC by
derivative/zero-coordinate closure, with coefficient range no larger than R.

For every such conditional, source Lemma5.5 gives discrete gap >=t/(2N),
and pi_min>=t^(N/2)/(4^N R) at z=1. Define
 H=2N+ceil(log2 R)+(N/2)ceil(log2(1/t)).
Then ln(1/pi_min)<=H. The standard reversible L2 bound gives
 TV(P^J(X,.),pi)<=0.5*pi(X)^(-1/2)*exp[-Jt/(2N)].
A sufficient directly computable integer is
 J=ceil[(2N/t)(H+ceil(log2(1/theta))+1)],
with theta=rho/(256N), small enough for both sample stages. Binary logs
are computed by exact doubling comparisons and upper-bound natural logs.
J=O(N^3(H+log(N/rho))). The number Q of soft samples is
O(N^3 rho^-2 log(N/delta)). Every production transition uses at most
2ceil(log2 N)+2 nontrivial rational binary choices. Let Nc be the actual
deterministic bound Q*J*[2ceil(log2 N)+2]. Cap each binary choice at
r=ceil(log2(4Nc/delta)) fair bits. One unresolved coin has probability
<=2^-r. Aborting the whole count on any unresolved choice costs <=delta/4.
All loops, random tape length, and rational output sizes are bounded even
on bad histories. Pseudorandom seeded tests are reproducibility tools, not
a claim that a computer PRNG supplies mathematical independent fair bits.

ARITHMETIC, BIT, MEMORY AND THERMAL COSTS

The local component uses O(log N) rational operations per transition.
The general field-acquisition source bound loses its O(N) coefficient-scan
factor here: balance work becomes O(N^9 B^2 log N log B), rather than the
generic O(N^10 B^2 log B), before bit costs. This is an implementation
structure saving, not a new gap theorem. More substantially, symmetry removes
that entire balancing stage and gives the displayed O(N^6 H rho^-2 polylog)
count arithmetic bound, with the explicit logarithms above.

Let ell bound local positive rational table encoding lengths. At z=1 the
tree has integer masses <=2f, and local candidate ratios involve <=3
ell-bit entries. No global N*ell-bit coefficient occurs per transition.
With conservative schoolbook rational costs, one may upper-bound the full
count by the arithmetic count times
 O(r(ell+log N+r)^3), plus Q initializations at
 O(N*poly(N ell+NlogN)) bit work, and the final rational products at
 polynomial cost in N ell+Nlog M+Nlog(1/t).
This deliberately loose bit bound does not call rationals unit-cost objects.
Instance/state/tree storage is O(N(ell+logN)) bits plus output/tape-generator
state; streaming samples need only two witness states and frequency counters.
For arbitrary rational z, charge the full sum of their encoding lengths to
tree sums; source common-base fields permit the sharper O(K_updates logN)
encoding because total exponent absolute value is capped. That improvement
does not silently extend to an opaque real-field oracle.

For the actual thermal approximation, with
 C=3sum alpha+sum(b+|c|), tau=2beta C,
 m=ceil max{1,4tau,20tau^2/epsilon}, p=#local terms,
the conservative baseline circuit has 2mp gates and N=8mp variables.
The reduced counter's tolerance rho is a constant fraction of physical
epsilon; the known scalar exp(-beta C) is evaluated at charged finite
precision as in the baseline. N scales as p[1+beta C+(beta C)^2/epsilon].
No logarithm-of-temperature runtime claim follows. Local bit size is
ell=O(L+log m). The sample planner for a three-qubit four-term c=0 model,
beta=1/2 and epsilon=1/10 already gives m=6235, 49,880 gates, N=199,520.
These gates were NOT allocated/simulated. The improved polynomial is still
far from a practical many-body simulation algorithm at this conservative
product-formula certificate. Execution energy, backend token costs, and any
hardware preparation cost are UNKNOWN; no joule estimate is manufactured.

STATUS
An internally derived candidate theorem improves the unchanged universal
product decoder from the baseline O(N^-1/4) bound to O_(M,B)(N^-1/2).
The derivation below transports the entire positive mixing density, avoiding
the baseline comparison of individual N-fold products. A matching isotropic
lower bound makes N^-1/2 sharp FOR THIS UNCHANGED DECODER. It is not a lower
bound for every possible reverse channel. A direct finite-bit preparation
construction has classical cost polynomial in input length and log(N/epsilon)
at fixed public M,B, plus N native local emissions. It achieves the coarser
N^-1/2 state approximation; it does not replace the stronger known-parameter
O(N^-3/2) separable-cone theorem or its accuracy-dependent compiler.

The proof is independent and unaudited by another worker. The main residual
audit gate is the weighted-L1 Duhamel implementation of the mixing-density
semigroup. Its hypotheses and a stochastic-flow justification are stated
below. No end-to-end certified interval code or quantum hardware was executed.
Historical priority, especially the 1991 critical-fluctuation paper, is open.

1. EXACT INTERFACE AND IMPORTED BASELINE

J_i=(1/2)sum sigma_i^(a), s=N^(3/4),
K=2J^2/N+Q_A(J)/N^(3/2)+b.J/s,
rho=exp(K)/Tr exp(K), A=A^T real, ||A||<=M, |b|<=B.
M,B are fixed public bounds. The construction below is uniform over A,b
and permits noncommuting anisotropy and field. It concerns the entire N-qubit
density, including multiplicities, without external-reference preservation.

Let a=2N^-1/4 and
P_N(x)=tau_N(x)^tensor N,
tau_N(x)=exp(a x.sigma)/(2cosh(a|x|)).
Let p_(A,b)(x) be the normalized Lebesgue density proportional to
exp[-4|x|^4/3+x^TAx+b.x]. The universal cq decoder is unchanged:
S_N p=integral P_N(x)p(x) dx. It knows N, not A or b.

The audited baseline has a positive isotropic heat identity
exp(tJ^2)=integral exp(y.J) nu_t(dy), t>0,
nu_t=(4pi t)^-3/2 exp(-t/4-|y|^2/(4t)) sinch(|y|/2)dy.
The elementary character proof is in
work/cycle1/spin_full_separability_audit.txt, Section 1.

C=A+MI>=0, t0=2/N-M/N^(3/2)>0, H=Q_C/N^(3/2)+b.J/s.
Write C=sum lambda_i n_i n_i^T. The baseline CP semigroup is
L(X)={H,X}/2-(1/4)sum lambda_i/N^(3/2)[F_i,[F_i,X]],
F_i=n_i.J. Equivalently its quadratic summand is
(lambda_i/(4N^(3/2))){F_i,{F_i,X}}.
Every Gaussian-filter trajectory is g^tensor N; hence
sigma=exp(L)(exp(t0J^2))/Tr[exp(L)(exp(t0J^2))]
is an iid-product mixture. The existing double-commutator Duhamel proof gives
T(rho,sigma)<=Esep_N=O_(M,B)(N^-3/2), T=||.||_1/2.

For an explicit public certificate, put
x_j=j(j+1)/s^2, ell_j=j/s,
a_j=(2j+1)m_N(j)exp[2j(j+1)/N],
m_N(j)=(2j+1)/(N+1) binom(N+1,N/2-j),
Z0=sum a_j exp(-Mx_j),
U=sum a_j exp(Mx_j+B ell_j)
 [2Mx_j+(B/2)ell_j+(2Mx_j+B ell_j)^2/3].
Then Esep_N=min(1,3M U/(2s^2 Z0)). This is an imported INTERNAL theorem,
not an external publication or a new contribution of this worker.

After y=4N^-1/4 x, the exact normalized base mixing density is
q_N,M(x)=f_N,M(|x|)/Z_N,M,
f_N,M(r)=sinch(ar) exp[N logcosh(ar)-2sqrt(N)r^2/d0],
d0=1-M/(2sqrt(N)). Its limit is q_inf,M proportional to
exp[-4r^4/3-Mr^2]. The baseline proves, for every fixed L,K,
integral e^(Lr^2+Kr)|q_N,M-q_inf,M| dx=O_(M,L,K)(N^-1/2)
and uniform weighted polynomial moments. The compact Taylor remainder and
quartic/interior, Gaussian/exterior envelopes are reproduced in Section 5.

2. EXACT PRODUCT-ORBIT TRANSPORT OPERATOR

For a unit vector n apply g_u=exp(u n.sigma/2) to a normalized qubit.
If its Bloch vector is m=tanh(|y|)y/|y|, then
dm/du=n-(n.m)m.
Therefore its natural parameter y evolves with velocity B(y)n, where
B(y)=beta(|y|)I+[1-beta(|y|)]ee^T,
beta(z)=z coth z, e=y/|y|, B(0)=I.
For x=y/a, define v_n(x)=a^-1 B(ax)n and
s_n(x)=N tanh(ar)n.x/r.
The unnormalized N-fold branch trace has logarithmic derivative s_n.
Consequently the EXACT matrix identity is
 {F_n,P_N(x)}=v_n(x).grad P_N(x)+s_n(x)P_N(x).

The density adjoint is
T_n f=s_n f-div(v_n f).
Integration by parts gives
integral P_N T_n f= {F_n,integral P_N f}.
Thus the exact mixing-density semigroup has generator
mathcal L_N f=sum_i lambda_i/(4N^(3/2)) T_(n_i)^2 f
                 +(1/(2N^(3/4)))T_b f,                 (1)
where v_b,s_b,T_b are linear in b. Its positive action is not an assumed
P-representation: push each density through the smooth diffeomorphism
x -> a^-1 log-natural-coordinate(g tau_N(x)g*) and multiply by the branch
trace weight [Tr(g tau_N(x)g*)]^N, then average the Gaussian filter paths.
The local boost vector fields are globally Lipschitz and have linear growth.
The local two-by-two linear Stratonovich filter SDE exists on every finite
time interval. Each path is invertible and gives a smooth interior Bloch-ball
diffeomorphism, so the push-forward density and the positive semigroup exist.
Finite Gaussian products and their stochastic limit give (1), including
degenerate C; no ellipticity or eigenvalue gap is required.

Useful explicit bounds follow from elementary coth inequalities:
0<=beta-1<=min(z,z^2/3), 0<=beta'<=1, 0<=beta''<=2/3.
The z^2/3 bound follows by differentiating
(z^2+3)sinh z-3z cosh z; the derivative is
z(z cosh z-sinh z)>=0. The z bound follows from e^(2z)-1>=2z.
beta'=coth z-z csch^2 z is between 0 and 1, and
beta''=2 csch^2 z(beta-1)<=2/3.
Differentiating B=I+(beta-1)(I-ee^T) gives
||B||<=1+z, ||DB||<=3, ||D^2 B||<=7; the more conservative 16
is used for the last two bounds below. For the second derivative use
||D ee^T||<=2/z, ||D^2 ee^T||<=8/z^2,
beta'/z<=2/3 and (beta-1)/z^2<=1/3. Origin limits are smooth.
Hence
|v_n|<=N^(1/4)(r+1/2), ||Dv_n||<=16,
|div v_n|<=48, |grad div v_n|<=48a,
|s_n|<=2N^(3/4)r, |grad s_n|<=2N^(3/4).
The exact divergence is -2(beta(ar)-1)(n.x/r)/(ar), which also directly
checks the removable origin behavior.

3. THE N^-1/2 DENSITY DEFECT, WITH AN EXPLICIT POLYNOMIAL

Set V(x)=x^TCx+b.x and f_t=e^(tV)q_N,M, 0<=t<=1.
For N>=M^2 (d0>=1/2), its log gradient and Hessian obey
 |grad log f_t|<=G(r)=(16/3)r^3+(8M+4/3)r+B,
 ||Hess log f_t||<=H0(r)=16r^2+8M+4.              (2)
To see the cancellations, N a tanh(ar)-4sqrt(N)r/d0 is the radial
derivative of the log-cosh/Gaussian factor. Use
0<=z-tanh z<=z^3/3 and tanh^2 z<=z^2. Its Hessian radial and
tangential eigenvalues are bounded by 16r^2+4M. The log-sinch gradient
is at most a^2 r/3, and its Hessian at most a^2<=4; this also follows
from the variance formula for log E exp(zU), U uniform on [-1,1].
The tV derivatives add at most 4Mr+B and 4M. This proves (2), and
|grad f_t|<=G f_t, ||Hess f_t||<=(H0+G^2)f_t.

Write h_n=s_n-div v_n. Direct expansion gives
 T_n^2 f=(h_n^2-v_n.grad h_n)f
         +[-2h_n v_n+(v_n.grad)v_n].grad f
         +v_n^T Hess(f) v_n.                          (3)
The leading multiplication term is lambda_i(n_i.x)^2 f. In fact
 |lambda_i s_n^2/(4N^(3/2))-lambda_i(n_i.x)^2|
 <=(8/3)lambda_i N^-1/2 r^4,
because |tanh^2 z-z^2|<=2z^4/3 and a^2=4N^-1/2.
All remaining terms of (3) have N^-1/2 or smaller coefficients.
One fully explicit sufficient polynomial, using the displayed 16/48 bounds,
is
 P_Q(r)=(8/3)r^4+48r+576+(49/2)(r+1/2)
          +(r+28)(r+1/2)G(r)
          +(r+1/2)^2[H0(r)+G(r)^2]/4,
 P_B(r)=(4/3)r^3+24+(r+1/2)G(r)/2,
 P_(M,B)(r)=6M P_Q(r)+B P_B(r).
For example |h|<=(2r+48)N^(3/4) and
|grad h|<=98N^(3/4), while sum lambda_i<=6M.
The field multiplication error is at most (4/3)B N^-1/2 r^3;
its divergence and transport terms give the remaining P_B terms.
Thus POINTWISE, uniformly in all admitted A,b and t,
 |(mathcal L_N-V)f_t|(x)<=N^-1/2 P_(M,B)(r) f_t(x).    (4)
This bound compares classical densities, not individual product branches.

4. UNIFORM MASS AMPLIFICATION AND WEIGHTED-L1 DUHAMEL

For a signed density k, positivity gives
||mathcal P_u k||_1<=integral |k(x)| R_(u,N)(x)dx,
where R is the expected trajectory trace weight from P_N(x), u<=1.
Set alpha=(2M+1)/N^(3/2) and K0=B^2/4. The quantum H obeys
H<=alpha J^2+K0 I by completing the field square.
The central curve exp[u(alpha J^2+K0)] is a supersolution for L;
CP Duhamel and selfadjointness therefore give
R_(u,N)(x)<=exp(K0)Tr[P_N(x)exp(alpha J^2)].
Using the positive heat integral, scalar Hoeffding for each qubit, and
sinch(|y|/2)<=exp(|y|^2/8), Gaussian integration gives
Tr[tau^tensor N exp(alpha J^2)]
 <=exp(-alpha/4)d^-3/2 exp[alpha N^2|m|^2/(4d)],
d=1-alpha(N+1)/2. If N>=4(2M+1)^2 then d>=1/2, so
 R_(u,N)(x)<=C_R exp(K_R r^2),
C_R=2^(3/2)exp(B^2/4), K_R=2(2M+1).                    (5)

For fixed N the flow semigroup has finite L1 norm; its diffusion coefficients
have at most quadratic growth and the weight coefficients are smooth.
Apply the ordinary variation-of-constants identity to the smooth f_t,
initially with compact radial cutoffs. Integration by parts identifies its
generator with (1). The cutoff terms converge to zero in weighted L1 because
f_t and its first two derivatives have quartic/interior and Gaussian/exterior
tails, and (5) controls the remaining semigroup. Dominated convergence gives
 mathcal P_1 q_N,M-e^V q_N,M
   =integral_0^1 mathcal P_(1-t)(mathcal L_N-V)f_t dt
in L1. This is the load-bearing new analytic step to audit independently.
It uses the actual positive flow semigroup, not merely a formal PDE.

Define the entirely public one-dimensional radial integral
 I_N(M,B)=integral q_N,M(x)
       exp[(2M+K_R)r^2+Br] P_(M,B)(r) dx.
Equations (4)-(5) imply
 ||mathcal P_1 q_N,M-e^V q_N,M||_1
 <=C_R I_N(M,B) N^-1/2.                              (6)
I_N is finite and uniformly bounded for fixed M,B by the quartic-tail lemma.
It is reducible to radial elementary integrals and can be enclosed by interval
quadrature plus the explicit tails; it was not interval-evaluated in code here.

Both unnormalized masses in (6) are >=1. The e^V mass is >=1 by rotation
symmetry, C>=0, and pairing x,-x. For the flow mass use selfadjoint L and
scalar Jensen on each spin block:
Tr exp(L_j)I_j/d_j>=exp(Tr H_j/d_j)>=1.
For positive unnormalized f,g of mass at least one,
TV(f/int f,g/int g)<=||f-g||_1.
Consequently sigma is within C_R I_N N^-1/2 of the product mixture
with density proportional to e^V q_N,M.

5. QUARTIC PRIOR REPLACEMENT AND THE NEW UNIVERSAL RATE

Put
 Rprior_N=integral e^(2Mr^2+Br)|q_N,M-q_inf,M|dx.
Normalization and the mass lower bounds above give a further state error
at most Rprior_N. The M shift cancels:
normalized e^V q_inf,M =p_(A,b).
Thus the EXPLICIT NEW PUBLIC BOUND is
 T(rho_(N,A,b), S_N p_(A,b))
 <=min(1,Esep_N+C_R I_N N^-1/2+Rprior_N).                (7)
Uniform weighted quartic moments give I_N=O_(M,B)(1), and
Rprior_N=O_(M,B)(N^-1/2), hence the claimed O_(M,B)(N^-1/2).

The scalar prior rate can be seen without importing a convergence slogan.
On z=ar<=1, log-cosh Taylor with bounded sixth derivative and
1/d0=1+M/(2sqrt N)+O_M(N^-1) give
|log f_N,M+4r^4/3+Mr^2|
 <=N^-1/2[(2/3+M^2)r^2+64r^6].
logcosh z<=z^2/2-z^4/48 and sinch z<=exp(z^2/2) give an
integrable quartic envelope after every fixed exp(Lr^2+Kr) tilt.
For z>=1, logcosh(z)/z^2 is decreasing and
delta=1/2-logcosh(1)>0, giving an exterior envelope
exp[-4delta sqrt(N)r^2+Lr^2+(K+2)r]. Its start is r=N^(1/4)/2,
so its mass is exponentially small for sufficiently large N.
A compact annulus bounds the normalizer below. Mean-value control of
exponentials on the interior and normalization yield the stated weighted
O(N^-1/2) rate. All constants depend on fixed numeric M,B.

This improves only the REVERSE product-decoder rate. The baseline forward
measurement channel still has its own stated error. No bidirectional N^-1/2
experiment theorem, sharper memory lower bound, or local-lattice transfer
is asserted. One retains the stronger O(N^-3/2) parameter-dependent cone
distance and its distinct input interface.

6. SHARPNESS FOR THE UNCHANGED DECODER, ALREADY AT A=b=0

For M=A=b=0, rho_N is EXACTLY the mixture with q_N,0. On every fixed compact
radius interval, the log-density expansion is
 log f_N,0=-4r^4/3+N^-1/2 h0(r)+O(N^-1 polynomial(r)),
 h0(r)=(2/3)r^2+(64/45)r^6.
The sixth coefficient of logcosh is 1/45; an exploratory coefficient
128/45 was corrected before this immutable checkpoint.
The same envelopes upgrade the expansion to weighted L1:
sqrt(N)(q_N,0-p0) -> p0[h0-E_p0 h0].                 (8)
Here p0 proportional to exp(-4r^4/3), and the radial law has density
proportional to r^2 exp(-4r^4/3).

The product-state total-spin measurement R_j=(j+1/2)/N^(3/4) concentrates
at the input radius r. To prove it, let m=tanh(ar). In tau_N(x)^tensor N,
E J^2=3N/4+N(N-1)m^2/4,
Var J^2<=3 binom(N,2)/4+3 binom(N,3)m^2/2.
These identities follow by writing
J^2=3N/4+(1/2)sum_(i<j) sigma_i.sigma_j.
Disjoint pairs have zero covariance; an overlapping pair's real covariance
is m^2-m^4; a pair-square expectation is 3-2m^2.
After division by N^3, the variance vanishes, while E J^2/N^(3/2)->r^2.
So R_j->r in probability for each fixed r, including r=0.

Apply a bounded radial test approximating sign(h0-E h0) to that measurement.
Equation (8), dominated convergence and concentration imply
 liminf sqrt(N) T(rho_N,S_N p0)
 >=(1/2)E_p0 |h0-E_p0 h0|.
The opposite limsup follows from the exact product-mixture representation
and TV(q_N,0,p0), proving the exact limit
 lim sqrt(N) T(rho_N,S_N p0)=kappa>0,
kappa=(1/2)E_p0|h0-E_p0 h0|.                         (9)
The floating value is approximately 0.5558492847922311. This is an exact
integral definition; the decimal is only a diagnostic. It makes the new
N^-1/2 rate sharp for this map, even at zero unknown perturbation. A different
N-dependent universal decoder could conceivably improve the rate.

7. DIRECT FINITE-BIT KNOWN-PARAMETER PREPARATION CONSEQUENCE

Known rational A,b and fixed rational M,B admit a much simpler route at the
accuracy (7): sample p_(A,b), then prepare the selected tau_N(x) N times.
Use a three-dimensional Gaussian proposal with density pi^-3/2 exp(-r^2),
realized as three standard normals divided by sqrt(2). Put
c=3(M+2)^2/16+B^2/4, k=ceil(2c+1), Henv=2^k.
The elementary envelope
-4r^4/3+x^TAx+b.x+r^2
 <=-4r^4/3+(M+2)r^2+B^2/4<=c
and ln2>=1/2 prove rejection probability
alpha(x)=exp[-4r^4/3+x^TAx+b.x+r^2]/Henv<=1.
The target normalizer is >=(4pi/3)exp(-4/3-M-B) by the unit ball.
A legal explicit lower acceptance bound is
p0=2^[-k-ceil(2(4/3+M+B))-1]>0.
No partition function or inverse original quantum success probability is used.

For rational/dyadic epsilon, truncate at K=O_(M,B)(log(1/epsilon)) attempts,
choosing Kp0>=log(8/epsilon) by conservative rational powers-of-two bounds.
Emit a fixed product on exhaustion; its probability is <=epsilon/8.
Clip all 3K normals at L=O_(M,B)(sqrt(log(1/epsilon))) using a certified
rational upper endpoint; the union tail is <=epsilon/8.
The baseline endpoint-safe inverse-CDF construction draws finite dyadic bits
and encloses the normal CDF with a rational integrated exponential series.
It couples each retained normal within h, with bit cost polynomial in
log(1/h)+L^2. No exact-normal oracle is retained.

On retained proposals r<=R=sqrt(3/2)(L+1), the rejection exponent has gradient
at most La=(16/3)R^3+2(M+1)R+B. Enclose complete normal scales and alpha to
their assigned output tolerance. Dyadic uniform comparisons, clipped to [0,1],
have disagreement O((La+1)h) per attempt; even an unresolved comparison
stops at its declared tolerance and is charged by this coupling bound.
Thus choose h<=epsilon/[C K(La+1)] for a stated sufficiently large absolute
C (e.g. 1024). Compute tau_N(x) to local trace error epsilon/(64N), and
also require h<=epsilon/(1024 N^(3/4)); qubit Bloch contraction gives
T(P_N(x),P_N(x'))<=N a|x-x'|/2=N^(3/4)|x-x'|.
All total errors, with constant slack, are <epsilon.

Selected Bloch-vector magnitudes near zero use a slack threshold and the
maximally mixed state; otherwise direction normalization has a known
inverse-polynomial lower bound. Dyadic Bernoulli eigenstate choices plus
native single-qubit preparations each of trace error epsilon/(32N) cost
N local emissions and add <=epsilon/32. A fixed discrete gate set needs
its own compiler overhead. No quantum entangling gate or filter success is
needed in this declared native-preparation model.

Worst-case classical bit work is polynomial in
Lin+log N+log(1/epsilon) for FIXED numeric M,B (the number of proposals is
O_(M,B)(log(1/epsilon))). Expected proposal count is O_(M,B)(1).
Retain only one 3-vector and local-qubit description, with
O(Lin+polylog(N/epsilon)) classical bits; the N output systems are separately
counted. Parameter acquisition is not free: rational input is supplied, or a
polynomial-cost precision interface must be charged. Constants deteriorate
with numeric M,B, and growing bounds have no uniform polynomial claim.
The averaged output state error is (7)+epsilon. For sharper accuracy below
the N^-1/2 scale use the existing Brownian-mixture compiler, whose stronger
known-parameter approximation and eta-dependent costs remain intact.

This is a constructive finite-bit THEOREM, not an executed finite-bit program.
It does not require O(N) binomial preprocessing unless the explicit public
certificate Esep_N is evaluated. Direct radial I_N and Rprior_N certificates
require separately charged interval integration/tail enclosures.

STATUS AND STRONGEST CLAIM

New internally derived theorem candidate: the EXISTING parameter-independent
product decoder has uniform whole-density error O_(M,B)(N^-1/2), improving the
baseline O(N^-1/4) proof. For that precise decoder this exponent is sharp:
at A=b=0 the error is c_iso N^-1/2+o(N^-1/2), where c_iso>0 has the explicit
one-dimensional integral below (floating evaluation 0.555849284792...).
This is not an optimal-deficiency lower bound against every possible decoder.

The proof retains the baseline known-parameter O(N^-3/2) separable-proximity
theorem as a distinct, NON-sharp upper bound. Scalar A=aI gives an exact
identical-product mixture whenever 2/N+a/N^1.5>=0, for every field b; thus
an N^-3/2 lower bound cannot hold uniformly across the admitted family.
No interval sampler, hardware experiment, or historical-priority clearance
has been acquired.

EXACT INTERFACE

N qubits, J_i=sum sigma_i/2, s=N^3/4, A real symmetric with ||A||<=M,
b real with |b|<=B, public FIXED finite M,B>=0:
 rho_N(A,b)=e^K/Tr e^K,
 K=2J^2/N+Q_A(J)/s^2+b.J/s,
 Q_A=sum A_ik(J_iJ_k+J_kJ_i)/2.
Trace distance is T=||rho-sigma||_1/2.
The classical input is ONE exact observation with density
 p_(A,b)(y)=Z_(A,b)^-1 exp(-4|y|^4/3+y^TAy+b.y).
The decoder remains EXACTLY
 S_N(y)=[e^(2N^-1/4 y.sigma)/Tr e^(2N^-1/4 y.sigma)]^tensor N.
It uses neither A nor b. The assertion concerns full density, with all Schur
multiplicity registers included; no external reference/purification is retained.
Source sampling, source acquisition and finite presentation are distinct costs.

BASELINE FACTS RECONSTRUCTED

Let C=A+MI>=0, t0=2/N-M/s^2>0 and diagonalize
 C=sum lambda_i n_i n_i^T. Put c_i=lambda_i/s^2, F_i=n_i.J,
 H=Q_C/s^2+b.J/s.
The exact positive heat identity on every SU(2) representation is
 e^(tJ^2)=int e^(v.J) nu_t(dv),
 nu_t(dv)=(4pi t)^-3/2 e^-t/4 e^(-|v|^2/(4t)) sinch(|v|/2)d^3v.
Rotation invariance plus the spin-j character and a differentiated Gaussian
cosh integral prove this identity. The j=0 case proves nu_t has mass one.

The CP local-filter generator is
 L(X)={H,X}/2-(1/4)sum c_i[F_i,[F_i,X]]
     =sum c_i{F_i,{F_i,X}}/4+{b.J/s,X}/2.
Gaussian sandwiches represent every quadratic exponential; finite Trotter
products and the limit preserve identical-single-qubit-product mixtures.
For W=e^L(e^(t0J^2)), sigma=W/Tr W, the baseline Duhamel/rotation estimate gives
 T(sigma,rho)<=Esep_N=O_(M,B)(N^-3/2).
It is enough here to retain that internally reconstructed bound; its exact
public binomial certificate is in work/cycle3/critical_separability_audit.txt,
equation (A). No imported frontier preprint is needed for this finite-dimensional
heat/filter/Duhamel argument.

1. THE EXACT CLASSICAL DENSITY GENERATOR

Write q=N^-1/4, epsilon=N^-1/2. Use scaled BLOCH coordinates
 m=2q x, |x|<R_N=1/(2q), k_N(x)=((I+2q x.sigma)/2)^tensor N.
These coordinates differ from the decoder's exponential coordinate y.

A sandwich e^(alpha n.J) has local filter e^(alpha n.sigma/2).
Its normalized Bloch flow and trace weight obey
 dm/dalpha=n-(n.m)m,  d log w/dalpha=N(n.m).
Differentiating w(alpha) k_N(m(alpha)) gives exactly {n.J,k_N}.
Consequently, with u_n=n.x and a_n(x)=n-4epsilon u_n x,
 B_n=s D_n, D_n=2u_n+(epsilon/2)a_n.grad,
 and B_n k_N={n.J,k_N}.
Iterating this identity gives B_n^2 k_N={n.J,{n.J,k_N}}, without an
approximation. The field uses (b.J)/(2s), so its generator is D_b/2.

On densities, formal adjoints with respect to d^3x are
 T_n f=-(1/2)div(a_n f), D_n^*=2u_n+epsilon T_n.
The exact positive trace-WEIGHTED classical filter semigroup Q_t has generator
 G_N^* f=sum_i (lambda_i/4)(D_ni^*)^2 f+(1/2)D_b^* f.       (1)
Its integration against k_N intertwines with e^(tL). Positivity is supplied
by the actual random normalized-filter flows and their positive trace weights;
it is not inferred merely from the displayed differential expression.

Define h(x)=x^TCx+b.x. Expanding (1) gives EXACTLY
 (G_N^*-h)f =
 epsilon sum_i (lambda_i/4)[2u_i T_i f+T_i(2u_i f)]
 +(epsilon/2)T_b f
 +epsilon^2 sum_i (lambda_i/4)T_i^2 f.                     (2)
Every coefficient of (2) and of its first two derivative terms is bounded
by a fixed polynomial in |x|, uniformly epsilon<=1. Lambda_i<=2M.
The leading operation is multiplication by h. The existing branchwise
N^-1/4 bound misses this density-level cancellation.

The vector fields are tangent to the Bloch-ball boundary:
 a_n.x=(n.x)(1-4epsilon |x|^2)=0 at |x|=R_N.
Invertible filters preserve the open ball.

2. UNIFORM WEIGHTED SOBOLEV CONTROL OF THE EXACT SEED

The exact normalized seed in exponential coordinates y has density
 q_N,M(y) proportional to
 sinch(2q r) exp[N logcosh(2q r)-2sqrt(N)r^2/d0],
 r=|y|, d0=1-M/(2sqrt(N)).
It is mapped to Bloch coordinates by
 F_N(y)=tanh(2q r)/(2q) y/r.
Let f_N=F_N#q_N,M. Its unnormalized radial density is explicitly
 ftilde_N(x)= (u/t) cosh(u)^3
             exp[N(logcosh u-u^2/(2d0))],
 t=2q|x|<1, u=atanh t, u/t=1 at zero.                    (3)
Indeed the radial Jacobian is (u/t)^2/(1-t^2), and multiplying
sinch u=t/(u sqrt(1-t^2)) produces (3).

For every fixed K,L>=0 and integer k, and |alpha|<=2,
 sup_N int_(|x|<R_N) e^(K|x|^2+L|x|)(1+|x|)^k
                      |partial^alpha f_N(x)| d^3x <infinity             (4)
for all sufficiently large N. Here is the full elementary control.

On 0<=u<=1, t and u are comparable, du/d|x|=2q cosh^2u<=Cq.
Let ell=log ftilde_N. Since
 u-tanh u<=u^3/3,
 d0^-1-1=M/(2sqrt(N)d0),
 and log(u/t)+3logcosh u is even analytic with bounded first two
derivatives on [0,1], one has
 |ell_u|<=C[N u^3+M sqrt(N)u+u],
 |ell_uu|<=C[N u^2+M sqrt(N)+1].
Also u_rr=8q^2 sinh u cosh^3u<=Cq^2u.
These imply
 |ell_r|<=C_M(r^3+r),
 |ell_rr|+|ell_r|/r<=C_M(1+r^4), r=|x|.
The r=0 value is the continuous even limit. Thus first/second Cartesian
derivatives of ftilde_N are bounded by C_M(1+r)^8 ftilde_N.
For f(u)=u^2/2-logcosh u, f(u)>=u^4/48 on [0,1].
Since u>=2qr, N f(u)>=r^4/3. The additional M term is nonpositive.
Hence ftilde_N<=C exp(-r^4/3) on this entire region.

On u>1, f(u)>=delta u^2 with delta=1/2-logcosh1>0;
the ratio logcosh u/u^2 is decreasing. Formula (3) then gives
 ftilde_N<=C(u+1)e^(3u-delta N u^2).
Differentiating at most twice with respect to x introduces at most
C N^2(1+u)^k exp(4u), times a fixed power of q; these factors follow
from du/dr=2q cosh^2u and u_rr=8q^2 sinh u cosh^3u.
Volume measure is C q^-3 tanh^2u sech^2u du, while r<=1/(2q).
Any weight in (4) is bounded here by
C N^(k/4) exp(Ksqrt(N)/4+L N^1/4/2).
The resulting integral is bounded by a polynomial in N times that last
subexponential factor times int_1^infinity (1+u)^k
 exp(Cu-delta N u^2)du. It is O(exp(-cN)), for sufficiently large N.
Finally (3) converges on a fixed ball to e^(-4r^4/3-Mr^2); its normalizer
is bounded above and below by positive constants. This proves (4).
At the boundary the log-Gaussian factor beats every power of distance:
f_N and all fixed-order derivatives extend by zero smoothly.
These facts justify integrations by parts and the generator domain below.

3. POSITIVE PROPAGATION AND A STRONG L1 DUHAMEL ESTIMATE

For an initial product branch x, the mass of Q_t delta_x is
 R_t(x)=Tr e^(tL) k_N(x)=Tr k_N(x)e^(tL)I.
The equality uses Hilbert-Schmidt selfadjointness of L.
The central supersolution from the baseline is
 e^(tL)I<=exp[aJ^2+K0], a=(2M+1)/s^2, K0=B^2/4, 0<=t<=1.
It follows by H<=aJ^2+K0 and CP Duhamel comparison; the central
curve has nonnegative residual, so no operator-monotonicity of exp is used.
Gaussian heat integration and Hoeffding imply
 Tr tau_m^tensor N e^(aJ^2)
 <=e^-a/4 d^-3/2 exp[a N^2|m|^2/(4d)],
 d=1-a(N+1)/2.
For large N, d>=1/2, and |m|=2q|x|, so uniformly
 R_t(x)<=2^1.5 e^(B^2/4) exp[2(2M+1)|x|^2].              (5)
By positivity, every signed density z therefore satisfies
 ||Q_t z||_1<=C int e^(K1|x|^2)|z(x)|dx.                  (6)

Set v_t=e^(th)f_N. The ordinary semigroup Duhamel identity is
 Q_1 f_N-v_1=int_0^1 Q_(1-t)(G_N^*-h)v_t dt.             (7)
For fixed N, smooth extension by zero, tangent flows and bounded polynomial
coefficients on its compact ball put these v_t in the generator domain.
Alternatively (7) follows by differentiating Q_(1-t)v_t and integrating;
(4)-(6) justify the absolute integrations. No uniform spectral gap is assumed.

Derivatives of h are linear/constant and h<=2M r^2+Br.
Equations (2), (4) show
 int e^(K1 r^2)|(G_N^*-h)e^(th)f_N|dx<=C_(M,B)epsilon
uniformly 0<=t<=1. Combining with (6)-(7) proves
 ||Q_1 f_N-e^h f_N||_1<=C_(M,B)N^-1/2.                  (8)
The mass mu=int Q_1 f_N is >=1: each determinant-one filter g has
g*g=e^(v.sigma), and the rotation-invariant seed satisfies
Tr rho_seed e^(2v.J)>=Tr rho_seed by paired magnetic weights.
The mass of e^h f_N is bounded below because h>=-Br and (4) includes
tightness on a fixed ball. Normalization in (8) therefore preserves O(epsilon).
Integration against k_N is trace-distance contractive, so (8) bounds the
whole quantum output, rather than only collective moments.

4. IDENTIFY THE DESIRED LIMIT LAW WITHOUT A PARAMETER ESTIMATE

The baseline scalar-prior proof gives, for any fixed K,L,
 int e^(Kr^2+Lr)|q_N,M-q_inf,M|dy=O_(M,K,L)(epsilon),
 q_inf,M proportional to exp(-4r^4/3-Mr^2).
This can also be obtained directly by the sixth-order logcosh expansion,
quartic interior envelope and exponentially small exterior described above.

Pull e^h f_N back by F_N: it is q_N,M(y)e^(h(F_N(y))).
Since tanh z<=z and z-tanh z<=z^3/3 globally,
 |F_N(y)-y|<=(4/3)epsilon |y|^3,
 |h(F_N(y))-h(y)|
 <=epsilon[(16M/3)|y|^4+(4B/3)|y|^3].                   (9)
Both h values are <=2Mr^2+Br. Weighted prior control, (9) and
|e^a-e^b|<=|a-b|(e^a+e^b) show that this unnormalized law differs
in L1 by O(epsilon) from q_inf,M(y)e^(h(y)).
The latter, after normalization, is exactly p_(A,b).
Normalizers are uniformly positive at fixed M,B. Pushing forward by F_N
and integrating against k_N proves
 T(sigma,S_N p_(A,b))=O_(M,B)(epsilon).
Adding the baseline O(N^-3/2) proximity yields
 sup_(||A||<=M,|b|<=B) T(rho_N(A,b),S_N p_(A,b))
      =O_(M,B)(N^-1/2).                                (10)
The decoder exists at every N; the finitely many N not satisfying the heat/
denominator thresholds can be included by enlarging the fixed constant and
using T<=1. No growing-M,B uniformity is claimed.

5. SHARP ASYMPTOTICS FOR THIS DECODER AT THE ISOTROPIC POINT

At A=b=0, the exact heat identity gives rho_N=S_N q_N,0, without approximation.
Write p0(y) proportional to e^(-4r^4/3). Taylor expansion gives in L1
 sqrt(N)(q_N,0-p0) -> p0(y)(H1(r)-mu1),
 H1(r)=(2/3)r^2+(64/45)r^6,
 mu1=E_p0 H1=2 E_p0 r^2
     =2(4/3)^-1/2 Gamma(5/4)/Gamma(3/4).                 (11)
The first contribution is log sinch(2qr)=2epsilon r^2/3+O(epsilon^2 r^4);
the second is Nlogcosh(2qr)-2sqrt(N)r^2
=-4r^4/3+(64/45)epsilon r^6+O(epsilon^2 r^8).
Uniform scaled L1 convergence follows by the same interior Taylor envelope
and exterior tail split. E r^6=(15/16)E r^2 proves the stated mean.

H1 is strictly increasing for r>0, so H1(r)-mu1 has one zero r_*.
Sector measurement J^2 recovers the sign asymptotically. Conditional on an
input branch with direction u, J^2 and J_u commute and
 E(J^2-J_u^2)=N/2,
 Var(J_u)=N(1-|m|^2)/4<=N/4,
 E J_u/s=[N^1/4/2]tanh(2qr) ->r.
Because j>=|J_u| and (j-|J_u|)^2<=j(j+1)-J_u^2,
 E[(j/s-|J_u|/s)^2]<=1/(2sqrt(N)).
Therefore j/s->r in probability, for every fixed r and direction.
The actual distinguishing measurement is just the PARAMETER-INDEPENDENT
event j/s>r_*, so it does not use the unknown direction.

Trace-norm contractivity gives limsup sqrt(N)T<=E|H1-mu1|/2.
Applying that event to (11) and dominated convergence gives liminf equal
to int_(r>r_*) p0(H1-mu1)=E|H1-mu1|/2.
Thus the exact full-state asymptotic is
 T(rho_N(0,0),S_N p0)=c_iso/sqrt(N)+o(N^-1/2),
 c_iso=(1/2)E_p0|H1(r)-mu1|>0.                           (12)
This disproves o(N^-1/2) for this unchanged decoder. It does not disprove
other N-dependent decoders or establish an optimal operational deficiency.

6. SEPARABILITY BOUNDARIES AND NEGATIVE RESULTS

For A=aI, set t=2/N+a/N^1.5 and v=b/N^.75. If t>0,
 e^K=e^(v.J/2)e^(tJ^2)e^(v.J/2)
     =int [e^(v.sigma/4)e^(y.sigma/2)e^(v.sigma/4)]^tensor N nu_t(dy).
All integrands are positive identical-qubit products. Normalize with their
explicit trace weights. At t=0, e^K=e^(v.J) is one product. Hence exact
separability holds for all scalar A at t>=0. The claim includes negative a
when t remains nonnegative. At t<0 this positive heat argument is unavailable;
no extension is asserted.

The general baseline O(N^-3/2) bound passes the reconstructed Duhamel audit,
