STRONGEST CLAIM AND SUPPLIED INTERFACE
Known rational a,b with |a|<=1, |b|<=1/2, N=2, and
 A=diag(a,-a,0), field vector=(0,0,b).
Use the baseline critical-spin Gibbs model
 K=2J^2/N+Q_A(J)/N^(3/2)+b J_z/N^(3/4), J_i=sum sigma_i/2.
This entire two-qubit density has an explicit seven-branch convex decomposition
into IDENTICAL local product densities. For ab!=0 the anisotropy and field do
not commute. The delivered Python compiler acquires all branch weights through
exact rational outward intervals, rounds to an exactly normalized dyadic law,
selects that law with finite fair bits, and returns local state/gate descriptions.
For a=1/4,b=1/8, requested trace error 2^-32, its certified mixture error is
 22266063/18446744073709551616 < 1/4294967296.
A separately evaluated 4-by-4 matrix-exponential interval certificate gives
 12916911/9223372036854775808 < 1/4294967296.
Both are rational inequalities checked without floating-point decisions.
The finite recorded sample chooses +X and returns H on each initialized qubit.
No physical device has been used. The selected deterministic transcript is not
evidence of random-source fairness. Under an ideal fair-bit source the sampler's
AVERAGED output density has the certified error; a single pure branch does not.

COMPLETE EXACT DERIVATION
Write XX=sigma_x tensor sigma_x, etc. Direct Pauli multiplication gives
 J^2=3I/2+(XX+YY+ZZ)/2,
 J_x^2-J_y^2=(XX-YY)/2,
 J_z=(ZI+IZ)/2.
The scalar 3I/2 cancels on Gibbs normalization. Set
 u=a/(2sqrt(2)), v=b/2^(3/4), d=sqrt(u^2+v^2).
The remaining exponent H has even-parity block, in (00,11),
 H_even=I/2+u X+v Z,
and odd-parity block, in (01,10),
 H_odd=-I/2+X.
Thus, with E=exp(1/2), O=exp(-1/2), S(d)=sinh(d)/d, S(0)=1,
 exp(H_even)=E[cosh(d) I+S(d)(u X+v Z)],
 exp(H_odd)=O[cosh(1) I+sinh(1)X].
Let Z=2[E cosh(d)+O cosh(1)] and define
 m=2E v S(d)/Z,
 c_x=2[E u S(d)+O sinh(1)]/Z,
 c_y=2[O sinh(1)-E u S(d)]/Z,
 c_z=2[E cosh(d)-O cosh(1)]/Z.
Their only nonzero Pauli moments are mean_z=m at each qubit and the diagonal
correlations c_x,c_y,c_z. In particular
 rho=1/4[I+m(ZI+IZ)+c_x XX+c_y YY+c_z ZZ].

For r in {+/-e_x,+/-e_y,+/-e_z,0}, put tau_r=(I+r.sigma)/2.
The seven weights in this order are
 (c_x/2,c_x/2,c_y/2,c_y/2,(c_z+m)/2,(c_z-m)/2,1-c_x-c_y-c_z).
Expansion of tau_r tensor tau_r gives exactly the preceding Pauli moments.
The last weight is equivalently 4O[cosh(1)-sinh(1)]/Z>0. Therefore they sum
exactly to one; it remains to prove all six other weights nonnegative.

For the admitted rational rectangle,
 |u|<=1/(2sqrt(2))<3/8,
 |v|<=1/(2*2^(3/4))<3/10,
 d^2<=1/8+1/(4*2^(3/2))<1/4.
The second strict bound follows on squaring from 8>625/81.
For 0<=d<=1/2, S(d)<=S(1/2)<21/20. To verify the last inequality, its first
nonconstant positive series term is 1/24, and each subsequent term/previous
term is <=1/80. Their sum is <=(1/24)/(1-1/80)=10/237<1/20.
Also exp(2)>=1+2+2+4/3+2/3=7, hence
 exp(-1)sinh(1)=(1-exp(-2))/2>=3/7,
 exp(-1)cosh(1)=(1+exp(-2))/2<=4/7.
Consequently
 |u| S(d)<63/160<3/7,
 cosh(d)-|v| S(d)>=1-(3/10)(21/20)=137/200>4/7.
These are precisely the required inequalities c_x,c_y>0 and c_z>|m|.
All seven weights are strictly positive, uniformly over this rectangle.

Noncommutation is explicit: the even-block anisotropy uX and field vZ have
commutator -2iuvY, nonzero whenever ab!=0. This is a small admitted quantum
instance, not a commuting shortcut or a claim about all anisotropies/fields.

FINITE-BIT IMPLEMENTATION AND PROOF OF ITS CERTIFICATE
Artifact: work/cycle6/c07_s03/certified_two_spin.py.
Every interval endpoint is a rational dyadic, rounded outward after operations.
Working precision P=k+32; executed k=32, P=64. Roots use integer isqrt on
scaled rational endpoints. exp(x) range reduces to |x|<=1 and uses exact Taylor
partial sums with remainder <=3/(n+1)!; for negative x it uses a certified
reciprocal. cosh and S use positive even series, with omitted tails bounded by
3/(2n+2)! and 3/(2n+3)! respectively. This handles d=0 without division by d.
Internal series tolerance is 2^-(P+8). All denominator intervals must exclude
zero. The output checks strict positivity of all seven exact weight enclosures.
The 32 guard bits are a chosen finite budget. Successful output is certified by
the final rational inequalities, not by a claim that 32 bits always suffice;
a guard failure raises an exception rather than silently emitting a certificate.

Let W=k+8, D=2^W. Round the midpoints of the first six weight intervals downward
to n_i/D, and put n_7=D-sum_(i<=6)n_i. Check n_i>=0 and sum n_i=D exactly.
Let e_i=max(|n_i/D-lo_i|,|n_i/D-hi_i|). Since every exact weight lies in its
enclosure, the total variation between the two categorical laws is <=sum e_i/2.
Convexity and the unit trace norm of each product density give
 T(rho, sum_i(n_i/D) tau_i tensor tau_i)<=sum_i e_i/2.
The delivered program verifies this bound <=2^-k using rational comparisons.
Thus no exact-real root, Gaussian, coefficient, normalization or selection oracle
is hidden in the executed compilation. No rare physical filter is performed.

Selection draws EXACTLY W fair bits for an integer in [0,D); cumulative integer
weights implement the dyadic law. A selected Pauli eigenstate uses at most two
ideal Clifford gates on each initialized qubit. A selected tau_0=I/2 uses two
additional INDEPENDENT fair bits to choose +/-Z at the two qubits; averaging those
bits gives tau_0 tensor tau_0. Sharing one such bit would instead give a correlated
state and is not the implementation. At most W+2 bits and four one-qubit gates
are needed per output. Initialization/reset and physical gate noise remain explicit
device interfaces; no actual reset cost, energy or hardware runtime is supplied.

The second certificate constructs a direct 4-by-4 Taylor enclosure of exp(H),
independent of the two-block exponential formula. The program verifies the
interval row-sum norm bound <=2, so the operator-series remainder is <=
9*2^(n+1)/(n+1)! (exp(2)<=9). Dividing by a certified positive trace encloses
the normalized Gibbs matrix. For delta a 4-by-4 Hermitian difference,
 T<=||delta||_F<=sum_(i,j) entrywise absolute upper bound.
This conservative rational bound is checked <=2^-k. It audits the exponentiation
and mixture reconstruction; the exact Pauli reduction above still needs proof,
and no finite execution establishes its general mathematical correctness.

c08_s01 04492258a38afabeed53d25b8e4530e9568a45186cab4295d13ec656176ca444
Source: outputs/round6/frozen_initials/c08_s01/INITIAL.txt
ROUND 6 INITIAL / c08_s01 / independent Sol construction
7 October 2026. Requested gpt-6.1-sol/max; observed model UNKNOWN.
Immutable after hash is recorded. Internal candidate proofs; external
correctness and historical novelty remain UNKNOWN. No breakthrough claimed.

STRONGEST RESULT

The entropy/power deficit controls the actual normal Lyapunov rate, its
nonhyperbolic volume fraction, an invariant-line strain template, and
finite-time stretching under a projective invariant measure. A connected
Sol family has deficit ->0 and Ricci curvature L2 ->infinity, refuting
deficit-only curvature/C2 stability even under simple bounded sustaining
forces. These extend the baseline pointwise strain deficit beyond equality.

INTERFACE

M is a smooth closed Riemannian 3-manifold, X a smooth nowhere-zero
divergence-free autonomous field, mu=dVol/V and phi_t its flow.
H=h_mu(phi_1)>0, natural logs per specified physical time unit.
S=Def X. The physical constitutive equation is
  nabla_X X-2nu div S+grad p=F, nu>0,
with total power W=integral <F,X>dVol=2nu integral |S|^2dVol.
Set Delta=W/(4nu V)-H^2=(1/2)E_mu|S|^2-H^2.
Put r=|X|, e=X/r, P=I-e tensor e, q=<Se,e>=X log r, b=PSe,
B=PSP on N=e-perp, B0=B+(q/2)I_N, eigenvalues +/-s.
The audited moving-normal cocycle Q_t=P_(phi_t x)Dphi_t|N_x has
  d/dt log|Q_t v|=<B xi_t,xi_t>, xi_t=Q_t v/|Q_t v|,
  |S|^2=2s^2+(3/2)q^2+2|b|^2.
Its exponents are chi,-chi, chi>=0, and the full spectrum is chi,0,-chi.
J=E_mu chi, beta=mu({chi>0}); Ruelle gives H<=J.

1. SHARP LYAPUNOV-RATE AND HYPERBOLIC-VOLUME STABILITY

  E_mu(chi-H)^2<=Delta.                                 (1)
  beta>=H^2/(H^2+Delta).                                (2)

Proof. The normal estimate gives chi<=sbar, the continuous-flow time
average of s. Jensen/Fatou, or conditional-expectation contraction,
give E chi^2<=E sbar^2<=E s^2<=H^2+Delta. Thus
  E(chi-H)^2<=Delta-2H(J-H)<=Delta.
The invariant A={chi>0} satisfies
  J=integral_A chi dmu<=integral_A s dmu.
This uses invariance and time averages; chi<=s is not asserted pointwise.
Cauchy on A gives H^2<=J^2<=beta integral_A s^2<=beta(H^2+Delta).

Both constants are sharp on disconnected closed manifolds: put a Sol
suspension with rate H/beta on a component of volume fraction beta and
a nonzero flat-torus Killing field on the remainder. Then chi=H/beta or
zero, aggregate entropy H, and Delta=H^2(1/beta-1)=E(chi-H)^2.
No connected sharpness claim for this mixture example is made.

2. EXACT INVARIANT PROJECTIVE STRAIN TEMPLATE

There exists a flow-invariant probability eta on the compact projective
normal bundle P(N), projecting to mu, whose restriction over A is the
graph of the normal unstable Oseledets line L+(x). Over A0={chi=0}, eta
may have nontrivial fiber measures. For ell at x define
  K(x,ell)=H(2Pi_ell-P_x).
Then
  integral|S-K|^2deta=2Delta-4H(J-H)<=2Delta.             (3)
Smooth-volume Pesin equality J=H makes (3) an exact identity. If chi>0
almost everywhere it is a base-space L2 bound with the actual normal
unstable line. This line is invariant for Q, not necessarily for Dphi
on X-perp; no smoothness, domination or acquisition algorithm is claimed.

Complete lift construction. The simple top Oseledets line on A is
invariant under every fixed time t, because Q_t intertwines the time-one
cocycle and Q_t and its inverse are bounded for fixed t. Its graph measure
eta_A is invariant. Over A0, start with Haar fiber directions above its
normalized invariant base measure and Cesaro-average under the continuous
projective flow. Compactness supplies a weak limit eta_0; its projection
remains the same base measure and it is invariant for all fixed times.
At each regular base point in A0 every nonzero normal vector has exponent
zero. For the bounded rate a(x,ell)=<B xi,xi>, invariance and the additive
log-norm identity give n integral a=integral log(|Q_n v|/|v|).
Divide by n; dominated convergence gives integral a d eta_0=0. The same
argument on eta_A gives the mean top exponent. Combine with weights beta
and 1-beta: integral a deta=J. Also integral q deta=E_mu X log r=0.
Since |K|^2=2H^2 and <S,K>=2H(a+q/2), expansion yields
  integral|S-K|^2=2(H^2+Delta)+2H^2-4HJ,
proving (3). This argument includes zero exponents and avoids a false
assumption of an invariant line on every zero-exponent fiber.

Let theta be the acute angle between ell and the top eigenline of B0
where s>0; define s sin^2theta=0 where s=0. The identity is equivalently
  Delta=E_mu(s-H)^2+(3/4)E_mu q^2+E_mu|b|^2
          +4H integral s sin^2theta deta+2H(J-H).       (4)
Thus the invariant-line angular loss is <=Delta/(4H). The baseline only
isolated Var(s), q and b. Coefficient 2 in (3) is sharp, including on the
connected warped Sol family below, which has J=H.

3. SHARP FINITE-TIME NORMAL STRETCHING STABILITY

Define Qhat_T=sqrt(r(phi_T x)/r(x))Q_T, whose normal absolute determinant
is one. For every T>0 and v spanning ell,
  integral[(1/T)log(|Qhat_T v|/|v|)-H]^2deta<=Delta.     (5)
For raw Q_T the corresponding bound is <=(4/3)Delta. These are eta-average
bounds, not uniform bounds on all orbits/vectors. Coefficient 1 in (5)
is sharp uniformly over T>0; raw coefficient 4/3 sharpness is not claimed.

Proof. The corrected instantaneous rate is ahat=<B0 xi,xi>. The traceless
normal 2x2 tensor B0-H(2Pi_ell-I_N) satisfies
  (ahat-H)^2<=(1/2)|B0-H(2Pi_ell-I_N)|^2.
Drop the nonnegative q,b components in (3): integral(ahat-H)^2<=Delta.
The finite-time corrected rate is the time average of ahat. Jensen and
eta invariance prove (5) for every T. For raw a-H=< (S-K)xi,xi>, use
the traceless 3x3 inequality <Rxi,xi>^2<=(2/3)|R|^2 and (3), then the
same Jensen argument. No convergence rate of Oseledets acquisition is used.

4. CONNECTED CURVATURE COUNTEREXAMPLE / SHARPNESS

Fix A=[[2,1],[1,1]], lambda=(3+sqrt(5))/2, a=log lambda and the baseline
Sol mapping torus, in its eigenbasis with the original transformed lattice,
quotient (v,t)~(Av,t-1). For integer k, omega=2pi k and 0<eps<a let
  f(t)=a t+(eps/omega)sin(omega t),
  g=e^(2f)dx^2+e^(-2f)dy^2+dt^2, X=partial_t.
The metric descends since f(t-1)=f(t)-a. Volume density is constant, X
has unit speed and preserves volume. The measured flow and volume measure
are the baseline cat-map suspension, hence H=a. Exact calculations give
  S=diag(f',-f',0), q=b=0, chi=a, Delta=eps^2/2,
  div S=-2(f')^2X, nabla_X X=0,
  Ric orthonormal=diag(-f'',f'',-2(f')^2).
The periodic pressure and constant-amplitude divergence-free force
  p=-(8nu a eps/omega)sin(omega t)
       -(nu eps^2/omega)sin(2omega t),
  F=4nu(a^2+eps^2/2)X
satisfy the physical stationary equation exactly, W=4nu V(a^2+eps^2/2).
Sectional curvatures are (f')^2, -f''-(f')^2, f''-(f')^2, and
  E_mu|Ric|^2=eps^2omega^2+4a^4+12a^2eps^2+(3/2)eps^4.
Taking eps=a/(2sqrt(k)) gives Delta=a^2/(8k)->0 and
  E_mu|Ric|^2=pi^2a^2 k+O(1)->infinity.
The manifold is fixed/connected, V unchanged, g->g_Sol in C1, strain and
F stay bounded, and the pressure gradient stays bounded and tends to zero.
Curvature divergence cannot be removed by coordinate changes. Thus no
deficit-only curvature or C2 estimate exists; weaker metric topology or
extra derivative/curvature hypotheses are not refuted.
For its top normal line the finite-time rate variance is exactly
  Delta [sin(omega T/2)/(omega T/2)]^2.
It tends to Delta as T->0, proving the sharp coefficient in (5).

STRONGEST RESULTS AND STATUS

1. INTERNAL DERIVATION: an exact projective strain-defect identity. Let M
be a smooth closed connected Riemannian 3-manifold, X a smooth nowhere-zero
divergence-free autonomous field, mu normalized volume, H=h_mu(phi_1)>0,
S=Def X, V=Vol(M), and W=2 nu integral |S|^2 dVol for the specified
strain-viscosity sustaining model. Define delta=W/(4 nu V)-H^2.
There is an invariant probability eta on the projectivized normal bundle
PN projecting to mu, for which, with lambda the volume-average top normal
Lyapunov exponent and P the normal projector,

 delta = (1/2) integral_PN |S-H(2 Pi_l-P)|^2 d eta
                         +2H(lambda-H).                         (1)

Here Pi_l is the orthogonal projector onto the transported normal line l.
Ruelle gives lambda>=H, so the first integral is at most 2 delta. This
quantifies strain proximity to a TRANSPORTED line distribution. It is not
closeness of the metric to a homogeneous metric, a smooth frame theorem
for near equality, or an acquired computational certificate.

2. EXACT COUNTEREXAMPLE: on the fixed Sol suspension of the cat map
[[2,1],[1,1]], there are smooth metrics g_n with the same volume form,
the same unit divergence-free X, and EXACTLY the same entropy H=a>0,
where a=log((3+sqrt(5))/2), such that

 delta_n=C n^(-10),   osc_M Scal(g_n)>=c n,
 g_n -> g_0 in C^1,   F_n -> 4 nu a^2 X uniformly.                (2)

C,c>0 are explicit fixed bump-integral/geometric constants. Therefore
near equality alone supplies no scalar-curvature-oscillation bound, even
with fixed M,V,H,|X| and bounded sustaining forces, and does not imply
uniform C^2 geometric rigidity. Scalar-curvature oscillation is invariant
under diffeomorphism. This refutes that precisely stated stronger route;
it does not refute weak measured stability, or stability with externally
supplied curvature/derivative compactness bounds.

3. EXACT PHYSICAL-INTERFACE OBSTRUCTION: a volume-preserving finite-volume
system cannot represent an infinite nonrepeating computation by disjoint
configuration cells with a positive-volume set of perpetually correct
initial states. For distinct configuration labels s_0,...,s_N and disjoint
measurable cells C_s, let A_N be the initial states correctly following all
N+1 labels. Then

                    mu(A_N)<=1/(N+1).                         (3)

In particular a fixed input cell of volume alpha has conditional success
at most min(1,1/[alpha(N+1)]). Positive-volume finite-horizon robustness
does not by itself supply positive entropy, or a positive work per
instruction. This is a recurrence/volume obstruction, not a complexity
lower bound, and does not cover changing cells, overlapping codes, hidden
environment registers, or variable-volume/noninvertible apparatus.

The baseline equality derivation survived the adversarial checks below.
Historical priority of (1), (2), or their combination remains UNKNOWN.
These are candidate internal results, not external verification or a
field-breaking claim. The main broad scientific objective remains open.

Read work/cycle6/baseline/00_START_HERE.txt and 01_CORE.txt at pinned Astra
37a01798e92a0763e64a4649ae00024079d2a266, plus NEW_FRONTIER.txt,

The baseline is the smooth steady closed-manifold normalized-volume
inequality W>=4 nu V H^2 and its exact equality frame. Its force law is
 nabla_X X - 2 nu div S + grad p = F.
W is force work per unit density, not thermodynamic entropy production or
digital instruction cost. The supplied g and X determine continuum
derivatives, flow and entropy; neither entropy acquisition nor actuation
nor a finite-precision interface is provided by this input.

I. FULL DERIVATION OF THE PROJECTIVE IDENTITY

Write r=|X|, e=X/r and N=e^perp, P=I-e tensor e. The quotient-normal
cocycle is Q_t=P_(phi_t x) D phi_t|N_x. It is invertible and composes
because D phi_t maps the X-line onto itself. If w=Q_t v, the moving-normal
calculation gives P nabla_X w=P nabla_w X. Consequently

 d/dt log|w| = <B w,w>/|w|^2,   B=P S P|N.                     (4)

This retains the moving-normal connection; no frozen ambient plane is
substituted. Put q=<S e,e>=X log r and b=P S e. Since div X=0,
tr B=-q. Set B_0=B+(q/2)I_N, with eigenvalues +/-s, s>=0. Direct block
algebra gives

 |S|^2=2s^2+(3/2)q^2+2|b|^2.                                  (5)

All one-step full derivative and inverse norms are bounded on compact M;
the same holds for Q_1 and Q_1^(-1). Since r is bounded above and below,
the X-line exponent is zero, and |det Q_t|=r(x)/r(phi_t x) has sublinear
logarithm. Oseledets thus gives full exponents chi,0,-chi, chi>=0, and

 lambda=integral_M chi dmu,
 lim_(T->infinity) (1/T) integral_M log||Q_T|| dmu=lambda.         (6)

The continuous-time limit follows from integer time and the uniformly
bounded derivative for the remaining time in [0,1]. Dominated convergence
uses |log||Q_T||/T| bounded for T>=1. Ruelle's Theorem2(b), applied to
phi_1 and mu, supplies H<=lambda. No Pesin equality is required.

Here is a construction of the invariant lift eta, avoiding an assumed
measurable invariant unstable line on neutral components. Let nu_0 have
base marginal mu and, in each fiber PN_x, the uniform angular measure
induced by g. Let Phi_t be the projective flow of Q_t. Define

 eta_T=(1/T) integral_0^T (Phi_t)_* nu_0 dt.

Each eta_T projects to mu. PN is compact. Any weak convergent subsequence
T_j->infinity has a probability limit eta, still with marginal mu.
For each fixed u, the total variation norm of
(Phi_u)_* eta_T-eta_T is at most 2|u|/T, so eta is Phi_u-invariant.

Let A(x,l)=<S v,v> for any unit v in l. It is continuous on PN, and (4)
gives

 integral A d eta_T=(1/T) integral_PN log|Q_T v| dnu_0.

For the two singular values alpha>=beta>0 of Q_T at a fixed x, angular
averaging gives
 log alpha-log 2 <= E_angle log|Q_T v| <=log alpha.
Indeed the lower bound follows from |Q_T v|>=alpha |cos(theta)| and
E log|cos(theta)|=-log 2. Thus (6) implies integral A d eta=lambda.
The construction is an EXISTENCE limit, with no uniform rate or acquired
projective sampler asserted.

Because q=X log r and mu is invariant, integral q dmu=0. Define the
trace-free ideal strain J_l=H(2 Pi_l-P), whose eigenvalues are 0,H,-H.
Its norm squared is 2H^2. The block decomposition gives

 <S,J_l>=H(2<S v,v>-tr B)=H(2A+q).

Integrating over eta yields integral <S,J_l>=2H lambda. Expansion of the
squared difference therefore gives

 (1/2) integral |S-J_l|^2 d eta
 = (1/2) integral |S|^2 dmu+H^2-2H lambda
 = delta-2H(lambda-H),

which proves (1). The stationary work identity W=2 nu integral |S|^2 is
obtained by pairing the force law with X; advection and pressure integrate
to zero, and integration by parts gives the positive viscous term.

If theta is the angle of l with the +s eigenline of B_0, the same identity
has pointwise integrand

 (1/2)|S-J_l|^2=(s-H)^2+4Hs sin^2(theta)+(3/4)q^2+|b|^2.          (7)

Thus the strain-amplitude and transported-line errors share ONE deficit.
For t=s/H>=0 and z=sin^2(theta) in [0,1],
 (t-1)^2+4tz>=z:
if 4t>=1 subtracting z is nonnegative; if 4t<1, its minimum in z is at
z=1 and equals t^2+2t. Hence eta-average sin^2(theta)<=delta/H^2, with
an arbitrary convention where s=0. This controls an invariant LINE
DISTRIBUTION, not a differentiable eigenframe or its Lie brackets.

Exact equality delta=0 forces eta to lie on the smooth +H strain
eigenline, q=b=0 and s=H everywhere. Full-support base measure and
continuity then make the +H line invariant everywhere; the orthogonal
-H line is invariant by the pairing ODE. Their growth is exactly e^(+/-Ht).
This independently repairs/checks the projective saturation step without
using a measurable Oseledets line or ergodicity.

II. FULL GEOMETRIC COUNTEREXAMPLE

Let A=[[2,1],[1,1]], lambda_A=(3+sqrt(5))/2 and a=log lambda_A. In an
oriented orthonormal eigenbasis, take the rotated lattice Lambda of
covolume one. On (R^2/Lambda) x R quotient by
(x,y,t)->(lambda_A x,lambda_A^(-1)y,t-1), define global coforms
 alpha=e^(at) dx, beta=e^(-at) dy, gamma=dt.
The metric g_0=alpha^2+beta^2+gamma^2 and X=partial_t descend. Volume is
dx dy dt, V=1, X has unit norm, and phi_1 is the cat automorphism times
the retained t-coordinate. The Haar entropy is H=a. This is the
baseline's exact cat-suspension entropy computation; no ergodicity of the
time-one map is assumed or needed.

For ANY global smooth scalar f, set
 g_f=e^(2f) alpha^2+e^(-2f) beta^2+gamma^2.                       (8)
Its volume form is EXACTLY unchanged, so X is still divergence-free,
the measured dynamical system and H are unchanged, and |X|=1. In a
local product chart put u=at+f, so
 g_f=diag(e^(2u),e^(-2u),1).
The strain endomorphism has diagonal entries u_t,-u_t,0. Hence

 W_f/(4 nu V)=integral_M (a+Xf)^2 dmu
            =a^2+integral_M (Xf)^2 dmu,
 delta_f=integral_M (Xf)^2 dmu.                                 (9)

The cross term vanishes because integral Xf dmu=0. This is exact for
every f, including large transverse derivatives. Entropy does not need
to be recomputed for each metric.

For a smooth explicit bump define rho(z)=exp(1-1/(1-z^2)) when |z|<1
and 0 otherwise, and psi(xi,eta)=xi^2 rho(xi)rho(eta). Then
psi(0,0)=0, dpsi(0,0)=0, psi_xixi(0,0)=2, psi_etaeta(0,0)=0.
Choose a small fixed t interval strictly inside a fundamental period,
center t_0 and fixed L>0, and put chi(t)=rho((t-t_0)/L). Choose a fixed
transverse product chart centered at (x_0,y_0). For sufficiently large
integer n set epsilon_n=n^(-2), amplitude_n=n^(-3), and

 f_n=amplitude_n chi(t)
      psi((x-x_0)/epsilon_n,(y-y_0)/epsilon_n),

extended by zero outside the chart. All f_n are C-infinity. Equation(9)
and change of variables give EXACTLY

 delta_n=C n^(-10),
 C=[integral chi'(t)^2 dt][integral_R2 psi(xi,eta)^2 dxi deta]>0.

For a general u(x,y,t), direct curvature calculation gives

 Scal=2e^(-2u)(u_xx-2u_x^2)
      -2e^(2u)(u_yy+2u_y^2)-2u_t^2.                            (10)

At the center p=(x_0,y_0,t_0), f_n=df_n=0 and f_n,xx=2n, so
 Scal(g_n)(p)=-2a^2+4e^(-2at_0)n.
Outside the bump support Scal=-2a^2. This proves the oscillation claim.
Moreover sup|f_n|=O(n^-3), sup|df_n|=O(n^-1), so g_n->g_0 in C^1.

The divergence of S, as a coordinate vector, is
 ( e^(-2u)(u_tx-2u_t u_x),
  -e^(2u)(u_ty+2u_t u_y), -2u_t^2 ).                           (11)
X is geodesic for (8), because g_tt=1 and g_it=0. Thus p=0 and
F_n=-2 nu div S sustain the field; (11) yields
F_n-4 nu a^2 X=O(n^-1) uniformly. Second transverse derivatives of f
do not enter this force. The counterexample therefore survives a
uniformly close and bounded continuum-force interface. It does not
include a digital force compiler or charged actuator resolution.

No claim is made that g_n is far from g_0 in C^0, measured geometry, or
Gromov-Hausdorff distance: it actually converges in C^1. Its purpose is
the exact failure of derivative/curvature conclusions from the deficit.

III. FULL POSITIVE-VOLUME CONFIGURATION OBSTRUCTION

Let T be an invertible probability-measure-preserving transformation.
Let C_s be pairwise disjoint measurable decoding cells. Suppose a
specified computation has distinct labels s_0,...,s_N. Define
 A_N=intersection_(j=0)^N T^(-j) C_(s_j).
Then T^j A_N lies inside C_(s_j); these N+1 translates are disjoint and
each has measure mu(A_N). Their union lies in the unit-volume space, so
(N+1)mu(A_N)<=1. This proves (3). The infinite-run set is contained in
every A_N, hence has measure zero by letting N->infinity. For initial
states uniform on C_(s_0) of mass alpha, divide (3) by alpha. The bound
is sharp in the finite-horizon abstract interface: a cyclic permutation
of N+1 equal-volume cells has mu(A_N)=1/(N+1).

Complete configuration labels are essential. Outputs alone may repeat
while hidden state changes. For a physical apparatus with an environment,
the transformation, measure and cell capacity must include that
environment; ignoring it can invalidate the finite-volume hypothesis.
For continuous flows the same argument applies at a fixed clock step.

IV. PRIOR ART, EQUALITY AUDIT AND LIMITS

The baseline moving-normal cancellation, zero central exponent, determinant
endpoint, normal strain algebra and stationary work sign survive direct
reconstruction. Equality's invariant +H line also follows from (1). The
rest of its frame argument survives: q=b=0 gives normal invariance,
pairing makes the -H line invariant, exponential expansion/contraction
annihilate derivatives of every first integral, hence r is constant;
orienting the line bundles on a finite cover gives unit U_+,U_- with
[X,U_+]=-H U_+, [X,U_-]=H U_-. Jacobi and boundedness force
[U_+,U_-]=cX with constant c. This is the claimed local Sol/sl2 frame.

Ghys1987 is decisive prior-art context, but does NOT by itself prove the
same metric equality assertion. Its introduction and Theorem3.1 were
read in the primary original. Smooth stable and unstable line bundles in
3D can have exotic time parametrizations: the paper supplies
(SL(2,R)xR,SL(2,R))-structures, and smooth orbit equivalence to algebraic
flows outside the suspension case. Therefore one must retain the
EXACT exponential rate and constant metric bracket information rather
than conclude algebraicity merely from smooth foliations. The baseline
proof uses that additional information. No contradiction was found.
Benoist-Foulon-Labourie1990 is further contact-Anosov context; only the
primary introduction/statement was inspected, not its full proof.

Primary sources actually inspected:
 [Ruelle1978] Theorems1 and2(b) plus its five-page proof, cached original
 work/cycle1/pde_transport_sources/Ruelle1978.txt;
 https://www.ihes.fr/~ruelle/PUBLICATIONS/%5B51%5D.pdf

 https://www.numdam.org/item/10.24033/asens.1532.pdf
 [Benoist-Foulon-Labourie1990] primary abstract/introduction/statement:
 https://www.numdam.org/item/AIHPA_1990__53_4_395_0.pdf

Primary searches also located Hurder-Katok1990 and projective-Lyapunov
works; those results were not imported. The projective variational
construction above is fully supplied rather than relying on a random-
product theorem with a different base interface. Limited source search
does not establish novelty. No full historical search of the sharp
coefficient, defect identity, or localized Sol deformations is claimed.

STRONGEST CLAIMS

1. For the admitted smooth steady incompressible closed three-manifold
interface, the transverse strain and its OBJECTIVE RELATIVE ROTATION admit
a finite, noise-budgeted sufficient certificate of positive volume entropy.
If the transverse strain magnitude s has s>=s0>Omega>=|omega| everywhere,
then

  sqrt(s0^2-Omega^2) <= H <= E_mu s <= sqrt(W/(4 nu V)).       (A)

More precisely, put alpha=Omega/(s0+sqrt(s0^2-Omega^2)) when Omega>0.
Then

  H >= E_mu s-alpha E_mu |omega|,
  0 <= E_mu s-H <= Omega^2/(s0+sqrt(s0^2-Omega^2)).           (B)

The mechanism is classical strain persistence / invariant cones, applied
to the exact moving normal quotient and combined with the imported smooth
volume entropy formula. The rigorously specified finite acquisition/noise
interface is supplied below. This does not prove global near-equality
rigidity, acquire unknown regularity bounds, or certify arbitrary flows.

2. On the SAME closed Sol manifold and metric as the baseline optimizer,
there is a complete exact family of nowhere-zero divergence-free fields
Y=c E0+u E+ +v E- with

  H(Y)=a |c|,
  W(Y)=nu V a^2(4c^2+u^2+v^2),                            (C)

where a=log((3+sqrt(5))/2). For this entire homogeneous class,
s^2-omega^2=a^2 c^2. Thus the certificate is sharp there, including its
rotation threshold. In particular E0 and 2E+ have IDENTICAL pointwise
strain norms and integrated sustaining power but entropy a and zero.
Positive measured power alone has no positive entropy lower bound even
on one fixed manifold, metric, viscosity and positive-speed interface.

STATUS
Claims (A)--(C) are complete internal analytic derivations conditional only
on classical Oseledets/Birkhoff/Pesin facts and the baseline stationary
energy identity. They have not received an independent worker proof audit.
The exact verifier and homogeneous algebra have been executed. The generic
velocity-to-tensor interval compiler and measured apparatus are absent.
The next deeper attack is a finite-time C1 cone interface removing the
second-derivative acquisition requirement of the instantaneous certificate.

EXACT SCIENTIFIC INPUT

M is a smooth closed Riemannian three-manifold, g is supplied, V=Vol_g(M),
mu=dVol_g/V. X is smooth, autonomous, nowhere zero and div_g X=0. Its flow
is phi_t. H=h_mu(phi_1), natural logarithms and a fixed physical time unit.
Let r=|X|, e=X/r, P=I-e tensor e, N=e-perp, S=Def X. Define

  q=<Se,e>=X(log r),
  B=P S P|N, B0=B+(q/2)I_N,
  s=sqrt(tr_N B0^2/2), K=P(skew nabla X)P|N,
  D=nabla_X^N B0,
  R=[D,B0]/(4s^2), Omega_op=K-R,
  |omega|=||Omega_op||_op=||Omega_op||_HS/sqrt(2).             (1)

Here nabla^N_X is the induced metric connection on N and its endomorphisms.
The certificate admits only s>0; no eigenframe is chosen across a zero
eigenvalue gap. D contains second derivatives of X. Local eigenvector
signs change omega's sign, leaving |omega| invariant. All definitions are
intrinsic; an orientation of M or a global oriented eigenframe is unnecessary.

The physical model is the baseline strain-viscosity model
  nabla_X X-2 nu div S+grad p=F, nu>0,
  W=integral <F,X> dVol=2nu integral |S|^2 dVol.
F is a supplied or residual sustaining force, not an acquired actuator.

FULL DERIVATION OF THE STRAIN-PERSISTENCE CERTIFICATE

Step 1. The baseline moving normal cocycle is
  Q_t=P_(phi_t x) Dphi_t|N_x.
For an advected vector, its normal projection w solves
  nabla_X^N w=P nabla_w X.
This follows by decomposing the advected vector into w plus its flow-line
component: the normal moving-frame terms cancel because
nabla_X e=P nabla_e X. Q is a genuine cocycle since Dphi transports the
X-line to itself. Its normal symmetric generator is B, with tr B=-q.

Step 2. On any local strain eigenframe U+,U- with B0 U+=sU+ and
B0 U-=-sU-, let k=<nabla_X^N U+,U->. Differentiating this simple-eigenvalue
equation gives <D U+,U->=2sk. Hence R has matrix
  [[0,-k],[k,0]]
in this frame: explicitly [D,B0]/(4s^2) is exactly the eigenframe's angular
velocity, rather than the raw vorticity. Subtracting that frame rotation
from K gives the correct normal generator. With z=sqrt(r) times the
coordinates of w, X(log r)=q cancels the scalar -q/2 term, yielding

  d/dt [z1,z2]^T = [[s,-omega],[omega,-s]] [z1,z2]^T.         (2)

This includes both rotation of the normal plane and rotation of its strain
eigenlines. Merely measuring raw vorticity would omit R and is unsound.

Step 3. For z1!=0 put p=z2/z1. Direct differentiation yields
  p'=omega(1+p^2)-2sp.                                    (3)
For any constant 0<alpha<1, the cone |p|<=alpha is forward invariant if
  |omega|(1+alpha^2)<=2s alpha                             (4)
everywhere: at p=alpha the derivative is nonpositive, and at p=-alpha it
is nonnegative. Ordinary uniqueness/comparison for this scalar smooth ODE
gives nonescape. The cone can be defined globally by
||Pi- z||<=alpha ||Pi+ z||, so local eigenvector signs do not obstruct it.
Within the cone,
  d/dt log|z1|=s-omega p>=s-alpha |omega|.                 (5)
Starting on the positive eigenline at x gives, for every t>=0,
  log||Q_t(x)|| >= integral_0^t (s-alpha|omega|)(phi_tau x)d tau
                         +(1/2)log(r(x)/r(phi_t x)).       (6)
An initial cone boundary vector only adds a bounded norm factor. Since X
is nowhere zero on compact M, the endpoint term is uniformly bounded.

Step 4. Smooth volume preservation, the neutral flow line and the normal
determinant give the full Lyapunov spectrum chi,0,-chi. Oseledets applies
with bounded one-step derivatives and inverses. Apply discrete Birkhoff
to the bounded observable integral_0^1(s-alpha|omega|)(phi_tau x)d tau;
(6) then implies chi>=the time average of s-alpha|omega| almost everywhere.
For smooth invariant volume, the classical Pesin entropy formula for the
smooth time-one diffeomorphism gives H=E_mu chi. Therefore
  H>=E_mu(s-alpha|omega|).
The baseline estimate gives chi<=the time average of s and H<=E_mu s.

Step 5. If s>=s0>Omega>=|omega|, take
  alpha=Omega/(s0+sqrt(s0^2-Omega^2)) in (0,1).
Then Omega(1+alpha^2)=2s0 alpha and
s0-Omega alpha=sqrt(s0^2-Omega^2). Equations (A) and (B) follow. For
Omega=0, the positive strain eigenline is invariant for the normal quotient
and H=E_mu s directly. The universal upper power bound follows from
|S|^2=2s^2+(3/2)q^2+2|PSe|^2 and the energy identity. A constant generator
in (2) has exponents +/-sqrt(s^2-omega^2), so this uniform local-cocycle
constant cannot be improved under the given s0,Omega information.

FINITE ACQUISITION AND NOISE COSTS

Native admission data are a finite metric h-net {x_i}, verified coverage,
uniform Lipschitz bounds L_s,L_w for s and |omega|, and local evaluations
hat_s_i,hat_w_i with certified absolute errors eta_s,eta_w. These may come
from interval evaluation of an explicit finite smooth representation of
g,X. Raw observed values without uniform error/modulus data do not meet
the admission interface. Exact incompressibility is a separate premise.

Acquire the sufficient global enclosures
  s0=min_i hat_s_i-eta_s-L_s h,
  Omega=max_i |hat_w_i|+eta_w+L_w h.                        (7)
A rational verifier takes rational alpha in (0,1) and kappa>0 and checks
  Omega(1+alpha^2)<=2s0 alpha,
  kappa<=s0-Omega alpha.                                  (8)
If both pass, it certifies H>=kappa and W>=4nuV kappa^2. If they do not,
it returns UNKNOWN, not zero entropy. Using rational inequalities avoids
an uncertified square-root comparison. A positive margin permits a rational
alpha and kappa arbitrarily close to the sharp rate from below.

If the true margin gamma=inf s-sup|omega| is positive, choose the combined
measurement errors <=gamma/4 and (L_s+L_w)h<=gamma/4. For supplied bounded
three-dimensional atlas/metric geometry admitting N(h)<=C(1+h^-1)^3,
N=O(C(1+(L_s+L_w)/gamma)^3) local evaluations suffice. This is polynomial
in NUMERICAL inverse margin and Lipschitz bounds, not binary input alone.
Bit precision includes log(1/gamma), coefficient sizes and the cost of
constructing/validating the atlas, net, volume, regularity and tensor bounds.
Naive finite sums use O(N) fixed-dimensional tensor operations plus rational
arithmetic. No simulation time or asymptotic-entropy extrapolation is needed.

The higher-derivative burden is real: s uses first derivatives; omega uses
second derivatives, and a Lipschitz modulus for omega generally needs third
derivatives and a quantitative spectral gap and speed floor. Neither C1
velocity noise nor a finite set of strain/power samples provides these.
If native projected tensors B0,K,D are already enclosed with operator
errors eps_B,eps_K,eps_D, norm bounds S*,D*, and scalar error eps_s, with
both compared s values >=m>0, then commutator algebra gives a conservative
omega error
  eps_K+(eps_D S*+D* eps_B)/(2m^2)+D*S* eps_s/m^3.           (9)
The last term uses |s^-2-hat_s^-2|<=2 eps_s/m^3. Bundle/projection errors
must be folded into the primitive enclosures first; they are not free.

For power acquisition, suppose a certified volume partition has normalized
weights v_i, sum v_i=1, cells of radius <=h, sampled strain tensors S_i,
uniform Hilbert-Schmidt measurement error eta and a supplied strain
Lipschitz bound L. Put M2=sum v_i |S_i|^2 and delta=eta+Lh. Minkowski gives
  max(0,sqrt(M2)-delta)^2 <= E|S|^2 <= (sqrt(M2)+delta)^2.  (10)
Multiply by 2nuV for a sustaining-power interval and divide the upper
root by sqrt(2) for an entropy upper certificate. This power measurement
does not replace the dynamical lower certificate (7)--(8).

EXACT HOMOGENEOUS SOL CLASS AND POWER-ONLY OBSTRUCTION

Use the baseline cat mapping torus and its global orthonormal frame
  [E0,E+]=-aE+, [E0,E-]=aE-, [E+,E-]=0.
For any constant (c,u,v)!=(0,0,0), Y=cE0+uE++vE- is smooth, nowhere-zero,
constant-speed and divergence-free. Advected components in this frame
solve vdot=Gv with
  G=-ad_Y=[[0,0,0],[-au,ac,0],[av,0,-ac]].                 (11)
This follows directly from [Y,advected vector]=0. The orthonormal frame
makes component norms the true Riemannian norms, so the full Lyapunov
exponents are precisely the real parts of G's eigenvalues: ac,0,-ac.
When c=0, G^2=0 and its derivative growth is only polynomial. Pesin
therefore gives H=a|c|, including zero for c=0.

S=(G+G^T)/2 has
  |S|^2=2a^2c^2+(a^2/2)(u^2+v^2).                         (12)
Pairing the stationary residual force with Y gives (C). At the normalized
flow direction e=(c,u,v)/sqrt(c^2+u^2+v^2), q=0. With P=I-ee^T, the
projected generator is Q=PGP. Its symmetric/antisymmetric parts B,Keff
have
  s^2=a^2 c^2+a^2u^2v^2/(c^2+u^2+v^2),
  omega^2=a^2u^2v^2/(c^2+u^2+v^2).                        (13)
Thus (A) is exact for constant homogeneous data whenever c!=0. At c=0
with uv!=0 the threshold s=|omega| has zero entropy despite strictly
positive s. At c=0,u=2,v=0, s=0 but |S|^2=2a^2, exactly matching the
optimizer c=1,u=v=0. The complete pointwise dissipation density and total
power are identical although their entropy rates differ by a.

For v=0 and c!=0, omega=0, s=a|c| and H=E s, but the b cross-strain
term is a^2u^2/4. This shows that saturation of the intermediate entropy
estimate H<=E s alone does not force the full equality-frame rigidity.

c09_s01 5640d6bf1af8c136fdf39ec5f25eab3721dd48f35e2d8a33003dff96a07c95de

C09_S01 INITIAL: SCALAR OBSTRUCTION TO THE GENERIC COVARIANCE INFORMATION SUBSTITUTE
7 October 2026. Independent initial phase; no peer results read.
Origin: c09_s01; requested gpt-6.1-sol/max; observed model UNKNOWN.
Status: internally derived analytic counterexample; external review and exact
priority UNKNOWN. Does not solve/refute KLS or the fully constrained N75 route.

STRONGEST EXACT CLAIM

Bounded covariance, a bounded optimal Poincare constant, smooth Gaussian heat
regularization, small field mean, normalized field second moment, bounded field
Dirichlet energy, and EXACT scalar Sylvester/commutator identities do NOT imply
a universal cap-independent scale-integrated covariance-observation information
bound. This remains false with dimension one and the source's exact cap
f_L(q)=q/(1+q/L), covariance 1+f_L(T R(Y)^2), and dT/T^2 weighting.

Precisely, for every fixed heat time tau>=0 there are centered log-concave laws
eta_n and smooth positive scalar fields R_n such that
  Cov(eta_n)<=1+tau, P(eta_n)<=4+tau,
  E R_n^2=1, E |R_n'|^2<=1, E R_n->0,
  R_n'=H_n R_n, 0<=H_n<=1, [H_n,R_n]=0,
and for every fixed L>=256,
  liminf_(n->infinity) integral_0^infinity I(Y;Z_T) dT/T^2
       >= c_tau log(L/256),
where Z_T|Y ~ N(0,1+f_L(T R_n(Y)^2)) and c_tau>0 independent of n,L.
For tau=0 one may take c_0=Pr{|G|>=1}/128. For tau>0 one may take
  c_tau=(1-e^-1)e^(-tau/2) Pr{|G|>=1}/128.
Consequently no finite universal upper can follow from the displayed premises.

1. EXACT CONSTRUCTION

Let X_n have density e^-x/Z_n on [0,n+1], Z_n=1-e^(-(n+1)), n>=256.
Put V=sqrt(tau)G independently, W=X_n+V, Y=W-EW. Define
  h(u)=1/(1+e^-u), m_n=E h(W-n)^2,
  a_n=m_n^-1/2, R_n(y)=a_n h(y+EW-n).
The law of Y is centered and log-concave: the truncated exponential is
log-concave, as is its convolution with a Gaussian. For tau>0 its density is
smooth and strictly positive. R is smooth and positive even at tau=0.
The construction uses exact definitions, not an acquired density oracle or
an uncharged latent variable at the decoder. Z_T is a declared observation.

Since h'=h(1-h),
  E R^2=1, R'=(1-h)R, E(R')^2<=1.
This supplies scalar Sylvester R'=(H R+R H)/2, H=1-h, with bounded H and
zero commutators. Centering does not alter derivatives or information.

The base law has Var(X_n)=1-(n+1)^2 e^(-(n+1))/Z_n^2<=1 and
  P(X_n)=1/(1/4+pi^2/(n+1)^2)<=4.
For completeness, the selfadjoint generator is -f''+f' with Neumann boundary
conditions. Substitution f=e^(x/2)g gives -g''+(1/4)g=lambda g and Robin
conditions g'+g/2=0 at both ends. Positive eigenvalues are exactly
lambda_k=1/4+(k pi/(n+1))^2, k>=1; the remaining eigenvalue is zero.
The spectral expansion proves the formula. Conditional variance plus the base
and Gaussian Poincare inequalities gives P(W)<=P(X_n)+tau<=4+tau:
  Var f(X+V)=E_V Var_X f(X+V)+Var_V E_X f(X+V)
      <=(4+tau)E |f'(X+V)|^2.
Cov(W)<=1+tau follows by independence.

2. FIELD NORMALIZATION AND RARE MASS

For tau=0 an exact substitution u=x-n yields
  m_n=e^-n [h(1)-h(-n)]/Z_n,
  p_n=Pr(X_n>=n)=e^-n(1-e^-1)/Z_n,
so p_n a_n^2=(1-e^-1)/[h(1)-h(-n)]>=1/2.
Also E(R')^2 equals
  [(1-h(-n))^3-(1-h(1))^3]/[3(h(1)-h(-n))],
which is bounded by one. The small mean follows from
  E h(X_n-n)<=e^-n(n+1)/Z_n,
and m_n>=p_n/4, hence E R_n=O((n+1)e^-n/2)->0.

For fixed tau>0, extend the normalization integral from its actual interval
to the real line and substitute after conditioning on V:
  m_n<=e^-n E e^V/Z_n * integral_R e^-u h(u)^2 du
       =e^-n e^(tau/2)/Z_n.
The last integral is one because e^-u h(u)^2=h'(u).
The event X_n>=n,V>=0 is included in W>=n, giving
  p_n=Pr(W>=n)>=e^-n(1-e^-1)/(2Z_n),
  p_n a_n^2>= (1-e^-1)e^(-tau/2)/2.
Moreover m_n>=p_n/4, while
  E h(W-n)<=e^-n e^(tau/2)(n+1)/Z_n.
Thus E R_n->0 also after fixed Gaussian heating.

3. INFORMATION LOWER: A SINGLE EXPLICIT OUTPUT TEST

Write alpha=Pr(|G|>=1)>0. Fix the source cap L and use scales
  s=T a_n^2 in [256,min(n,L)].
Set A_s={|Z_T|>=sqrt(s)/(2sqrt(2))}, and let q_s=Pr(A_s).
For every y with W>=n, h(W-n)>=1/2. Since s<=L and h<=1,
  f_L(s h^2)>=s h^2/2>=s/8.
The conditional Gaussian variance is at least s/8. Therefore
  Pr(A_s|Y=y)>=alpha on W>=n.

On W<=n/2, h(W-n)<=e^-n/2, so the conditional variance is at most
1+s e^-n<=2. The elementary Gaussian Chernoff bound gives
  Pr(A_s|W<=n/2)<=2e^-s/32.
For tau=0,
  Pr(W>n/2)<=e^-n/2/Z_n<=2e^-n/2<=2e^-s/32.
For tau>0, conditioning on V gives the same bound with e^(tau/2):
  Pr(W>n/2)<=e^(tau/2)e^-n/2/Z_n.
For fixed tau and all sufficiently large n (e.g. n>=16tau/15), this is
at most 2e^-s/32 because s<=n. Consequently in both cases
  q_s<=4e^-s/32.

Mutual information is E_Y KL(P_(Z|Y)||P_Z). Applying relative-entropy data
processing to the binary output event A_s yields, for a_y=Pr(A_s|Y=y),
  KL(P_(Z|Y=y)||P_Z)>=d_bin(a_y||q_s)
       >=a_y log(1/q_s)-log 2.
On W>=n this is at least alpha(s/32-log 4)-log 2.
The expression is >=alpha s/64 for s>=256 (checked directly; alpha=erfc(1/sqrt2)).
The other conditional KL terms are nonnegative. Hence
  I(Y;Z_T)>=p_n alpha s/64.
No binary entropy of the rare input event is silently substituted for the
continuous input information, and no likelihood-ratio approximation is used.

Change variables s=T a_n^2; dT/T^2=a_n^2 ds/s^2. The selected scale interval
therefore gives
  integral I(Y;Z_T)dT/T^2
      >=p_n a_n^2 alpha/64 * log(min(n,L)/256).
The normalization bounds in section2 prove the exact claimed constants.
Take n->infinity with L fixed FIRST. The resulting lower grows as log L.
This matches the source's order of limits and rules out a cap-independent
universal constant under these premises.

4. WHAT THIS REFUTES, AND THE REMAINING KLS GATE

The exact target in family093 Section7 applies logarithmic Sobolev to every
observation posterior q_z(y), giving
  I(Y;Z_T)<= (1/(4lambda)) E sum_i tr(C_T^-1 D_i C_T C_T^-1 D_i C_T).
Near-commuting Sylvester identities then turn scale integration into <=9/4.
Our example demonstrates that the LSI entropy step cannot be replaced by a
generic inequality based only on covariance, Poincare, field L2 energy,
small mean, bounded Sylvester coefficients, or scalar commutation.
Gaussian heating of a compact log-concave input does not remove this failure.

It does NOT satisfy the hypothetical KLS anomaly sigma^2/P0->0: its ratio
tends to 1/4 before heating. Its logistic field also does NOT have sharp
Poincare-energy saturation E|DR|^2~P^-1 E R^2, nor is it constructed from
the complete source derivative hierarchy. Those stronger structural premises
remain a possible escape. N75's heat plateau and its covariance-only first-gap
construction are not refuted. The gap is narrowed: a viable replacement must
use the anomalous small covariance or additional hierarchy information in an
essential way; bounded covariance and near commutation alone do not suffice.

c09_s02 cae912f4896339ea5032f5a2c2e98cc9fd454664bd2e8174fd18d8c408138bab
Source: outputs/round6/frozen_initials/c09_s02/INITIAL.txt
C09_S02 INDEPENDENT INITIAL: ANALYTIC GAUSSIAN INFORMATION OBSTRUCTION
Worker requested gpt-6.1-sol/max; observed model UNKNOWN.
7 October 2026. Independent of every other cycle6 worker. No peer reads.
Internal derivations, not external proof/priority validation.

STRONGEST CLAIM

There is NO universal C for

  I(Y;Z) <= C P(mu) E_mu[J_q(Y)]                              (1)

over smooth centered log-concave references mu with bounded covariance,
even when mu is a Gaussian convolution of a compact log-concave density,
and q(z|y) is a SINGLE centered one-dimensional Gaussian with an analytic
variance field A(y)>=1. Here P(mu) is the Poincare constant and
J_q(y)=int q(z|y)|partial_y log q(z|y)|^2 dz is the channel Fisher information.

An explicit family has Cov(mu)<=2, P(mu)<=5, yet

  I(Y;Z)/(P(mu) E[J_q(Y)]) >= c R/(log R)^2 -> infinity.        (2)

The information and energy are both very small, of orders bounded below
and above respectively by R exp(-R) and exp(-R)(log R)^2. R is numerical.
This rules out replacing observation-likelihood log-Sobolev control by
Poincare with a universal constant, including for Gaussian covariance
observations. It does NOT refute a bound using extra constraints on the
particular N75 eigenfunction-derived matrix field, or solve/refute KLS.

A second explicit construction gives analytic Gaussian experiment families
on the SAME R^(2q), parameter interval [0,1], and noise covariance I. Their
FULL Fisher information is identically 1 at EVERY parameter, but the optimal
unassisted classical/quantum archive prelogs are 1/2 and q. All q are fixed
before epsilon tends to zero. The Gaussian affine-dimension memory principle
is already present in baseline M/N07; this construction specializes it into
an everywhere-equal-Fisher counterexample. It is not a new general principle.

1. ANALYTIC VARIANCE CHANNEL: COMPLETE DERIVATION

Let R>=4096. Let X have density exp(-x)/Z on [0,2R], where
Z=1-exp(-2R), and let G~N(0,1) be independent. Set Y=X+G.
For a centered reference replace Y by Y-E[X] and compose every field below
with that known translation. Translation preserves every displayed bound.

The truncated exponential is log-concave. Gaussian convolution preserves
log-concavity. Its density is positive and real analytic on the whole line:

 rho_R(y)= exp(-y+1/2)/Z
   * [Phi(2R-y+1)-Phi(1-y)].                                (3)

This follows by completing the square in int_0^(2R) exp(-x)phi(y-x)dx.
In particular rho_R(y)<=exp(-y+1/2)/Z. On [R,R+1], the bracket is at least
1/2 for R>=4, by Gaussian Chernoff bounds.

For truncated exponential on [0,L], direct moment integration gives

 Var(X)=1-L^2 exp(-L)/(1-exp(-L))^2 <=1.                     (4)

The exact Neumann spectral gap is 1/4+pi^2/L^2, so
P_X=(1/4+pi^2/L^2)^(-1)<4. To verify the spectral assertion, the weighted
generator is f''-f'. Write f=exp(x/2)u. The eigenproblem becomes
-u''+u/4=lambda u with u'+u/2=0 at 0,L. For lambda>1/4, putting
k=sqrt(lambda-1/4) gives u=a cos(kx)-a sin(kx)/(2k); the right boundary
requires sin(kL)=0. For 0<lambda<1/4, the corresponding hyperbolic solution
requires sqrt(1/4-lambda)=1/2 and hence lambda=0, not a positive eigenvalue.
At lambda=1/4 the linear solution is zero. The constant f gives lambda=0.
Regular Sturm-Liouville completeness gives the stated first positive value.

For any f, variance decomposition on the independent pair (X,G) yields
Var f(X+G)<=E|f'(X+G)|^2+P_X E|E_G f'(X+G)|^2
             <=(1+P_X) E|f'(X+G)|^2.
Thus P(mu_R)<=5 and Var(Y)<=2. No KLS conjecture is used.

Let kappa=4, c_R=R-1/2, M_R=2 log R, and define the analytic covariance

 h_R(y)=Phi(kappa(y-c_R)),
 A_R(y)=exp(M_R h_R(y)),
 Zout|Y=y ~ N(0,A_R(y)).                                  (5)

The name Zout distinguishes the observation from the scalar normalizer Z.
A_R lies in [1,R^2]. For a centered scalar Gaussian covariance channel,

 J_q(y)=(1/2)(partial_y log A_R(y))^2
       =M_R^2 kappa^2/(4pi) exp[-kappa^2(y-c_R)^2].           (6)

Using (3), integration of a completed Gaussian square gives

 E J_q(Y) <= kappa exp(1+1/(4kappa^2))/(sqrt(pi) Z)
                * exp(-R)(log R)^2.                       (7)

Indeed int exp[-kappa^2(y-c_R)^2-y]dy
=sqrt(pi)/kappa * exp[-c_R+1/(4kappa^2)]. This is an exact integral bound,
not a quadrature estimate. The reference need not obey a log-Sobolev bound.

To lower-bound information, let

 a_R=1/2+sqrt(2 log M_R)/kappa,
 B=1{Y>R-a_R},
 E=1{|Zout|>sqrt(8 e R)}.

On B=0, h_R(y)<=exp[-(sqrt(2log M_R))^2/2]=1/M_R,
so A_R(y)<=e and Pr(E=1|B=0)<=2exp(-4R).
Also p_B=Pr(B=1)<=exp(-R+a_R+1/2)/Z, so
log(1/p_B)>=R/2 for R>=4096.

On y>=R, h_R(y)>=Phi(2)>=3/4, whence A_R(y)>=R^(3/2).
The Gaussian small-ball bound Pr(|G|<=t)<=sqrt(2/pi)t gives
Pr(E=1|Y=y)>=1/2 for R>=4096. In particular, by restricting to [R,R+1],

 s=Pr(E=1)>= exp(1/2)(1-exp(-1))/(4Z) * exp(-R)
           >=(1-exp(-1))/4 * exp(-R).                      (8)

The posterior failure eta=Pr(B=0|E=1) is at most
8/(1-exp(-1))*exp(-3R), hence <=1/4. The Bernoulli divergence satisfies

 KL(B|E=1 || B) >=(1-eta)log(1/p_B)-h_2(eta)>=R/4.

Information data processing and its posterior-divergence formula now give

 I(Y;Zout)>=I(B;E)>=s KL(B|E=1||B)
              >=(1-exp(-1))/16 * R exp(-R).                (9)

Combining (7),(9), and P(mu_R)<=5 proves (2) with the explicit coefficient

 c_R=(1-exp(-1))*sqrt(pi)Z /
             [80 kappa exp(1+1/(4kappa^2))],

bounded below by a positive numerical constant for all R>=4096.
Every field and likelihood is analytic. The reference is smooth, strictly
positive and log-concave. No atom, support discontinuity, dimension growth,
unbounded reference covariance, or bad Poincare constant causes the failure.

Connection to posterior likelihoods: if ell_z(y)=q(z|y)/qbar(z), then

 int qbar(z) int |partial_y sqrt(ell_z)|^2 dmu dz
       =(1/4) E J_q(Y),
 int qbar(z) Ent_mu(ell_z) dz=I(Y;Zout).

Consequently a uniform integrated observation-likelihood log-Sobolev
substitute with only P(mu) would imply the false (1).

The unbounded parameter is the covariance contrast R^2. Actual N75 has
q=N(0,I+f_L(T Rfield(Y)^2)) with Rfield derived from a critical construction.
The supplied U summary does not supply that full proof or exact f_L/field
constraints. Our family is an admissible arbitrary analytic Gaussian
covariance field, but is not proved realizable by that critical Rfield.
Thus the general transfer is REFUTED; the special N75 gate is UNRESOLVED.
In addition sigma^2/P(mu) does not tend to zero here. This is not a KLS
counterexample or the hypothetical normalized spectral-plateau sequence.

2. COMPACT EXPONENTIAL LIKELIHOODS: EXACT SIMPLER OBSTRUCTION

On mu_L(dx)=exp(-x)/(1-exp(-L)) dx, 0<=x<=L, let
ell_L(x)=(1-exp(-L))exp(x)/L. Its posterior is exactly Unif[0,L]. Then

 Ent_mu ell_L=L/2+log(1-exp(-L))-log L,
 E_mu |partial sqrt(ell_L)|^2=1/4.

For delta=L^(-1/2), let ell_delta=1-delta+delta ell_L. The mixture identity
with a Bernoulli latent label gives

 Ent ell_delta >=delta Ent ell_L-h_2(delta),
 E|partial sqrt(ell_delta)|^2<=delta/4,
 TV(ell_delta mu,mu)<=delta,
 Hellinger^2(ell_delta mu,mu)<=2delta.

The entropy tends to infinity while energy, TV and Hellinger all tend to
zero, and P(mu_L)<4, Var(mu_L)<=1. The changed posterior ell_delta mu need
not be log-concave; only the reference is asserted log-concave. Section1
avoids relying on a freely supplied individual likelihood by using an
actual normalized analytic Gaussian observation channel.

3. EVERYWHERE-EQUAL FISHER, ARBITRARILY DIFFERENT MEMORY PRELOG

Fix q>=1, d=2q, S_q=sum_(k=1)^q k^2, and a_q=S_q^(-1/2).
Both models have parameter theta in [0,1] and one observation in R^d:

 L_theta=N(theta e_1,I_d),
 C_theta=N(m_q(theta),I_d),
 m_q(theta)=a_q(cos theta,sin theta,...,cos qtheta,sin qtheta).

Their Fisher information is EXACTLY 1 for every theta, since
||m_q'(theta)||^2=a_q^2 sum k^2=1. Their covariance is exactly I_d, their
observation densities are analytic and strongly log-concave, and each law
has Poincare/log-Sobolev constant 1. ||m_q(theta)||<=1.

Nevertheless, in the baseline blind-law-reconstruction resource model,

 B_cl^L(eps)=B_q^L(eps)=(1/2)log_2(1/eps)+O(1),
 B_cl^C(eps)=B_q^C(eps)=q log_2(1/eps)+O_q(1).               (10)

B means log_2 TOTAL retained dimension, including every input-dependent
classical record. Shared randomness is independent; no entanglement
assistance. The encoder sees X and not theta; a decoder returns a fresh Y
with sup_theta TV(Law_theta Y,P_theta)<=eps. q is fixed before eps->0.
The constants in O_q(1) are not uniform in q and can be very poorly conditioned.

Proof of full affine dimension: choose theta_j=j/d, j=0,...,d. If the
augmented mean matrix [1,m_q(theta_j)] were singular, a nonzero real
trigonometric polynomial of degree q would vanish at d+1=2q+1 distinct
points exp(i theta_j). Multiplication by z^q would give a nonzero ordinary
polynomial of degree at most 2q with 2q+1 distinct roots. Impossible.
Hence these d+1 means are affinely independent. The line's affine dimension
is 1. This is an exact root-count proof, not a numerical determinant claim.

For completeness the quantum memory lower is reconstructed here. Put a
uniform prior on those d+1 Gaussian means. Its marginal mu is a positive
mixture, and let b(x) be its posterior mean. Its Jacobian is
Db(x)=Cov(mean|x), strictly positive definite. On the compact convex cube
B=[-1,1]^d, its smallest eigenvalue has a positive minimum v. Therefore
||b(x)-b(x')||>=v||x-x'|| by integrated strong monotonicity on the segment.
The mixture density is <=(2pi)^(-d/2). For ANY action t, the sublevel
||b(x)-t||^2/4<=u intersects B in mass at most C u^(d/2): if nonempty, pick
x0 in it, pull back a ball of radius 4sqrt(u)/v, and use the density bound.

Use bounded loss ||mean-t||^2/4 on the convex hull of means; all norms <=1,
so its range is [0,1]. Its exact posterior regret is ||b(X)-b(Y)||^2/4.
Reconstruction TV bounds expected regret by eps under this finite prior.

For an arbitrary hybrid D-dimensional encoder rho_x and decoder POVM M(dy),
rho_x<=I implies the localized trace inequality

 Pr{X in B, regret(X,b(Y))<=u}<=D C u^(d/2).

Indeed Tr(rho_x M(dy))<=Tr M(dy); integrate over each regret sublevel, then
use int Tr M=D. Independent seeds average the same bound and consume no
additional input-dependent side record. With mass m=mu(B)>0, integration
of [m-DCu^(d/2)]_+ yields

 eps >= [d/(d+2)] m^(1+2/d) (CD)^(-2/d).

This proves log D>=d/2 log(1/eps)+O_q(1). The same proof with means 0,1
on the line gives the exponent 1/2. It uses full law reconstruction, not
an assumption of repeated/compatible coordinate POVMs. This exact general
trace/regret architecture was already read in M's posterior principle.

An independent explicit upper avoids importing absent N30/N31 proofs.
For scalar |theta|<=1, choose reference g=N(0,4) and transform
u=Phi(x/2). The transformed density is r_theta(x)=p_theta/g
=2 exp[-3x^2/8+theta x-theta^2/2]. Both it and its u-derivative vanish at
the endpoints. Moreover its second u-derivative is L1 uniformly bounded,
because

 ||q_theta''||_1=int |(r_theta'/g)'| dx,
 (r_theta'/g)'=(r_theta/g)[-3/4+(theta-3x/4)(theta-x/2)],

whose integrand has a Gaussian tail uniformly for |theta|<=1.
Extend periodically. Use an independent public uniform shift of a J-cell
grid in u; encode the cell, decode uniformly within that shifted cell, and
map back by 2Phi^(-1). This uses exactly J labels at every seed. Averaging
the seed gives triangular convolution. Taylor's integral remainder and
int triangle_h(t)t^2 dt=h^2/6 give

 TV(q_theta,triangle_h*q_theta)<=||q_theta''||_1/(12J^2).

For d coordinates, the product densities telescope under L1 contractions,
so total TV<=d M/(12J^2), M=sup_|theta|<=1 ||q_theta''||_1. Choose
J=ceil sqrt(dM/(12eps)); D=J^d. For the line, only coordinate1 is encoded
and all ancillaries are regenerated. This proves the matching (10).
Continuous random outputs are legal here. A deterministic floating output
is atomic and has TV1 to the Gaussian target. Certified CDF/inverse-CDF,
finite random-bit/lazy-real output, and model-acquisition costs remain separate.

This attacks the claim that parameter/Fisher dimension alone determines
fixed-experiment vanishing-error memory even under Gaussian/log-concave
regularity. It does not challenge N31's INDEPENDENT-GAUSSIAN-SEQUENCE
interface, which uses the full mean ellipsoid and acquires its known axes.
Nor does it contradict regular iid n->infinity coding with fixed tolerance;
the asymptotic variable here is eps->0 for one fixed noisy experiment.

c09_s03 58f39735bea2d5ba48f0e6aaf19cb92509722bd92cc72a391aa51a13a86e55cd
Source: outputs/round6/frozen_initials/c09_s03/INITIAL.txt
C09_S03 INITIAL — INDEPENDENT, IMMUTABLE CHECKPOINT
7 October 2026. Requested Sol/max; observed backend model UNKNOWN.

STRONGEST CLAIM
A complete internally derived, costed information bound for an admitted
Gaussian covariance-observation experiment. It substitutes a joint log-field
condition for a log-Sobolev assumption, for ONE explicit saturation profile:

Let Y have any probability law, B(Y) be a measurable positive definite m-by-m
real symmetric matrix, and E||log B||_F^2<infinity. For L>0 and every T>0 put
 f_L(x)=Lx/(L+x), A_T(Y)=I+f_L(T B(Y)),
 Z_T | Y ~ N(0,A_T(Y)). Then, in nats,

 integral_0^infinity I(Y;Z_T) dT/T
 <= (L^2/24) E||log B-E log B||_F^2.                    (A)

The constant does not depend on m, but the Frobenius variance can grow with m.
No log-Sobolev inequality, Gaussian law for Y, commuting-field assumption,
likelihood-ratio bound, or eigenvector regularity is used. If Y has Poincare
constant P and log B is in the scalar-entry Sobolev domain, (A) implies

 integral I(Y;Z_T) dT/T
 <= L^2 P/24 E sum_a ||partial_a log B||_F^2.          (B)

The matrix derivative is that of the MATRIX LOGARITHM, not entrywise log.
The expression cannot be replaced by covariance or Fisher eigenvalues alone.

An exact-rational certificate has been implemented for a supplied finite table
of diagonal positive rational B(Y) and rational reference probabilities. The
three-state, three-mode fixture gives a certified analytic upper about
0.03002831336990348 nats. This is an upper certificate, not an estimate or a
numerical computation of actual mutual information.

STATUS: internally derived candidate theorem with full argument below;
finite exact/interval evidence; external correctness and exact priority open.
This is an admitted information regime, not an archive codec or a KLS proof.

1. FULL DERIVATION

1.1 Fixed-scale entropy comparison.
Write A=A_T(Y) and M=EA. Since I<=A<=(1+L)I, all Gaussian mixture entropies
are well-defined and the mixture covariance is M. The nonnegativity of
D(Law(Z_T)||N(0,M)), or Gaussian maximum entropy, yields

 I(Y;Z_T) <= 1/2 [log det M-E log det A].              (1)

For F(C)=-log det C,
 D^2F(C)[H,H]=tr(C^-1 H C^-1 H)<=||H||_F^2
whenever C>=I. Integrating the second derivative on the line segment joining
M and A gives
 F(A)<=F(M)+DF(M)[A-M]+1/2||A-M||_F^2.
Take expectations; the linear term vanishes. Thus

 I(Y;Z_T)<=1/4 E||A-EA||_F^2
           =1/8 E||f_L(TB)-f_L(TB')||_F^2,           (2)

where B' is an independent copy. This uses an entropy upper bound directly,
not the inadequate replacement of entropy by bounded Hellinger distance.

1.2 Exact matrix scale integral.
For positive definite B,C choose eigenvalues b_i,c_j and orthonormal eigenbases
u_i,v_j. Put w_ij=|<u_i,v_j>|^2. Its row and column sums are one. Functional
calculus and expansion of the Frobenius square give

 ||f_L(TB)-f_L(TC)||_F^2
 =sum_ij w_ij [f_L(Tb_i)-f_L(Tc_j)]^2.                (3)

For b,c>0, a direct substitution T=L u gives
 integral [f_L(Tb)-f_L(Tc)]^2 dT/T = L^2 kappa(b/c),
 kappa(b/c)= (b+c)/(b-c) log(b/c)-2, b!=c,
 kappa(1)=0.                                        (4)

For an explicit integration certificate, writing d=b-c, the integrand after
removing L^2 is
 u d^2/[(1+bu)^2(1+cu)^2]
 = (b+c)b/[d(1+bu)]-b/(1+bu)^2
   -(b+c)c/[d(1+cu)]-c/(1+cu)^2.
The logarithmic terms integrate to (b+c)/d log(b/c); the two squared terms
integrate to -2. The boundary integrals are finite for positive b,c.
The script verifies this rational partial-fraction identity in 60 exact cases.

For t=log(b/c), kappa=t coth(t/2)-2. It is nonnegative by its integral form,
and
  kappa<=t^2/6.                                     (5)
To prove (5), for x>=0 define
 H(x)=(1+x^2/3)sinh x-x cosh x.
Then H(0)=0 and H'(x)=x/3 (x cosh x-sinh x)>=0 because the last bracket has
zero initial value and derivative x sinh x>=0. Hence x coth x<=1+x^2/3.
Take x=|t|/2. Also kappa<=|t| follows from
 2|t|/(exp(|t|)-1)<=2.

From (3)-(5),
 integral ||f_L(TB)-f_L(TC)||_F^2 dT/T
 <= L^2/6 sum_ij w_ij (log b_i-log c_j)^2
 = L^2/6 ||log B-log C||_F^2.                        (6)

The last identity expands both squared traces and the mixed trace. It does
not require B and C to commute. Integrate (2), use Tonelli, (6), and
 E||log B-log B'||_F^2=2 E||log B-E log B||_F^2.
This proves (A), including all dimension constants. Poincare applied to the
real scalar entries of log B and summed proves (B). Measurable fields with
finite log second moment are covered directly; no smooth approximation is
needed for (A). The gradient statement needs its stated Sobolev domain.

2. WHAT IS ACTUALLY ACQUIRED, AND COSTS

Implemented input: an explicit table p_1,...,p_K, with p_k>=0, sum p_k=1,
and b_ki>0 rational, i=1,...,m. Y is the finite reference state and
B(Y=k)=diag(b_k1,...,b_km). All K*m+K scalar inputs are read. No density,
posterior, matrix spectrum, state probabilities, or calibration is acquired
for free. The reference law is supplied by the table, rather than inferred
from one archival observation.

For every rational x>0, normalize x=2^k z, 1<=z<2. Write t=(z-1)/(z+1),
0<=t<1/3, and use
 log z=2 sum_n>=0 t^(2n+1)/(2n+1).
After N terms the omitted nonnegative tail is at most
 2 t^(2N+1)/[(2N+1)(1-t^2)].
The same series at t=1/3 bounds log 2. Sign-aware rational interval arithmetic
then bounds log x to absolute width eta. No floating logarithm is trusted.

Compute V=1/2 sum_ab p_a p_b sum_i (log b_ai-log b_bi)^2 with interval
arithmetic and return L^2 V_upper/24. The implemented transparent pair loop
uses O(K^2 m) rational operations, O(Km) stored log intervals, and
O(Km(q+log H)) series terms when eta=2^-q and H bounds the absolute binary
exponents of input rationals. The numerator/denominator bit sizes grow
polynomially in full table encoding length and q. It can be rewritten as
centered first/second moments in O(Km), but that optimization was not needed
for the fixture. m and K are charged, and L is numerical in the bound.
Matrix logs for general noncommuting supplied matrices are not implemented.
The proof does not imply a free matrix-log oracle.

Run: python3 work/cycle6/c09_s03/log_covariance_certificate.py.
The run took approximately 0.0154 seconds inside its own timer and about
0.04 seconds process wall time. Backend compute/energy/token costs UNKNOWN.
No large solver, download, Gaussian sampler, likelihood quadrature, or heavy
local process was used. The table bound, 60 rational identity fixtures, and
16 sign-aware interval scalar-kernel checks passed. Those finite checks do
not certify the general theorem or novelty.

An optional genuinely data-acquired variant is also elementary. Suppose one
can evaluate supplied diagonal B(Y) on fresh iid Y and has certified ranges
log b_i(Y) in [a_i,c_i]. Put W=sum_i(c_i-a_i)^2. From 2n draws form independent
pairs Z_j=1/2||log B(Y_(2j-1))-log B(Y_(2j))||_F^2 in [0,W/2]. Then EZ_j=V,
and Hoeffding gives, with probability >=1-delta,
 V<=mean Z_j+(W/2)sqrt(log(1/delta)/(2n)).
If each scalar log is computed within eta, add
 2 eta sum_i(c_i-a_i)+2m eta^2
for a deterministic rounding bound. Multiply the result by L^2/24.
This charges 2n source draws, O(nm) evaluations and logs, the range certificate,
and precision eta. To make the sampling part contribute <=tau/2 to the final
information upper, n>=L^4 W^2 log(1/delta)/(1152 tau^2) suffices.
This sample-bound variant was derived but not implemented/tested here. Without
known log ranges or a tail certificate, arbitrary rare covariance states can
escape finite-sample bounds; no distribution-free acquisition claim is made.

3. N75: ADMITTED TRANSFER AND THE STILL-MISSING GATE

The pinned N75 summary supplies only an internally claimed heat-flow plateau
and describes observations I+f_L(T R(Y)^2). Its full technical note, exact f_L,
field construction, and code are absent. The explicitly chosen rational soft
saturation above is therefore an admitted replacement experiment, not a claim
that its formula matches the absent original definition.

If the source field is nonsingular and this saturation is legal there, (A)
would give a scale-integrated entropy upper whenever Var_F(log R^2) is small
or otherwise controlled at the required rate. N75's small covariance and
Poincare plateau do not establish that premise. (B) also requires CONTROL OF
THE LOG-FIELD GRADIENT, whose inverse-small-eigenvalue behavior is substantive.
There is no justified KLS contradiction or general new algorithm regime from
N75 alone.

The naive gradient route can fail even in dimension one: take Y uniform on
[-1,1], B(Y)=Y^2, and the admitted f_L. Away from the zero,
 partial_y f_L(Ty^2)=2Ty/(1+Ty^2/L)^2,
 integral_0^infinity |partial_y f_L(Ty^2)|^2 dT/T
 =2L^2/(3y^2).
Its expectation is infinite. Thus integrating the absolute-field Poincare
energy cannot replace the required scale entropy bound, despite a bounded
Poincare constant and a smooth bounded field R(Y)=Y. This is a counterexample
to that proposed bound, not a counterexample to KLS or to the source strategy.
Here log(Y^2) nevertheless has finite variance, so (A) extends by truncation
and gives a finite bound. This difference is the useful second mechanism.

Positive definiteness/common support is essential. If B=0 on one positive-mass
reference state and B=b>0 on another, A_T converges to different covariances
I and (1+L)I. Their binary mixture has strictly positive mutual information
for every L>0. Hence integral I dT/T diverges at infinity. A COMMON fixed
nullspace can be removed and the theorem applied on its positive complement;
mismatched nullspaces are not silently regularized for free.

c10_s01 d079448f084850567851bd59f0ff7a141822549f2edfd685d135aa5ff2674adf
